MEETING: Sine Die Meeting | DATE: 2011-01-03 | CLIP_ID: 193 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 193

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
January 3, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in “Sine Die” session at 3:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers. Present were:  MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Ms. Ash,
Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.
The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Mayor Kaysen welcomed those in attendance, provided a general explanation of the term “Sine
Die” and the purpose of the session, which is administrative in nature.  He noted the session provides
for ceremonial recognition of outgoing Ward I City Council member Ms. Case, and outgoing 2010 City
Council President Ms. Broyles.
At Mayor Kaysen’s request, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer read a cer emonial resolution in i ts
entirety, honoring outgoing Council member Ms. Case, entitled as follows: “A Ceremonial Resolution
in recognition of the Honorable Judy Case for her Dedication and Outstanding Contributions to the City
of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and its Residents.”  May or Kaysen thanked Ms. Cas e for her de dication,
commitment and her caring for residents and the community throughout her years of elected service and
presented her with a framed version of the ceremonial resolution, an engraved plaque of appreciation,
and a token of appreciation on behalf of the City Council.  Ms. Case thanked citizens for their support
during her three terms on City Council, and stated she has enjoyed serving the community.  (#5254)
Mayor Kaysen commented on the leadership role, responsibility and commitment involved in
serving as Council President and expressed appreciation to Ms. Broyles as President of City Council
for 2010.  On behalf of the City Council, Mayor Kaysen presented her with an engraved gavel plaque
of appreciation.  Ms. Broyles thanked City Council members for their support during her service as
President and briefly noted topics of interest that had occurred during her term as president.
Purpose of the “Sine Die” session having been met, Mayor Kaysen adjourned the meeting at
3:09 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
PUBLISH: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
January 10, 2011

---

MEETING: First Regular Meeting of the Governing Body | DATE: 2011-01-03 | CLIP_ID: 195 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 195

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
January 3, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne assembled for its 2011 First Regular Meeting
on this date at 3:15 p.m. in City Council Chambers.  Mayor Kaysen welcomed those in attendance
and stated the purpose of the First Regular Meeting was to administer oaths of office to those
individuals who were elected, or re-elected, at the most recent general election to serve as municipal
officials, and to take action on the positions for City Council President and Vice-President for 2011
and for Mayoral administrative appointees.
The Honorable Thomas Campbell, Judge of the First Judicial District Court, administered
the oath of elected office for newly elected and re-elected City Council members: Sean D. Allen,
Ward I; Georgia Broyles, Ward III, and Mark D. Rinne, Ward II.
Mayor Kaysen officially called the meeting to order.  Those present were: MAYOR - Richard
L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles,  Mr. Collins, Mr.
Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
ELECTION OF  COUNCI L PRES IDENT F OR 2011.  May or Ka ysen announc ed that
pursuant to City Code, he would not be participating in voting for the election of Council President.
Mayor Kaysen stated nominations for election of the office of Council President was in order.  Ms.
Broyles moved to nominate and elect Dr. Rinne as City Council President for 2011, seconded by Mr.
Valdez.  Mr. Pierson moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion to cease
nominations carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor
Kaysen who did not vote.  M otion to nominate and elect Dr. Rinne carried.  Vo ting “yes” – a ll
members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
ELECTION OF COUNCIL VICE-PRESIDENT FOR 2011.  Mayor Kaysen announced that
pursuant to City Code, he would not be pa rticipating in voting for  the election of Council Vice-
President.  Mayor Kaysen stated nominations for election of the office of Council Vice-President was
in order.  Dr. Rinne moved to nominate and elect Mr. Brown as City Council Vice-President for
2011, seconded by Ms. Broy les.  Mr. Collins m oved that nominations cea se, seconded by Mr.
Pierson.  Motion to cease nominations carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  Motion to nominate and elect Mr. Brown
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who
did not vote.
APPROVAL OF A PPOINTMENT OF  CITY OFFI CIALS (pursuant to Title 2 of the
Cheyenne City Code) as follows: Matthew J. Ashby, Planning Services Director; Barbara E. Dorr,
City Treasurer; James Edward Elias, Public Works Director; Paul S. Galeotos, Municipal Court
Judge; Ra ndy K. Hays, Assistant C ity Tr easurer; Ca rol I ntlekofer, City  Cle rk/Assistant C ity
Treasurer; Ronnald J. Jeffr ey, Juvenile Court Judge; Brian N. Kozak, Chief of Cheyenne Police
Department; James A. Martin, Chief of Cheyenne Fire and Rescue Department; Mark T. Moran,
Municipal Court Judge; Rick L. Parish, Director of Parks and Recreation; Douglas G. Vetter, City
Engineer, and Daniel E. White, City Attorney.  Mayor Kaysen announced that pursuant to state law,
as these are Mayoral appointments, he would not be participating in the voting.  Mr. Spiker moved
to approve the appointments “en masse”, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion  carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  The
Honorable Thomas Campbell, Judge of the First Judicial District Court, administered the oa th of
office to the aforementioned Mayoral appointees.
Mayor Kaysen expressed appreciation to those in attendance at the meeting and extended a
special thank you to Judge Campbell for his participation in the meeting.  Mayor Kaysen announced
that those present were invited to enjoy light refreshments available in the City Council annex area
immediately following the meeting.
Purpose of the First Regular Meeting having been met, Mayor Kaysen adjourned the meeting
at 3:30 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
January 10, 2011

Pierce Qu antum) to b e re placed; age o f other b ackup u nits; o ther b ids re ceived; b id
specifications, and war ranty information submitted with bids.  Dis cussion followed a nd
included: Specific ations and warranty require ments set forth in the bid document; leng th of
warranty (frame) coverage (50 y ears) re quested in bid specif ications; use of the e ngine being
replaced after removal f rom f ront l ine se rvice; lo wer b id r eceived r elative to m eeting bid
specifications; b id d ocument w ording r egarding c onsideration o f t he t ime o f t ruck d elivery;
lowest bid received ($437,432.00) vs. proposed r ecommended bidder; bidding process, and age,
life expectancy, usage and maintenance costs for current and reserve engines in use.  Throughout
discussion Chief Martin provided infor mation on plans for extending the  life of the c urrent fire
truck a fter r etirement from front line se rvice; frame  war ranty; Fire  and Rescue A pparatus
Committee review of bids re ceived, staff me mbers compr ising the Committee and the
Committee’s recommendation to acquire a 2011 Pierce Arrow XT “D emo” engine; importance
of deliver y date c onsideration due  to maintenance costs incur red f or the c urrent truck;
differences in truck models, capabilities, vehicle frame warranty and time of delivery relative to
bid specifica tions and bids recei ved, and life  expectancy and use of  curr ent and r eserve fle et
engines.  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Director of Public Works, stated he was unsure whether any
frame failures on fire  engines have occurred, and John Fox, Divisi on Chief of Operations, Fire
and Rescue Department, explained bidders’ “checked-off” boxes indicating areas of bid
specification compliance on forms submitted with their bid, and importance of frame warranty
coverage that doesn’t expire when an engine is retired from the front line and the engine is put to
other fleet use.  Discussion continued on a  20 vs. 50 y ear frame warranty; type of f ire trucks
utilized; proximity of bidders’ dealer service centers to Cheyenne; consideration of delivery time
frame during bid evaluation proc ess; bidding pr ocedures, and concern with lowest bid rec eived
vs. proposed re commended bidde r.  During  discussion on frame  war ranty cover age, Dua ne
Dusett, Fr ont Rang e F ire, e xplained the 50 y ear war ranty provided by  Fr ont Rang e’s bid has
been in place s ince s tability f rame c hanges w ere ma de to  tr ucks f or b etter s tability a nd
durability.  In response to a procedural question, City Attorney Dan White advised if a cceptance
of the recommended bid is re jected, ideally it would be best to rejec t all bids and re-bid.  It was
confirmed the bid for the Pier ce Arrow XT from Front Range Fire was the only vendor that met
all bid specifications.  M r. White expressed concerns with proceeding with acceptance of a bid
from any other vendor that did not meet specifications. Upon inquiry regarding wh en it  is
decided to place a unit in reserves, Chief Martin advised it is b ased on maintenance concerns,
expenses, and life of  the vehicle , and explained the depar tment’s fleet replacement plan. Upon
inquiry, Mr. Elias indicated any time the City can standardize fleet equipment it may save costs
but ea ch department s hould determine w hat type of unit and e quipment they need, a nd Chief
Martin advised bid specifications were developed to be as general as possible and that past bid
documents we re use d as a g uideline.  Chief Mar tin further discusse d war ranty time frame ;
matching State L and and I nvestment Boa rd (SL IB) fundi ng deadline connec ted with the
purchase; recommendation to acc ept the propose d bid involving a he avier engine, transmission
and frame; different components offered by truck manufacturers, and estimated time frame if re-
bidding of the eng ine is conducted.  Upon query , Rudy Apodaca, Director of Purc hasing, read
bid docum ent wording  reg arding acc eptance of the  lowest bid in the sole discretion of the
governing body.  Pursuant to i nquiry regarding the time fra me for utilizing SL IB funds, Mayor
Kaysen re cessed the meeting f or 15 minutes to contact the G rants Mana ger r egarding the
deadline.  Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 7:45 p.m. and advised communication with
the Grants Manager was unable to be made .  Mr. Pierson moved to postpone to Fe b. 14, 2011,
seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr. Duse tt advised the demo truc k bid by Front Range Fire has extra
features th at weren’t r equired in  bid  sp ecifications a nd if  the  mot ion to a ccept t he bid  is
postponed, it’s possible that the dem o truck could be moved out or sold to someone e lse and
would no longer be available. Discussion followed on the postponement with regard to receipt of
information in volving the  SL IB fu nding tim e fr ame.  M r. Pie rson wi thdrew h is m otion to
postpone with concur rence by Mr. Spiker as se cond to the motion. As there we re no obje ctions
raised b y oth er m ember o f t he g overning bo dy, M ayor Ka ysen a nnounced th e mot ion to
postpone was being withdrawn.  Dr. Rinne moved to ame nd the main motion to reflec t approval
would be contingent upon the availability of SLIB funds, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mo tion to
amend c arried.  Voting  “yes” – all member s of the g overning body prese nt.  Main motion as
amended to approve the bid submitted by Front Range Fire for the Pierce Arrow XT demo truck
in the amount of $467,821.00 ca rried.  V oting “yes” – a ll members of the g overning body
present with the exception of Mr. Spiker voting “no”.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-16- 11 for f urnishing two ne w packer trucks for the Sanitation
Division (Solid Waste Equipment L ease/Purchase fund).  Motion (p er Consent Agenda) carried
(total amount not to exceed $259,632.00).
[CA] Consideration of B id # R-18-11 for  furnishing  twelve ne w 2011 Police vehicle s from
Spradley Ba rr Motors, Chey enne, Wyoming, for the Police De partment (2007- 2010 1% Sales
Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.

Post I nn, 1600 Wes t  L incolnway, Chey enne, Wy oming. ( License curr ently held by  Mantiff

Concern was expressed that timely clean up of the a rea should be ac hieved as the site is located

“An Ordinance amending Section 9.16.100 (B),  Open House Assembly, of Chapter 9.16,
Offenses By or Against Minors, of Title 9, Public Peace a nd Welfare, of the Code of the City  of
Cheyenne, Wy oming, pe rtaining to the definition of ‘Adult’.”   Speaking  in support of the
ordinance were:  Matthew  Bowen, who e xplained his rea sons for support included e mpowering
18-20 y ear o lds to  b e r esponsible a nd to  u nderstand th e c onsequences o f b reaking th e law;
Debbie T aylor, spokesper son and volunteer  victims advocate  aff iliated with the Wy oming
Chapter of Mothers Against Drunk Driving (MADD) organization, who discussed streng thening
penalties for minor in possession violations, parental perception, and sending a clear message to
youth that undera ge dr inking is not acce ptable, and L isa Scholz, C heyenne Police D ept.,
manager of a Core Prevention Grant that the Department has received that invo lves a “Parents
Who Ho st Lose t he M ost” c ampaign, p rovided s tatistical information af filiated wi th u nderage
drinking and reviewed addiction factors involving youth drinking.  During comments, Cheyenne
Police Chief B rian Kozak re viewed Police D epartment statisti cs fr om rec ent y ears involving
underage drinking, officer time and resources involving response, safety and control measures,
and stated the proposed ordinance would hold 18-20 year olds accountable if they were involved
in hosting a dr inking party.  He a dvised a violation of the pr oposed ordinanc e would be a
criminal off ense a nd would require  the cited individ ual(s) to appea r in Municipal Court.  He
provided brie f informa tion on a community  educa tional prog ram, throug h g rant funding , the
Police Department will be offe ring in the near  future called “Parents Who Host Lose the Most”,
and noted the D epartment is also working  with the local mil itary base on unde rage drinking
issues.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins stated there was no
recommendation on the item from Committ ee.  Mr. Collins m oved to appr ove on sec ond
reading, seconded by Ms. Broy les.  Discussion included the f ollowing topics:  I nformation on
underage drinking  and tee n hosting of pa rties involving a lcohol; public educa tion; parental
providers of alcohol to y outh; methods being utilized by  youth to acquire  alcohol; whethe r the
proposed ordinance will make a differ ence; past ordinances approved to assist with training of
alcohol s ervers in b usinesses li censed to  se rve a lcohol, a nd ke g re gistration co ntrol;
inconsistency relative to 18 y ear old s being able to ser ve alcohol in restaura nt liquor licensed
establishments, and c urrent penalties for  underage drinking.  Ms. Broy les moved to amend by
substitute, seconded by Mr. Spiker. I t was noted the pur pose of the substitute i s to chang e the
general penalty provision in Section 9.16.100 (E) by striking the current language and inserting
the following:  “Each violation of this section shall be punished in ac cordance with the ge neral
penalty provisions of City  Code as dete rmined by the Municipal Court.  Each person cited for a
violation o f t his s ection shall appear i n M unicipal C ourt.”  C omment w as m ade t hat a  M IP
(minor in possession) offense should also be a  must appear (in court) offense.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. Further discussion followed
and included comments on research involving strategies that limit access of alcohol to underage
individuals; whether harsher punishments will reduce  underage alcohol usage; allocating funds
for more after school programs; teenage vs. adult brain development and youth comprehension
of consequences of a violation; limited resources for evening youth activities; helping youth to
become responsible citizens; and an ordinance providing a deterrent to youth relative to hosting
parties invo lving alcohol.  M ain motion as amended by substitute carried.  V oting “yes” – all
members of the g overning body present with the exception of Ms. Ash and Mr. Collins voti ng
“no”. [City  Clerk’s note: Due  to passag e of the  amendment, the r evised e ntitlement of the
Ordinance is as follows: “An Ordinance amending Section 9.16.100 (B), Open House Assembly,
of Chapter 9.16, Offe nses By or Against Minors, of Title 9, Public Peace  and Welfar e, of the
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, pertaining to the definition of ‘Adult’, and providing a
penalty for violations.”]

Complex) and the Council process for speaking on the item. Mayor Kaysen advised public input
could be made at the Finance Committee meeting on Feb. 22, 2011 in Room 122 at 11:00 a.m.
and again on Feb. 28, 2011 at the City Council meeting.
There being  no further  business to come befo re the g overning body , Mayor Ka ysen
adjourned the meeting at 7:31 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

“An Ordinance amending Section 9.16.100 (B),  Open House Assembly, of Chapter 9.16,

Mr. Spiker and Mr . Valdez.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins and Mr. Pierson.
(#3913)

“An Ordinance repealing in its entirety,  Chapter 8.44,  Solid Waste, and creating a new
Chapter 8.44, Solid Waste, of Title 8, Hea lth and Safety, of the Code of  the City of Chey enne,
Wyoming.”  Rick Fierro, President, RAM Trucking; Drew Kelley, Wyoming Disposal Systems,
and Jim Cla rk, Countryside Disposal, each spoke in o pposition to the ordinance with regard to
private busine sses providing  roll-off c ontainer se rvices.  Topics and c oncerns prese nted
included: Difficulty private businesses have in competing with the City in the area of providing

businesses and e mployees; construction and demolition w aste, and recyclable disposal se rvices
by the private  section; investment in the community  by  private businesse s; Cit y roll-off f ee
structure, and cur rent re strictions private haule rs have  in offering roll-off c ontainer se rvices
within the City.  Frank Cole, developer, discussed automated curbside pickup of solid waste and
recyclables a nd his concerns re garding unsightly  container  storag e plac ement on non-pic kup
days; p roposed s chedule of  pic kups inc luded in  the  or dinance, a nd su pport for free e nterprise
relative to p rivate haulers.  Joh n Palmer questioned what new changes were being proposed in
the ordinance vs. current City Code.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve on second reading, seconded
by Mr. Brown.  M r. Brown moved to amend by substitute ordinance, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, advised the substitute addresses the allowable
practice of bringing recyclables to the City  blue bins by  amending Section 8.44.010, Subsection
F. to add two sentences at the end of the paragraph as follows: “Waste generated outside the city
may not be  brought into the city for disposal.  Waste generated outside the ci ty i s prohibited
from being placed into alleys, vacant lots, residential or commercial containers within the City
(including tho se loc ated in pa rks o r o ther c ity fa cilities).  Co unty re sidents ma y us e pr ivate
haulers or dispose of waste at the transfer station. THIS PARAGRAPH DOES NOT PROHIBIT
COLLECTORS OF RECYCLABLE MATERIAL GENERATED OR COLLECTED OUTSIDE
THE C ITY F ROM B RINGING S UCH M ATERIAL IN TO T HE C ITY A ND P LACING IT
INTO THE BLUE BIN CONTAINERS OR DROPP ING IT AT THE T RANSFER STATION;
SUBJECT T O T HE F EES, L IMITATIONS AND RE QUIREMENTS O F TH IS CH APTER.
RECYCLABLE MATERI AL GENERATED  OR COL LECTED OUTSI DE THE CI TY MAY
NOT B E PL ACED I NTO R ESIDENTIAL OR  CO MMERCIAL RECYCLE CO NTAINERS
INSIDE THE CITY NOR MAY THEY BE PLACED OUTSIDE BLUE BIN CONTAINERS IF
THOSE CONTAINERS ARE FULL.”  Pursuant to comment on lack of a penalty affiliated with
disposing of items on the ground if blue bins are full, Mr. Elias advised their goal is to encourage
recycling from residents outside the city limits, and noted further review of littering p enalties
will be conducted prior to final reading of the ordinance.  Upon inquiry, he discussed wording of
“containers” in Sec tion 8.44.030, Subs ection E.6.; provided inf ormation on the City ’s
responsibility fo r t he colle ction, tr ansfer a nd dis posal of  a ll w aste wi thin th e City; pr ivate vs .
City roll-off c ontainer services, which ha s not been specifically codified in the pa st; collection
programs utili zed in other c ommunities, and e xplained that the City ’s roll-off  container
operations a re no t a n e xpansion (i .e. in creasing the  nu mber o f c ontainers) o f c urrent s ervices.
Mr. Elias stated staff is still working on the comprehensive draft of the Solid Waste Master Plan,
which involves re viewing and ana lyzing seve ral a reas, including f inancial a nd opera tional
aspects as well as the Integrated Solid Waste Plan.  Discussion continued on the cost to process
waste; whe ther the issue involving  private businesse s to prov ide roll-of f conta iners wa s
instrumental to be in cluded in the propose d ordinanc e at this time; allowing pr ivate roll-of f
providers to pa rticipate in discussions concer ning pr oposed ordinanc e cha nges; ordinanc e
wording relative to billing (errors) adjustments and time frame in which customers must provide
notification of same, and City’s cost to dispose of waste and maintain City landfill.  During
discussion, Mr. Elias provided fu rther information on the topics raised.  Dr. Rinne raised a point
of or der c oncerning the  a llowance of  fu rther p ublic comment. M ayor Ka ysen r uled h e wo uld
deviate slightly from the normal public participation pr ocess and would allow a dditional
comments from the public to be  made following a 10 minute rec ess. Mayor Kaysen reconvened
the meeting at 8:35 p.m.  The following  individuals spoke further on the  proposed or dinance:
Mr. Cole; Mr. Fie rro; Brian Clark, Countryside Disposal, and Mr. K elley.  Comments included
concerns with customers responsibility to notice and request an adjustment for any discrepancies
in billing statements with a 60 day time limit; blue bins usage volume on weekends; clarification
needed for ordinance wording regarding roll-off containers, and respect for services provided by
private businesses. Motion to amend by  substitute carried.  V oting “yes” -- all members of the
governing b ody with the exception of Ms. Ash and Ms. B royles voting “ no”.  Discussion
continued on ordinance Section 8.44.050, Subs ection B. 1. involving re sidential pickup services
and proposed ordinance wording indicating residential collection services would be suspende d
during weeks that beg in with a Monday  of a 5th we ek; operational efficiency, and deliver y of
services o verall i n co njunction wi th assessment o f f ees.  M r. E lias cl arified t hat t he l anguage
contained in Sec tion 8.44.050, Subs ection B.1. indicates the City  may suspend servic es during
that time frame , with proper notice , to provide for  staff training  or equipment maintena nce.
Main motion as amended by  substitute carried.  Voting “ yes” -- all member s of the g overning
body with the exception of Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles and Mr. Collins voting “no”.

Center Cost  Reduction Progr am, to assist in the establishment of a Da ta Center, f unding
electricity and broadband expenses for three years.
Under other City business:
John Palmer expressed his concerns with the public participation format utilized at City
Council meetings, requesting Council members consider changing the process, and the ability to
find agenda information on the City’s website.  Comment was made later under other business
that meeting agenda information is also available through staff at the Municipal Building.
Don Threewitt, State Historic Preservation Board, provided a brief update on the Board’s
activities and projects, and invited members of the governing body to a Board Strategic Planning
session to be held on March 12, 2011.  Mayor Kaysen requested Mr. Threewitt submit  details on
the meeting place and time that would be shared with City Council members.
Upon inquiry regarding a letter  Council members re ceived from Safe house, Dan White,
City Attorney, provided information regarding the lease of City-owned land to Attention Homes
and dis cussions held betwe en Attention Homes a nd Safehouse  rela tive to assignme nt of the
property lease to S afehouse.  H e stated his concerns with assignment of the lease without City
approval, n oting a ssignment r equirements c ontained in the exis ting le ase, te rms o f t he le ase
regarding ownership of build ings that have be en placed upon the City ’s property, and ser vices
that are to be provided ( i.e. youth crisis service s) at the site.  He  indicated c ommunication was
made with Safe house representatives encouraging review of lease provisions with the City , and
discussed research conducted regarding ownership of permanent fixtures that have  been pla ced
on the prope rty. He noted one  buildi ng on the site was c onstructed with g rant funds f or the
specific purpose of providing youth crisis services by Attention Homes.  He further stated that he
had presented a proposed lease to Safehouse for consideration to submit  to the City  Council for
approval but that Safehouse “ did not ag ree” and the assignment of the prope rty lease  from
Attention Homes to Safehouse  has occ urred.  Upon inquiry , he re viewed possible options on
how to potentially proceed regarding assignment of the lease.
There being no further other business, City Attorney Dan White requested the governing
body me et in  executive se ssion fo r t he so le pu rpose of  dis cussing a  po tential la wsuit a gainst
Laramie County to c hallenge the validity of recently approved County subdivision regulations.
Mr. Pierson moved to meet in executive se ssion, seconded by  Mr. Collins.  M otion carr ied.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  M ayor Kaysen announced members would
meet in  ex ecutive session in  Cou ncil Ch ambers, n ot o pen to  the  pu blic; r equested th e City
Attorney, City  Cle rk, and other pe rsons approve d by  the City  Attorney , be pre sent at the
executive session, and stated there would be no action taken by the governing body as a result of
the executive session and tha t the reg ular City  Council meetin g would officially adjourn
following closure of the executive session.  ( Clerk’s Note: Executive session convened at 9:22
p.m.; Ms. Ash vacated Council Chambers prior to the e xecutive session being convened and was
not present during the session.)
The mee ting wa s officia lly adjourne d at 9:55 p.m. following c losure of the executive
session.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

“An Or dinance amendi ng Section 5.12.060, Sales and Consumption--Restrictions, of

“An Ordinance amending  Ordinance  No. 3419,  which  established a new compensation
schedule fo r m embers of  the  City  Cou ncil o f t he City  of Cheyenne, to  c larify the  ma nner i n
which compensation is to be computed, and providing for an effective date.”  Mr. Valdez moved
to approve on third and final r eading, seconded by Mr. Brown. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --

“An Ordinance repealing in its entire ty, Chapter 8.44, Solid Waste, and cre ating a new
Chapter 8.44, Solid Waste, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming.” Matthew B owen e xpressed his conce rns with the City  opera ting a s a “r oll-off
monopoly”; questioned the le gal authority  of the g overning body; commented in support of
private entrepreneurs securing the right to provide roll-off container services; ordinance effect on

questioned e nforcement responsibility re lative to v iolations of  the  or dinance a nd sp oke in

City At torney, r eviewed r esearch h e ha d c onducted r egarding mun icipalities’ a uthority to

City limit s. J im Elias, Publ ic Works Direc tor, clar ified that since the  last City Council and

amendment, noting a 2 week postponement was requested at Finance Committee.  In response to

to c onduct business pursuant to Wyoming Statutes, and case law.  Vicki Nemecek, Assistant
Public W orks Di rector, re viewed au thority p rovided b y W yo. S tat. §  1 5-1-103, an d l anguage
contained in the or dinance allowing  the Ci ty to make reasonable  rules and r egulations for

relative to roll-offs, a nd stated his belief tha t the City should not be in the roll-off business a nd

Consideration of Bid #R-21-11 for furnishing 76 new computers and operating systems
for the City  of Chey enne, Information Technology Division (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax Capital
Equipment f und).  M r. Va ldez mov ed to  a ccept t he bid  fr om H oward Industries, Inc., Laurel,
MS, in an am ount n ot t o e xceed $ 39,672.00, s econded b y M r. B rown.  U pon in quiry, T yler
Nelson, Assistant Director, Information Technology Division, provided information on the age
of the c omputers that will be re placed; software compatibility issues involving older c omputers
and the Division’s annual user replacement plan.  Concern was expressed with the City’s Lotus
Notes e-mail system and effects of not acquiring the proposed equipment for a year to possibly
utilize the funds to acquire a different e-mail system.  Mr. Nelson confirmed the e-mail software
being used is up-to-date  and is compara ble to other softwa re, and stated upg rading to a new e -
mail system would be more c ostly than the fund ing that is available f or the propose d annual
equipment purc hases. The resa Sny der, D irector of I nformation Te chnology, revie wed L otus
Notes’ f unctionality e lements; im plementation o f t he so ftware pr ogram pu rsuant t o p ast C ity
Council direction, history  of the a nnual equipment re placement plan and e ffects on users if the
plan w ere no t in  pla ce, a nd e xplained th e pr ocess e ntailed in  re searching e -mail d ocuments
pursuant to a recent request for public records.  Comments followed on use of 5  penny money,th
as authorized by voters through the 1% ballot question, for the purchase vs. an e- mail system,
and cost per computer per the bid submitted.  Ms. Snyder advised the bid amount was the lowest
that has been received in past years, and Mr. Ne lson explained the cost of a business cla ss work
station computer is differ ent, including maintenance/replacement provisions, than the cost of a
computer for home use.  Motion ca rried.  V oting “yes” -- all members of the g overning body
present.

Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, advised r epresentatives from the establishment wer e present at the
Finance Committee meeting , but no questions were  posed to them.  Comments included
indication that the licensee involved has indicated TIPS training would be provided to employees
and time frame, per City Code, in which emplo yees must receive training.  Ms. I ntlekofer noted
that Police Chief B rian Kozak a nd Offic er St eve N eisen ha d discussed City  Code wording
regarding the time frame for training at the last City Council meeting and had indicated a review
should be made to see if any clarification changes should be considered.
There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body , May or Ka ysen
adjourned the meeting at 9:34 p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
March 24, 2011

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-03-28 | CLIP_ID: 211 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 211

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
March 28, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and
Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Mr. Pierson.  A lso present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Broyles, and pursuant to support received
by Mr. Collins and Mr. Allen, agenda items #14 & 15 were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr.
Collins  moved to approve  remaining Consent Agenda items, sec onded by Mr. Brown.  Mot ion
carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 14, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Collins each declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to
vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and the B icycle Station, Inc. respectively, and
vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker move d to appro ve payment of vouche rs as pre sented,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Inquiry was made concerning the following vouchers:  Casper Star Tribune
($89.96), legal notice for Open House Assembly ordinance; Cornerstone Programs Corp. ($10,000)
payment per c ontract for juvenile de tention services for F eb. 2011; Division of Victim S ervices
($1,163.46), return unused federal grant funds due to resignation of victim services coordinator; La
Familia ($750.00), adve rtising and live broadc asting for a nnual Super Da y event; Labor Ready
($2,131.92), tempor ary labor staf fing (Tra nsfer Station). Providing information on the
aforementioned vouchers were: City Treasurer Barb Dorr, Mayor Kaysen, Police Lt. Mark Munari,
Parks a nd Re creation D irector Ri ck Pa rish a nd A ssistant D irector T odd Feezer, Pu blic Works
Director Jim Elias, and Solid Waste Manag er D ennis Pino.  Mr. Spi ker move d to amend by
removing the voucher payable to La Familia in the amount of $750.00 from the payables listing to
allow for payment after services are rendered, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Feezer
stated he did not believe contract provisions ask for payment in advance.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Main motion as amended
carried.  Vo ting “ yes” --  a ll mem bers of the  g overning bo dy pr esent i n Co uncil Ch ambers.
Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Collins returned to Council Chambers. 
“An Ordinance amending Section 5.12.060, Sales and Consumption--Restrictions, of Chapter
5.12, Alcoholic Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City
of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to authorize the issuance of a Malt Beverage Permit or Catering Permit
limited to malt beverages, with open container waiver, in conjunction with adult softball/baseball
games and tournaments at the Pioneer Park Baseball Complex.”  Ron Kailey, Cheyenne Grizzlies
owner, confirmed that Cheyenne Grizzlies is a community business and explained the group provides
clinics for youth and fund raisers for non-profit organizations.  Reporting for Finance Committee,
Mr. Valdez moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Mr. Valdez moved
to postpone for 2 weeks (to April 11, 2011), seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon request, Mr. Valdez
withdrew the motion to postpone, with concurrence by Mr. Brown as second to the motion, in order
to allow discussion on the main motion.  Comments were made regarding activities held in City
parks that include alcohol consumption through issuance of permits, including particular reference
to an upcoming activity in Lions Park, and regulation of distribution and c onsumption of beer at
Grizzlies games at Pioneer Park through permitting and regulatory measures that Mr. Kailey has
indicated he will provide.  Upon inquiry , Rick Pari sh, Parks and Recreation Director, explained
many different events go through their office for permitting and diagrams outlining dispensing and
consumption areas are reviewed closely by staff prior to signing off.  He noted he did not recall the
aforementioned activity scheduled for L ions Park, and Carol I ntlekofer, City Clerk, advised the
activity involving a c onsumption permit was brough t to her attention shortly  before the c urrent
meeting, advising that a consumption permit had been issued for the event, which was listed on the
application form as a run and barbeque.  Discussion included the following topics: Ordinance intent;
permit application staff review and approval process; various venues and retail marketing/products
that expose youth to alcohol; fairness and equality issues; limitation on the number of malt beverage
permits that can be  issued at the same  location, as ou tlined in State Statute, and individual
application/approval process for e ach requested date by the Grizzl ies to serve  malt bever ages at
baseball games. Upon inquiry, Ms. Intlekofer clarified that a separate permit application would be
required for each date requested to sell malt beverages with separate review of each application for
approval, and Police L t. Mark Munar i advised off -duty police officers can be hire d to provide
security in conjunction with a permit to sell malt beverages at the games.  Mr. Kailey acknowledged
that he understood approval of a permit would be on a game-to-game basis; commented they will
provide extra security with off-duty police officers and that he was aware there would be an affiliated
cost, and stated if problems arose or sales were not financially favorably, he could decide not to
apply for pe rmits. He furthe r commente d that approva l of the ordinanc e would provide  the
opportunity to provide malt beverage sales, similar to other leagues in the state.  Mr. Valdez moved
to postpone for 2 weeks.  Motion died due to the lack of a second.  Comment followed regarding the
number of affirmative votes required (6) for an ordinance to pass on final reading, and the possibility
of a f uture ordinance being drafted to addre ss concerns and issues that ha d been r aised during
discussion on the proposed ordinance.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash,
Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Spiker and
Mr. Valdez. (#3915)
“An Ordinance repealing in its entirety , Chapter 8 .44, Solid W aste, and creating a new
Chapter 8.44, Solid Waste, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming.”  Mark Shubert spoke in o pposition to t he or dinance, c iting c oncerns w ith pr ivate
enterprise competition intrusion by government, and spoke in favor of preserving free enterprise and
allowing private companies to provide roll-off container services within the City.  Mr. Valdez moved
to approve on third and fin al reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Valdez moved to amend by
substitute, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, advised the
substitute provides for language clarification of waste collection regarding roll-off containers within
City limits by private providers.  He explained it is not the City’s intent to affect private haulers from
providing roll-off containers, and provided information concerning a meeting that had been held with
the private haulers to discuss the issue.  He reviewed proposed new wording for Section 8.44.030
E.6. as follows: “Prior to placement of waste collection roll-off containers within the City limits, a
commercial (private) hauler must provide to the City Public Works Director, or designee, a written
report containing the followi ng: name of company, address of company, primary contact name,
contact information (during  and af ter business hours) , and a description of roll-off c ontainers
including c olor scheme , number a nd siz e.  Ea ch conta iner shall be c learly marke d with the
company’s name and business phone prior to placement.  Commercial (private) haulers shall provide
the written report upon business startup and annually thereafter not later than January 31  of eachst
year.  Failure to provide this report could result in a suspension of roll-off container privileges for
a period of up to one year.  Failure to comply with container marking requirements of this paragraph
could result in removal and disposal of unmarked/unreported containers.”  Brian Clark, Countryside
Disposal, suggested the City put a tonnage requirement on their containers to make it a fair playing
field.  Concer ns were  raised r egarding curr ent ordinance language ( also included in propose d
ordinance) referencing that all solid waste accumulated in the City must be collected, conveyed and
disposed of by  the City  under the  sup ervision of the Publ ic Works Direc tor, or desig nated
representative (Section 8.44.010 C.) and possible futur e interpr etation, and pr oviding f uture
protection to private hauler s with regard to revised wording in Section 8.44.030 E. 6.  Mr. Elias
provided information on the City’s control of the waste stream as a means to provide services and
secure future financing for solid waste programs, noting the revised wording in Section 8.44.030 E.
6. will not eliminate roll-off container services by private haulers, but is a mechanism for tracking.
Upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, confirmed that private haulers are not required to obtain
a City business licens e to provide roll-off container services.  Upon inquiry , City Attorney Dan
White advised he views Sec tions 8.44.010 C., C .1. and C.2. to specific ally preserve un iversal
authority by the City over disposal of solid waste with the limited exception, under the direction of
the public works director, of Section 8.44.030 E. 6.  He noted the public works director could
suspend the privileges of private haulers if they are not following requirements, otherwise, if a hauler
is complying with conditions, they will be allowed to do business.  Pursuant to suggested wording
offered by Mr. White in response to the comments of concern, Mr. Collins moved to amend Section
8.44.010 C.2. to include the following language at the beginning of the paragraph:  “Except as
provided in Section 8.44.030 E.6., ...”, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. White clarified the amendment
wording would be inserted as an introductory clause to the first sentence in Section 8.44.010 C.2.
to read: “Except as provided in Section 8.44.030 E.6., all solid waste accumulated in the City shall
be collected, conveyed, and disposed of by  the City .”  Matthew  Bowen spoke in support of the
amendment.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
Motion to amend by substitute, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend Section 8.44.050 B.1. by removing the 2  sentence (i.e.:nd
The city reserves the right to suspend residential collection during weeks that begin with a Monday
which is the fifth Monday of the month in order to maintain equipment and train personnel); the 3rd
sentence (i.e.: Notification of suspension of residential collection will be provided via a media
release at least two weeks prior to any suspension of service), and the word “also” contained in the
4th sentence, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Allen suggested a friendly amendment to the motion to
delete the words “A fifth week” in the last sentence of the section so the sentence would begin with
“A suspension or change in service…” with Dr. Rinne and Mr. Collins concurring as maker and
second to  the  mot ion. D iscussion f ollowed o n ma intaining c onsistent services a s is  c urrently
provided; initial section wording providing for flexibility regarding maintaining equipment and staff
training, and conc erns with the pr oposed amendment in relationship to fee s.  Motion to amend,
including the friendly amendment, failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mr.
Spiker. Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Upon
inquiry, Mr. Elias advised a copy of the proposed substitute had been provided to private haulers of
roll-off containers and that staff ha s not heard a ny opposition from the private hau lers regarding
wording in the substitute (Section 8.44.030 E. 6.).  Main motion, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body present. (#3916)
“An Ordinance amending various sections of Chapter 5.36, Circuses, Carnivals, Exhibitions,
and Amusement Rides, within Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne City
Code.”  Mr. V aldez moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr.
Valdez moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry, Bruce Wilson, Chief
Building Official, reviewed changes contained in the substitute ordinance pursuant to discussion at
the last City  Council meet ing.  Motion to amend car ried. Voting  “yes” - - all member s of the
governing body  prese nt. Discussion followed on public safe ty, cer tified NAAR SO (Na tional
Association of Amusement Ride Safety Officials) inspector providing amusement ride inspections
(reference to Cheyenne Frontier Days event was noted), and reducing the City’s liability exposure.
Concern was expressed with Frontier Days amusement ride equipment operators consuming alcohol
on their breaks then returning to operate equipment and whether language should be inserted into
the ordinance to prohibit this conduct. Matt Jones, Cheyenne Frontier Days Concession Committee
Chairman, stated the  current carnival ride pr ovider for Frontier Days has strict rules on a lcohol
consumption by ride operators, as well as other rules, and that drinking by operators is absolutely
not allowed.  He explained that spot checks and patrolling of the premises has been conducted for
several years, and that drinking activity by ride operators has never been observed; the issue would
be addressed if discovered.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present.  (#3917)
“An Or dinance submitt ing to the qua lified proper ty owner s of re al prope rty within the
boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District of Cheyenne, Wyoming,
excluding real property used exclusively for residential purposes, at a Special Election on Tuesday,
May 10, 2011, the following question: ‘Shall a special assessment of ten (10) mills be levied upon
the assessed value of real property within the boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development
Authority District, excluding real property used exclusively for residential purposes, for a period of
four (4) years pursuant to the provisions of Wyo. Stat. §15-9-217 (b) and (c) for the purposes of (1)
administrative costs; (2) landscaping or maintenance of public areas; (3) planning or management
of development or improvement activities; (4) promotion of public events; and (5) activities in
support of business recruitment and development?’.”  Mr. Valdez moved to approve on third and
final reading, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Ms. Ash advised she has met with the DDA and concerns she
had previously expressed are being addressed.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present. (#3918)
“An Ordinance De-Annexing from the City of Cheyenne, a parcel of land situate in a portion

County, Wyoming, containing 37.0 acres, more or less (located in the vicinity North of Interstate 80,

“An Ordinance amending a portion of Ordinance No. 3891 by modifying the conditions
within the zoning classification of MUB Conditional Mixed Use with Business Emphasis District
for Lots 1 and 2; and the north 66- feet of L ots 3 and 4, Block 195, Or iginal Cit y, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of E. 23rd Street and Russell Avenue).”
Mr. Collins moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  It was clarified the original
ordinance provided for an exception to build a small garage for funeral home (vehicle) use at the
location, however, the size of the garage is too small for business purposes.  The proposed ordinance
increases the size of the garage area.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present.
“An Ordinance amending Section 5.12.040, Issuance-Term of Licenses and Permits–Renewal
of Annua l L icenses, of Chapter 5.12, A lcoholic B everages, of Title 5, B usiness L icenses and
Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to impose certain additional restrictions
on the sale of alcoholic beverages pursuant to temporary malt beverage permits and catering permits
on City-Owned and City-Managed property.”  Mr. Valdez reported there was no recommendation
on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Allen moved to approve on second reading, seconded by
Mr. Collins.  It was advised the ordinance was brought forward pursuant to discussion concerning
sales of alcohol at Cheyenne Grizzlies games at Pioneer Park (re: ordinance acted upon earlier in the
meeting), and that the intent of the ordinance is to provide for consistent practices involving alcohol
sales and consumption on City owned and managed properties pursuant to temporary malt beverage
and catering permits.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend Section 5.12.040 B. 2. A. by changing the size of
alcohol servings (containers) from 12 f luid oz. to 16 fluid oz.  Mot ion died due to the lac k of a
second.  Discussion included:  Concern with ordinance effect on private and small family events
relative to posting of sig nage and othe r requirements; size of alcohol ser vings, and whether an
amendment to the current city code is needed.  Main motion failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms.
Broyles and Mr. Spiker.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez,
and Mayor Kaysen.
“A Resolution authorizing the City Treasurer to discharge uncollectible debts in the amount
of $94,648.02, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §16-4-502.”  Mr. Valdez moved to adopt, seconded by Ms.
Ash.  Discussion followed on c ollection efforts, debts owed by businesses still i n operation, and
insufficient funds checks and acceptance of future checks from those who have issued uncollectible
checks in the past.  City Treasurer Barb Dorr explained past and current collection procedures and
efforts; reviewed measures her office is taking in an attempt to collect debts prior to submitting them
to the collection agency, and provided information generally on the age of the debts presented for
discharge, which may involve billing or receivables errors.  Mayor Kaysen clarified that even though
the debts would be written-off, the City can still pursue collection for payment.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Broyles voting
“no”.  (#5267)

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, April 11, 2011 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to receive public input and
comments on the Chey enne D owntown Deve lopment Authority ’s (DDA ) 2006 Marke t-Based
Downtown Plan pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §15-9-208; said plan, available for public inspection at the
DDA office, One De pot Square, 121 West 15th Street, Suite 200, Che yenne, Wyoming, will be
reviewed for recommendation by the Cheyenne Planning Commission at its Monday, April 4, 2011
meeting, open to the public, at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 8:24 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 April 8, 2011

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-04-11 | CLIP_ID: 213 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 213

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
April 11, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Broyles, and pursuant to support received
by Mr. Collins and Ms. Ash, agenda item #10 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon request
by Ms. Ash, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins and Ms. Broyles, agenda item #14(b)
was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins  moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda
items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 28, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to
Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, and  vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Pierson moved to appr ove
payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen. Dr. Rinne moved to divide the question
to allow for a separate vote on the voucher payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers.  Mr. Pierson moved to approve payment of the Ameri-Tech HVAC Services voucher in
the amount of $30,437.50, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present in Council C hambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr.
Brown returned to Council Chambers. Upon inquiry , Todd Feezer, Assistant Director, Parks and
Recreation Dept., explained a voucher payable to All Sports Enterprises, Inc. ($16,900.00) was for
replacement of the floor at the David R. Romero Youth Activity and Community Center as a sports
floor, as originally planned, as the current floor type is not conducive to sports activities.  Main
motion, excluding the previously approved voucher to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a restaurant liquor
license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Re-o-na Sushi and Thai d/b/a Re-o-na Sushi
Bar and Thai Cuisine, 112 W. 17th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming. There being no comments or input and
the purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing and referred
the item to Finance Committee.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hea ring to determine w hether compliance of annexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exists for parcels of land being situated in the SE1/4
of S ection 1 1, a nd i n t he N E1/4 o f Section 14, T .13N., R .67W., 6 th P .M., L aramie C ounty,
Wyoming (generally located at Interstate 25 and West College Drive).  Jim Flesher, Urban Planning
Office, reviewed requirements and compliance of conditions contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402,
including specific information indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi).
There being no further  comments or input and purpose  of the he aring having been me t, Mayor
Kaysen closed the public hearing.
 
Mayor Ka ysen opene d a public hea ring to receive public input and c omments on the
Cheyenne D owntown Deve lopment Authority ’s (DD A) 2006 Mar ket-Based D owntown Plan
pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §15-9-208.  Christie DePoorter, DDA Executive Director,  reviewed strategies
included in the Plan for growth, improvements and opportunities to benefit areas within the DDA
District, and noted the plan inadvertently was not presented to the governing body for approval after
its completion in 2006.  Upon inquiry, Ms. DePoorter clarified the DDA special mill levy election
is scheduled for May 10, 2011.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing
having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
“Ordinance amending a portion of Ordinance No. 3891 by modifying the conditions within
the zoning classification of MUB Conditional Mixed Use with Business Emphasis District for Lots
1 and 2; and the  north 66-feet of L ots 3 and 4, Bloc k 195, Orig inal City, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located at the southwest corner of the intersection of E. 23rd Street and Russell Avenue).” Reporting
for Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded
by Ms. Ash.  It was noted the amending ordinance will increase garage footage to allow for storage
of vehicles utilized by a funeral home.  Motion c arried.  Voting “yes” -- all members o f t he
governing body. (#3919)
“Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established, Section
17.08.040, Cheyenne City Map, of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification
from AR Agricultural Residential District to MR-2 Medium Density Residential-Developing District
for Block 1, Lots 6-15; Block 3, Lots 1-15 and Block 4, Lots 1-8; HR-2 High Density Residential-
Developing District for Block 1, Lots 1-5 and Block 2, Lots 1-26; a replat of a portion of Tracts 7
and 10, Dell Range Addition, 2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, also to be known as Granite Hills
Subdivision (located north of and adjacent to Carla Drive, between Ridge Road and College Drive)”
-- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Mr. Collins).
“Resolution adopting revised liquor license general guidelines for the review of applications
for Retail and Bar and Grill liquor licenses available through the City of Cheyenne and authorizing
a time frame to r eceive applications.”  Re porting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to
adopt, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend #5(d) of the revised guidelines by
adding the language “or business whose primary patrons are under the age of 21” (years) at the end
of the sentence, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Discussion followed on determining who is considered
to be an adult (18 vs. 21 y ears); interpretation of “primary patrons”; past application for a liquor
license involving distance from a dance studio involving youth participants, and utilizing the general
guidelines as a r eference when considering appli cations.  Upon inquiry , Matt Ashby , Planning
Services Direc tor, pro vided informa tion on the approxim ate leng th of a ty pical block in the
downtown area.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and
Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Br own, Mr. Collins, Dr.  Rinne and Mayor
Kaysen.  Main motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  (#5269)
“Resolution authorizing the Mayor to terminate Lease Agreement No. 4745 entered into by
the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne American Legion Post 6 Baseball Club, Inc., dated March 28,
2005.”  Ed Melko spoke generally in favor of the Resolution; questioned fees charged by Post 6 for
use of the field by other teams and whether the City has the capability to audit Post 6 records, and
expressed concerns regarding use of locker rooms, signage revenue received by Post 6, federal/state
grant funding that has been utilized to build the facility and affiliated use requirements relative to
the City’s future availability to receive grant funds.  Upon inquiry, Todd Feezer, Assistant Director,
Parks and Recreation Dept., explained user fee tiers in place providing revenue to the City.  Buck
McVeigh, Post 6 Board representative, explained that Post 6 i s a not for pr ofit organization that
relies on sponsors for funding, and reviewed the history of how Powers Field resulted in being built,
which involved fund-raising efforts by Post 6 to match the federal Land and Water Conservation
grant funds the City received.  He reviewed usage of the field by other teams; use of locker rooms
by Post 6; revenues received from fence signage (sponsorship advertising); Post 6 baseball program
benefits to community youth; changes that had occurred in the scheduling process; Post 6's facility
maintenance and supervisory responsibilities and financial investment; components of the current
agreement, and a letter dated April 2010 that had been received from a representative of the U.S.
Dept. of Interior, Land and Water Conservation grant funds office, regarding equal and open access
to the facility by the public pursuant to grant provisions.  Mr. McVeigh indicated his willingness to
further discuss issues involved in meeting the City’s grant funding requirements.  Jade Lain, Post
6 coach and supporter of Post 6's baseball program, spoke in opposition to the Resolution.  He
discussed Post 6's baseball program benefits to youth and team mem ber criteria; limited revenue
sources available for the program; Post 6 financial investment and maintenance of field and facility
areas, and the need for another baseball field at the Powers Field location.  During his comments he
presented Mr. McVeigh with a check donation to help towards building another field, adding that
he hoped the City and other user groups will join together to help fund a second field.  Discussion
included the following topics: Issues involving availability of both locker rooms to other teams who
utilize the facility; revenue received by Post 6 involving outfield fence (sponsorship advertising)
signage, and ope n and equal  access to Powers F ield by  others pursua nt to L and and Water
Conservation grant requirements.  Speaking in support of the benefits the Post 6 program provides
were Rudy Apodaca and Warren Ayala.  Dallas Lain, Post 6 Board member, discussed interpretation
of grant funding requirements at both the federal and state level; need for continuing transparent
discussions regarding parameters involving use of the federal grant funds involved in building
Powers Field, and scheduling of users/games for the facility.  Upon inquiry, Mr. McVeigh provided
information concerning Post 6's utiliz ation of locker rooms and f ence sponsorship signa ge, and
feasibility of an on-site storage unit in lieu of using one of the locker rooms for equipment storage.
Discussion continued on negotiations between the City and Post 6 relative to the “sticking points”
that had been raised involving the grant agreement (entered into by the City) requirements and the
current lease agreement with Post 6; federal and state staff interpretations of the Land and Water
Conservation grant requirements, and fence signage.  Ron Nimmo, Post 6 Board member, discussed
availability to o thers to use the ba seball f acility; e ligible pa rticipants fo r t he Pos t 6 p rogram;
scheduling changes that occurred in 2010 at the City’s request; use of locker rooms; sponsorship
signage; facility scheduling; proper interpretation of grant requirements, and importance of field
availability to Post 6's program.  Mr. Valdez reported there was no recommendation on the item from
Finance Committee.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry concerning
whether the City could transfer its obligations under the grant to other City facilities under a 6 (f)
conversion process, Dominic Bravo, Wyoming Dept. of State Parks and Cultural Resources, State
Liaison Officer for the Land and Water Conservation Fund Program, provided brief information on
the 6 (f) conversion requirements and stated the City doesn’t have the amount of land needed to meet
conversion requirements due to past 6 (f) conversions that have been approved.  Discussion
continued and included the following topics: Future federal grant funding opportunities that may be
jeopardized if the City does not meet grant agreement provisions involving the current l ease
agreement with Post 6; communications and discussions with Post 6 to address issues; community
importance of continuing eligibility by the City to seek Land and Water Conservation grants; use of
locker rooms; sponsorship signage areas and available placements; public use of Powers Field
generally, federal grant agreement requirements and state parks involvement, and enforcement of
requirements by federal and state agencies.  Mr. Spiker moved to postpone for 30 days to allow for
further discussions between the City and Post 6 representatives, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Lain
expressed her personal commitment to work toward re -negotiating.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Fe ezer
provided information he was aware of relative to offers made by the City to Post 6 regarding a
revised le ase a greement, and Mr. B ravo state d the City  mu st be in compli ance with gr ant
requirements, acknowledging that the City is attempting to remedy the issues involved, however,
timeliness to comply will factor in at some point, and confirmed that accessibility to any part of the
facility (including locker rooms and a dvertisement signage fence area) must be provided.  U pon
further inquiry, Mr. Feezer explained if the motion to postpone fails and the City Council decides
to terminate the lease, Post 6 would be asked to remove equipment, etc. from the property, and that
scheduling for use of the field for Post 6 and others would be on a fair and equitable basis.  Upon
inquiry, Jason Sanchez, Grounds and Facilities Director, Parks and Recreation Dept., advised City
staff would do their best to maintain the fields if an agreement is unable to be reached with Post 6,
however, it might re quire a dditional manp ower.  Discussion continued on the purpose  of
postponement; parties to be involved in discussions/negotiations during the postponement time
frame; fair and equitable use of the facility, including locker rooms and signage space; compliance
with federal requirements and the  City being potentially disbarred from re ceiving future f ederal
funding if compliance is not achieved; potential litigation; correspondence that had been received
from the st ate da ted J an. 21, 2011 and A pril 11, 2011, and issues to be discussed/re solved.
Following further comments and upon inquiry regarding the termination time frame if the Resolution
was adopted, Dan White, City Attorney, explained if the Resolution is approved it only authorizes
the mayor to terminate the lease.  Termination of the lease would not be effective until 60 days from
receipt of notice.  He noted if the motion to postpone passes, the Resolution will come back to the
governing body in 30 days, or an amended lease could be considered if negotiations are successful.
Mayor Kaysen confirmed that, as Post 6 has a legal contact, the City Attorney, Mr. White, would
continue to be the City’s point person for communication.  Ms. Lane verified Post 6's legal counsel
will contact the City Attorney to schedule a meeting. Motion to postpone for 30 da ys (to May 9,
2011) carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash,
Mr. Brown and Mayor Kaysen voting “no”.   It was noted that the item will be back on the City
Council agenda for its May 9  meeting at which time it will be referred to Finance Committee.th
Following a 15 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 9:05 p.m.
[CA] “Resolution approving the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority's 2006 Market-
Based Downtown Plan (Plan of Development) pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-9-208” -- referred to
Public Services Committee (sponsor - Dr. Rinne).
[CA] “Resolution determining that conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402
exist for the property described as a parcel(s) of land being situated in the SE1/4 of Section 11, and

 Contract between the City of Cheyenne and WINhealth Partners for wellness coaching. Mark
Shubert expressed concerns with the cost of employee health insurance and commented on employee
vs. em ployer s hare o f p remiums an d t he b enefits em ployees h ave b y b eing provided co verage
through the City vs. health insurance coverage and costs in the private sector.  Upon inquiry whether
Council members, who may participate in the City’s health insurance plan, have a conflict of interest
in voting on matters involving the health insurance program, City Attorney Dan White opined that
it was not a conflict of interest as it does not represent an individual interest or benefit that isn’t also
received by other program members.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon
inquiry, Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, advised an end date isn’t listed in the contract as it is implied the
City will enter into another agreement for the term.  Tracy Peterson, WINHealth, advised contract
term dates are flexible for the program; stated there will be no pro-ration of fees with regard to when
employees may participate in t he program, and explained components of the wellness program.
Upon inq uiry, M s. Do rr pr ovided in formation o n p otential e mployee inc entives; City Cou ncil
determination, through c onsideration of the  annual e mployee terms and conditions ordinance ,
relative to whether  any  premium adjustment will be assessed to non-participa ting pr ogram
employees; City Wellness Committee recommendation that non-participating employees pay 25%
of premium share with participating employees paying 12%; purpose of the wellness program to
keep down health insurance costs and employee educational sessions, and confirmed those Council
members who are on the City’s health insurance plan will also be subject to the potential premium
increase if the y are  non-par ticipants of the we llness prog ram through WINhealth.  Discussion
followed on the approval of the plan running concurrently with the employee terms and conditions
for employee awareness of a potential premium adjustment, offering of incentives to employees;
other public e ntities’ wellness programs (i.e. State, County  and B oard of Public Utili ties), and
whether a wellness program has been shown to provide effective savings.  Up on inquiry, Ms.
Peterson explained the contract cost is based upon participants, and Mr. White advised amendment
language could be added to the contract stating the City has the right to terminate the contract.  Judi
Just, Accounting  Division, advised that any  proposed c hanges to the contra ct will need to be
approved by  WinHealth’s  attorney; provided potential wording  for the te rms and conditions
ordinance that would reflect program participating employees would pay 12.5% of premium costs
and non-pa rticipating employ ees w ould pay  25%.  Upon inquiry , Rich Wi ederspahn, Human
Resources Direc tor, advised it has be en appr oximately 2½ y ears since the City has re quested
proposals for health insurance coverage, and Ms. Dorr explained if the City changes health insurance
providers, the City could still participate in WINHealth’s wellness program.  Upon query, Mayor
Kaysen advised, in conjunction with budget preparation for the upcoming fiscal year, the possibility
of an employee cost of living adjustment is still being reviewed.  Mr. Brown moved to postpone for
2 weeks (to April 25, 2011), seconded by Ms. Ash. It was noted that amendment wording would be
drafted relative to inclusion of a termination clause and term for services within the contract.  Motion
to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Wyoming C hildren’s Trust Fund Grant Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Youth
Alternatives, Special Friends, and State of Wyoming, Department of Family Services, Protective
Services Division, to provide Outreach Mentoring Program.  Ms. Ash declared a conflict of interest
(due to employment involvement with funding) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved

April 25, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by

Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive input and comments for Fiscal Year 2011-
2012 Ann ual Action Plan, Ci ty of Chey enne Community  Deve lopment Block G rant (CDB G)
entitlement. Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development Office, provided an overview
of the CDB G entitlement prog ram and advised the ac tion plan is consistent with t he 5-y ear
Comprehensive Consolidated Plan; cited some of the identified Plan goals and objectives for Fiscal
Year 2011-2012, and advised the City’s estimated entitlement allocation amount is $481,610.00,
which is 17% less than last year.  Ms.  Widauf explained the grant application process; public
notifications and opportunities for input, and review and recommendations of eligible applications
by the Advisory Council.  She reviewe d proposed recommended projects for funding, including
program a dministration, a nd e stimated f unding a llocation a mount f or e ach. Up on inq uiry, M s.
Widauf provided information on the status of fede ral HUD funding and program administrative
costs.   There being no further comments and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen
closed the hearing.
“Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established, Section
17.08.040, Cheyenne City Map, of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification
from AR Agricultural Residential District to MR-2 Medium Density Residential-Developing District
for Block 1, Lots 6-15; Block 3, Lots 1-15 and Block 4, Lots 1-8; HR-2 High Density Residential-
Developing District for Block 1, Lots 1-5 and Block 2, Lots 1-26; a replat of a portion of Tracts 7
and 10, Dell Range Addition, 2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, also to be known as Granite Hills

Reporting fo r P ublic S ervices C ommittee, M r. C ollins m oved t o ap prove on second re ading,
