Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, May 9, 2011 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to receive public comments
and input on the proposed amendment to the Major Street Plan Official Map 2003 by updating the
Official Map with newly platted and built roads, and by adding U.S. Highway 30 and Dell Range
Boulevard int ersection, Ea st Pe rshing B oulevard a nd Chr istensen Ro ad in tersection, a nd U. S.
Highway 30 and Christensen Road intersection details. 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Monday, May 9, 2011 at 6:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to receive public comments
and input on the City ’s intent to submit  a grant request to the Wy oming Business Council for a
Business Ready Communities, Business Committed Grant, for pedestrian improvements to West
Lincolnway, and stree tscape impro vements on West 17  Street, Thomes a nd Pioneer Ave nuesth
adjacent to Block 392, Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming.
Mayor Kaysen recognized youth in attendance at the meeting. 
Under other business, Da llas L ain, speaking on be half of the  Boa rd of Post 6 L egion
Baseball, provided an update with regard to a meeting and discussions concerning the agreement
between the City and Post 6 for use  of Powers B aseball Field (City  Clerk note: Ag enda item
(Resolution) was postponed for 30 days at the April 11, 2011 City Council meeting).  She expressed
appreciation to City Attorney Dan White and Todd Thibodeau, Planning and Grants Manager, State
Parks, Historical Sites and Trails Division, for meeting with Post 6 Board members, and advised they
had a very productive meeting.  Affiliated with discussions concerning requirements under the Land
and Water Conservation grant funding the City had received to assist with construction of Powers
Field, she stated Post 6 has submitted a proposal to the City by the April 22 deadline, stating Postnd 
6 has tried to come up with a proposal that is fair, equitable, honorable, and “what is right” in order
to move forward with opportunities for all ar ea youth to participate in ba seball programs.  Upon
inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised no final decision has been made to date on the Post 6
proposal, and Rick Parish, Director, Parks and Recreation Department, stated review of the proposal
with Mr. Thibodeau for his response would be conducted.  Mayor Kaysen advised the agenda item
would be back on the City Council’s May 9  agenda to be referred back to Finance Committee,th
which will meet on May  16, 2011 at 11:00 a.m .  Upon inquiry, Ms. Lain reviewed the essential
elements of the proposal that Post 6 submitted, which includes completion of a second Blue Ribbon
baseball field at the current site, as initially conceptualized, and sidewalks, and advised there was
no written contract for a second field, but it was portrayed in drawings utilized during fund raising
efforts for Powers Field. Upon inquiry, Mr. Parish reviewed dirt moving and rough grading activities
that had occ urred during construction; stated a  conceptual master plan ha d been dr awn up that
determined two fields could at some po int be conf igured in the ar ea, a nd commented on the
importance of lighting if a second field was built.  Phil Maggert, Post 6 Board member, stated the
proposal is not a threat; that Post 6 looked at value to the community and a win situation in building
a second field, and commented on the benefits of the City and Post 6 working together to resolve
issues.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 6:55  p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 May 6, 2011

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-05-09 | CLIP_ID: 217 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 217

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
May 9, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
and Mr. Spiker. Absent:  Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Collins moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Gove rning Body on April 25, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Comments were made regarding a voucher payable to Scott Gromer, refund request relative to lack
of a recreation center in Cheyenne; funding upgrades to the municipal pool, and proposed projects
for the ne xt 6  penny sales tax ballot question as reported in a  recent newspaper article.  Uponth
inquiry, Rick Parish, Parks and Rec reation Director, r eviewed a vouche r payable to Col orado
Analytical Lab for Airport Golf Course soil testing.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present.  Under other City business later in the meeting, Mr. Parish provided
further explanation reg arding the vouche r payable to Mr. Gr omer, stating  the funds had been
previously received by the City from the Air  National Guard for recreational use by the Gromer
family, who wish to utilize the funds toward recreational opportunities in another community. 
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a restaurant liquor
license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for 888 Mr. Hibachi, LLC d/b/a Mr. Hibachi,
1439 Stillwater Avenue, Suites 1 and 2, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments and the
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the item to
Finance Committee.
 
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hea ring to rec eive comments and input on the p roposed
amendment to the Major Street Plan Official Map 2003 by updating the Official Map with newly
platted and built roads, and by adding U.S. Highway 30 and Dell Range Boulevard intersection, East
Pershing Boulevard and Christensen Road intersection, and U.S. Highway 30 and Christensen Road
intersection details.  Tom Mason, Direc tor, Metropolitan Planning  Org anization, ex plained the
purpose of the public hea ring; reviewed the pr oposed amendments to the offic ial map base d on
PlanCheyenne, the addition of roads, street alignments and future growth, and stated the Planning
Commission had revie wed the proposed changes and r ecommended approval.  He re ferenced a
packet of information previously provided to members of the governing body, and reviewed how the
map is functionally related to street/road classifications.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Mason reviewed rights-
of-way development and identification procedures for future Greenway path placements, and advised
the purpose of  the offic ial map is for a  street plan, not a Greenway map.  Comments followed
regarding placement of Greenway paths on the map, and Christensen railroad overpass plans.  There
being no further comments and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the
hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input on the City’s intent
to submit a grant request to the Wyoming Business Council for a Business Ready Communities,
Business Committed Grant, for  pedestrian improvements to West L incolnway, and stree tscape
improvements on West 17  Street, Thomes and Pioneer Avenues adjacent to Block 392, Originalth
City, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, clarified the primary intent of this
type of grant is to build community infrastructure involving a specific business who has committed
to relocate or expand in the community.  She noted the local business entity is Western EcoSystems
Technology, Inc., who plans to move its current local office, due to business growth, to the historic
Dinneen’s building on West L incolnway, a downtown r edevelopment project by  Dinco
Development, LLC.  Ms. Schneider discussed the grant funding amount being sought ($770,625.00)
and required 10% match if the grant is approved.  She stated the 10% match would be provided by
Dinco Development (cash plus in-kind land donation) and Downtown Development Author ity
(DDA) funding in the amount of $15,000.00, and that the grant funding, if approved, is just a portion
of the overall redevelopment project being accomplished through private investment.  Randy Byers,
The Design Studio, Inc., architect for Dinco Development, submitted schematic drawings of the
proposed redevelopment project (on file with City Clerk’s office) and reviewed major components
of the plan involving  widening  of the sidewal k, raised landsca ping, buf fering, stre etscape
improvements and enhanced lighting on 17  Street.  Mr. Byers verified there is no request for projectth
funding assistance  from the City  nor surrounding  landowners, and explained the in-kind land
donation based on the  setback area of the  Dinneen B ldg. as recognized through the  Wyoming
Business Council.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Byers provided information on trees that will be planted as
part of the landscaping; possible irrigation plans after trees mature; project compatibility with DDA’s
established streetscaping design, and street lighting plans for W. 17  Street and proposed placementth
of a statue  through private f unding by the Dinnee n family.  Appre ciation was expressed to the
Dinneen family for the downtown redevelopment project involving the area and for making project
information a vailable to  th e p ublic th rough th e u se o f s ocial m edia.  T here b eing n o f urther
comments and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
“Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established, Section
17.08.040, Cheyenne City Map, of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification
from AR Agricultural Residential District to MR-2 Medium Density Residential-Developing District
for Block 1, Lots 6-15; Block 3, Lots 1-15 and Block 4, Lots 1-8; HR-2 High Density Residential-
Developing District for Block 1, Lots 1-5 and Block 2, Lots 1-26; a replat of a portion of Tracts 7
and 10, Dell Range Addition, 2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, also to be known as Granite Hills

Under other City business:
Frank Cole, Cole Shopping Center, submitted information he received in conjunction with
his attendance at a recent conference of the International Council of Shopping Centers relative to
economic and financial contributions by shopping center operations in Wyoming (copy on file with
City Clerk’s office).
Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, introduced Elaine Carmichael and Tom Gallaher,
consultants working on the downtown placemaking project involving revitalization.  Ms. Carmichael
and Mr. Gallaher expressed appreciation for everyone’s help thus far on the project.  It was noted
the next public meeting would be held Thursday, May 12  at 5:30 p.m. at the Plains Hotel to discussth
how to make downtown Cheyenne a better place to live, work, visit and shop.
Tom Lindsey spoke in support of possible development of a downtown hotel and suggested,
due to concerns with parking space, having an underground parking level for the hotel and a main
level with a health food store and restaurant.
Comments followed regarding proposed City projects for the next 6  penny ballot proposalth
pursuant to a recent newspaper article.  Mayor Kaysen advised very preliminary discussions have
been held regarding proposed projects, and provided information on his meetings with department
heads; request for information on proposed projects by the local newspaper resulting in the article;
statutory process relative to a ballot proposal; preliminary meeting with County officials, and City
Council’s input and approval, and role relative to meetings at the County meeting level.  Additional
comments were made regarding communication with Council members in conjunction with media
information requests received by the Mayor, and Council representation involving the process for
determining proposed 6  penny projects.th
Pursuant to inquiry regarding lack of lighting in some Greenway tunnels, Mayor Kaysen
advised Parks and Recreation staff would check into the matter.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 7:00  p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 May 19, 2011

---

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-05-23 | CLIP_ID: 220 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 220

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
May 23, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker
and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Ms. Ash.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Lois Huff, Deputy
City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited, led by members
of the Mayor’s Youth Council.
EXPRESSION OF APPRECIATION TO MAYOR’S YOUTH COUNCIL AND STATE OF
THE YOUTH REPORT.  M ayor Kaysen and Ja n Spires, Mayor’s Youth Council coordinator,
commended members of the 2010-2011 Mayor’s Youth Council and presented members present with
a certificate and gift of appreciation. Those recognized were: Karolyn Hopfensperger, Dean Jackson,
Ian Dome, John Matsen, Bobby McGarry, McKinzea Olsen, Jacob Peter son, Gary Sayler, Isaiah
Sims, and Sabrina Westover. Members not in attendance also recognized were: Kadi Davis, Monica
Mencer and Josh Walker.  Ms. Hopfensperger, Chair of the Youth Council, provided a State of the
Youth report and described various accomplishments and issues the group supported and learned
about during the past school year, and commented on members’ positive interactions and learning
experiences through involvement with local government staff and community organizations.  Mayor
Kaysen expressed appreciation to the members for their time, commitment and dedication as Youth
Council members, and also to members’ families for their support.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Collins moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 9, 2011.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Brown.
Mr. Brown moved to divide the question to allow for separate action on a voucher payable to the
Downtown Development Authority (DDA) in the amount of $580,126.27 for a TIF (Tax Increment
Financing) invoice for Fiscal Year 2010, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Moti on to divide the question
carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all m embers of the governing body present.  It was advised the City
Treasurer was reconciling information that had been submitted involving the DDA payment amount,
and that a postponement of action on the voucher was requested to complete the research.  Upon
inquiry, Lois Huff, Deputy City Treasurer, reviewed the two most recent TIF payments, which were
in lower amounts.  Mr. Pierson moved to postpone payment of the voucher to the DDA until the next
regularly scheduled City Council meeting on June 13, 2011, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Spiker
raised a procedural point of clarification with Mayor Kaysen advising a main motion should be
introduced prior to a motion to postpone.  Mr. Collins moved to approve the payment to DDA in the
amount of $580,126.27, sec onded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr. Spiker moved to postpone a ction on the
voucher to June 13, 2011, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting  “no”.  U pon
inquiry regarding a voucher for costs of staff attendance at a New World conference in San Antonio,
Texas, Police Chief Brian Kozak explained attendance at the conference was to receive training on
the department’s records management software system due to the recent retirement of the system
coordinator.  Upon further inquiry, Chief Kozak reported police service dogs that recently received
veterinarian care were doing fine.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Huff clarified a voucher payable to the Brown
Palace Hotel, Denver, CO, was in conjunction with a workshop on CDBG (C ommunity
Development Block Grant) funding.  Main motion, excluding the postponed voucher payable to the
DDA, carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
“Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel(s) of land being situated
in the SE1/4 of Section 11, and in the NE1/4 of Section 14, T. 13N., R.67W., 6  P.M., Laramieth
County, Wyoming (generally located at Interstate 25 and West College Drive).”  Reporting for
Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.

U.S. Highway 30 and Christensen Road inter section details.”  Mr. Collins  moved to approve on
second reading, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Motion car ried.  Vot ing “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body present.
“Ordinance amending the City of Cheyenne’s Sanitation Fee Schedule” – referred to Finance
Committee (sponsor - Mr. Brown).
“Ordinance approving and esta blishing revised water rates, sewer rates, tap fe es, system
development fees, administrative fees, service charges and pe nalties and se ptic and commer cial
dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utili ties” – referred to Finance Committee
(sponsor - Mr. Pierson). 
“Ordinance appropr iating mone ys for the Cit y of Chey enne, Wyoming, f or the va rious
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year
beginning July 1, 2011, and ending June 30, 2012” – referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Dr.

Mr. Valdez).
“Resolution authorizing the Mayor to terminate Lease Agreement No. 4745 entered into by
the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne American Legion Post 6 Baseball Club, Inc., dated March 28,
2005.”  Tagg Lain, Post 6 Legion baseball coach, reviewed the club’s baseball program generally;
its mission to develop and build a high level baseball program, and the club’s financial investment
in the Powers Field facility.  He commented on past and recent meetings that had been held with the
City as well as other baseball and girls’ softball groups, and State representatives involving past
Federal grant (Land and Water Conservation) funding received by the City to assist with building
Powers Field; discussed information on projected costs to build a new, unlighted second baseball
field at the facility and previous site design and earthwork that had been completed, and reviewed
components of the recent proposal from Post 6 to the City, during negotiation discussions pursuant
to the agenda item, involving completion of a second ballfield (i.e. varsity high school field) at the
facility in conjunction with the long range plan that was envisioned when the facility was built.  He
stated his belief tha t during negotiation discussions i nformation had be en learned that had not
previously been shared, causing a lack of an open and honest dialogue; that the potential termination
of the lease agreement and community publicity involved has affected the morale of team players,
and that the c urrent lease a greement is valid and Post 6 has complied  with provisions of the
agreement.  Mr. Lain  noted his hopefulness that a solution between the parties could be reached.
Ed Melko stated that although he agreed another field is needed, Post 6's suggestion to complete a
second field was not a solution.  His comments included Federal grant requirements related to use
by others of locker rooms, and outfield signage (advertising by Post 6 sponsors); contract wording
that indicates the Legion must comply with all Federal and state requirements, and problems with
initial drafting of the agreement.  He suggested the City build a facility for equipment storage for all
users, that the outfield fence height be raised to allow for sponsor signage for Post 6 as well as for
sponsors of other teams, and that the proposal for a second field be placed on the next 1% 6  pennyth
ballot.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez stated there was no recommendation on the
item from t he Committee.  M r. Collins moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Brown.  D iscussion
included the following topics: Status of negotiations between the parties in reaching an agreement
regarding signage and use of locker rooms; potential jeopardy to the City of receiving future grant
funding if provisions involving Federal Land and Water Conservation grant requirements are not
met; quality field maintenance; progress of efforts toward amending or modifying the current lease
agreement; support for Post 6 baseball program, and concern with a financial commitment by the
City involving construction of a second field.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, provided
information on negotiation discussions and proposals, advising a City resolution had been drafted
outlining that the City would install sidewalks to the 1  and 3  base dugouts and build a securest rd
storage facility.  He explained reasons why the proposed resolution should not be “married to the
agreement” as the proposed improvements at Powers Field and compliance with Federal law (grant
requirements) are two separate issues; discretionary authority of the mayor to issue a termination
notice if the r esolution is adopted and ef fectiveness of ter mination (60 day s after rec eipt); that
continuing discussions can still be held, and elements of a formal mediation process.  He stated the
issue is that the cur rent lea se is not in compliance with F ederal law.  Pursuant to re quest by
Councilman Pierson to  Mayor Kaysen, as c hair, Mayor Kaysen advised the  public participa tion
process involving the agenda item would be flexible during discussion. Upon inquiry, Mr. White
reviewed communications with Post 6 legal representatives involving a draft of a revised lease
agreement he had se nt them on Dec. 16, 2010 which would have  resolved the locker rooms and
signage issues, noting he was advised that Post 6 would not sign the proposed revised agreement.
Jack Speight, attorney, noted he and Hank Bailey, attorney, were present at the meeting to assist Post
6.  He  sta ted Po st 6  wo uld be  de lighted to  se ttle the iss ues su rrounding the  le ase a greement;
discussed Post 6's response by attorney Dallas Lain to City Attorney White regarding the proposed
resolution involving installation of sidewa lks and building of a  storage facility by the City, and
explained that the Board would want the resolution tied to a lease agreement.  Mr. Speight discussed
trust factors between the parties; willingness to negotiate, and a formal mediation process involving
an outside mediator.  Mr. Melko commented in opposition to Post 6's stance regarding consideration
of a revised lease, and repercussion to the City relative to availability of future Federal grant funding
for many City projects, not just Parks and Recreation areas, if the current lease is not terminated.
In response to inquiry, Mr. Speight noted issues of conc ern are more than just locker r ooms and
signage, but involve terminating a valid lease and furthering youth baseball in the community, and
commented on extending the current lease agreement term by an additional 5 years in any revised
lease as “quid pro quo” with the City.  Additional response included comments by Mr. Lain on trust
issues, importance of a commitment for a second field, scheduling and use of Powers Field by other
teams, and pr oposed fund-raising efforts by Post 6 t oward a hig h school varsity  baseball field.
Discussion continued and, upon query, included confirmation by Mr. White that the resolution on
the agenda relates to the lease agreement dated March 28, 2005 and non-compliance of le ase
provisions with Federal Land and Water Conservation grant requirements as communicated by a
Federal grant representative.  Rick Parish, Director of Parks and Recreation, provided information
on Powers Field (baseball field and facility) shared maintenance duties between the City, Post 6, and
other users, and City maintenance responsibilities if the lease agreement were terminated.  Upon
further inquiry, Mr. Parish stated his understanding that if the lease is terminated, Post 6 would pay
for use of the fi eld in accordance with other use r groups.  Fur ther discussion included: I ssues
surrounding Federal Land and Water Conservation grant requirements; Post 6 program success;
building of Powers Field with public dollars and Post 6 fund-raising efforts; proposed draft of new
resolution involving the City’s commitment to install sidewalks and a storage facility; draft revised
lease agreement and proposed 5 year additional term extension; building/maintaining a blue ribbon
baseball field; use of field by Post 6; lack of playing facilities for girls’ softball teams; negotiations
and communications pertaining to a proposed revised lease agreement and new resolution; numerous
Federal grants awarded to the City for many community projects and jeopardy of future disbarment
from re ceiving g rant funding ; sections of cur rent lea se ag reement i nvolving r esponsibility for
compliance with laws and regulations, and termination; change in use of field scheduling effected
last year, and possible agenda scheduling of the new resolution draft and a revised lease agreement
to coordinate with action on the current agenda item.  Mayor Kaysen relinquished duties of the Chair
to Council President Rinne to enable him to participate in discussion on the m otion on the floor.
Upon inquiry, Mr. White reviewe d his recollec tion of  communications with Ms. L ain and the
sequence of events involving the new resolution that was drafted proposing the City install certain
sidewalks and build a storage facility at Powers Field, which was intended to not be tied to the lease
as it was a se parate issue from no n-compliance with Cit y obliga tions as a L and and Water
Conservation grantee, and clarified if a termination notice was issued the current lease would still
be in effect for 60 days, which would  provide the opportunity for further discussion on compliance
matters.  Mr. Speight spoke in support of resolving issues through further discussions.  Mr. Pierson
moved to postpone to J une 13, 2011, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  It was advised the postponement
would allow for introduction of the new resolution that had been drafted, under other business later
in the meeting, for referral to Finance Committee.  Comments followed regarding termination of the
lease to send a message of the City’s intent to meet Federal grant compliance, information contained
in a letter dated April 2, 2010 from a Federal representative for the Land and Water Conservation
grant program to the State’s grant program representative indicating the current lease agreement was
not in compliance, and support for a new resolution and the December 2010 revised agreement draft.
Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez. Voting “no” --
Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins and Mayor Kaysen. [City Clerk note: Dr. Rinne,
presiding as chair, chose not to vote]  Main motion to adopt the resolution carried.  Voting “yes” --
Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mayor Kaysen and Dr. Rinne, who, as chair, voted to break
a tie vote. Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez. (#5275)  Following
announcement of the vote, President Rinne returned the duties of the chair to Mayor Kaysen.  Upon
inquiry under other business later in the meeting, Mayor Kaysen clarified the City will continue to
try to reach a resolution with Post 6 prior to any final termination date of the current lease agreement.
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at approximately 8:35
p.m.
“Resolution authorizing the May or and the  City Clerk to sign a  final plat for Chey enne

Filing; Lots 2 and 3, Block 2 and Lots 5 and 6, Block 1, Cheyenne Business Parkway, 3rd Filing,
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to Christensen Road, between Campstool Road

Cheyenne, Wyoming.”  Mr. Valdez moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Valdez moved
to amend by substitute, which changes the grant request amount to $770,625.00, seconded by Mr.

“Resolution se lecting a  pr eferred p roposal a nd a uthorizing the  May or to e nter i nto
negotiations for the sale of approximately 28 acres of City-owned land located near the southeast
corner of the inter section of Windmill  Road and De ll Range Boulevard, Cheyenne, Wyoming.”
Scott Nuttelman, Menards representative, spoke in favor of the proposal submitted by Menards to
potentially acquire the site.  He commented they have met with staff on several occasions to discuss
potential sites for the development of a Menards in the community, and advised he views the project
as more than just the construction of a Menards store but also for infrastructure improvements,
commercial development, building of streets, and as an enhancement to the Greenway and parks
space within the vicinity.  Upon inquiry, he clarified Menards would use approximately 20 acres of
the site and that a few lots along Dell Range Blvd. could be available for further development that
would complement the Me nards store. He advised the store’s  concept plan in size and footprint
would be almost identical to the Casper store.  Mr. Valdez reported there was no recommendation
on the item from Finance Committee. Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr.
Pierson, seconded by Mr. Collins, moved to amend as follows: Modify the Resolution entitlement
by deleting the language “selecting a preferred proposal and”; deleting the 2  and 5  “Whereas”nd th
clauses, and dele ting “with _____” contained in the “ Now, Therefore, Be It Resolved” cla use.
Discussion followed on the intent of the amendments, which is to provide authority for the Mayor
to negotiate with interested parties;  opportunity for discussion with other interested developers;
receiving an updated property appraisal, and issuance of a Request for Proposals (RFP), resulting
in one proposal being received.  During discussion and upon suggestion, Mr. Pierson revised his
previous motion to amend to include deletion of the word “preferred” in the 3  “Whereas” clauserd
with concurrence by Mr. Collins, as second to the motion to amend.  Motion to amend, as revised,
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Brown
voting “no”.  Upon inquiry regarding use of proceeds if the property was sold, Dan White, City
Attorney, discussed Parks a nd Recre ation documents af filiated with a pa st relea se of a  g rant
boundary designation under Section 6 (f) [Federal Land and Water Conservation Act] provisions,
and stated further research is being conducted to ascertain how any potential property sales proceeds
may be  us ed.  Up on inqui ry, Mayor Ka ysen c larified th e int ent o f t he re solution is t o b egin
negotiation discussions and that no final dollar amount involving a  sale ha s been de termined.
Continuing discussion inc luded the following  topics: Concerns with the City  disposing of the
property; history of City ownership and allowable property use since acquisition of Section 28;
traffic and safety concerns pursuant to commercial development near Dell Range; use of the property
by the public since the City acquired ownership; previously approved PlanCheyenne amendment and
change in zoning involving the property; current depressed property market value; use of any sales
proceeds for Parks and Recreation purposes; past discussions involving potential sale of the property,
and commercial development in the are a.  During discussion, Councilman Allen referenced and
reviewed written information he had previously submitted to members of the governing body (on
file with  office of City Clerk) in opposition to sale of the property, and referred to 3 maps he
displayed during his comments.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Ms.
Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Dr.
Rinne and Mr. Valdez.  (#5279)

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY

June 13, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Kilee Stahley and Da llan Whistance, representatives of the  2011 Gre eley Independence
Stampede Committee, were in attendance to present a proclamation issued by the Committee to the
governing body.  Ms. Stahley read the proclamation and presented it to the governing body.  She
noted June 28  has been designated as Cheyenne Day during the upcoming 89th anniversary of theth
Greeley Stampede.  Ms. Stahley and Mr. Whistance invited members of the governing body and the
Cheyenne community to attend this year’s event in Greeley, CO. 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be

other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by
Ms. Ash and Mr. Brown agenda item #19 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins
moved to approve  remaining Consent Agenda items, sec onded by Mr. B rown.  Motion car ried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gove rning Body on May  28, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to

Voting “yes” -- all members of  the g overning body.  Mr. Spiker moved to appr ove the vouc her

Governing B ody meeting , seconde d by  Mr. Pierson.  Ms. Dorr requested the vouche r not be

Ordinance amending the Major Street Plan Official Map 2003, by up-dating the entire Major

Ordinance amending the City of Chey enne’s Sanitation Fee  Schedule.  Ed Me lko, John
Palmer, Edward Ernste and James Perot McNeil made comments in opposition to any fee increases
at t his t ime, referencing hardships on residents on fixed incomes, annexation and development
growth as a dditional revenue to help offse t costs, and us e of sanitation revenues to support the
general fund.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  Discussion included educating the public on how sanitation revenues are
used; assessing fees for multiple unit residential buildings; yard waste containers; concern with fee
increase effects on individuals on fixed incomes; municipalities’ limitation on generating revenue
to support City services as restricted by state law; general fund vs. enterprise fund; recycling efforts;
increases based on the Consumer Price Index amount of 3.2%; compost facility and transfer station
weekend hours, and estimated amount of revenue that would be generated if proposed fee schedule
increases were approved (i.e. approximately $250,000.00).  During  comments and upon inquiry
information was provided by Jim Elias, Publ ic Works Direc tor, concerning assessment of f ees
specifically for buildings that have multiple tenants; recouping cost of services that are provided;
availability of yard waste containers and a proposed $10 fee to pick up yard waste containers at the
end of the season if individuals do not wish to participate in the yard waste pick-up program, and
fees to assist with the cost of processing recyclable materials.  He discussed overtime labor costs and
indicated they will review Saturday hours at the compost facility and transfer station for possible
adjustment.  Upon inq uiry, Ba rb Dorr , City  Trea surer, advised Sanitation fee s have be en
incorporated within the g eneral fund since  at least the 1980s, a nd indicated the pr oposed 3.2%
increase adds up to approximately $250,000.00 in projected revenue for next year’s budget.  Motion
carried.  Voting “ yes” -- Ms. Ash, Ms. Br oyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. P ierson, Dr. Rinne and May or
Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Ordinance approving and establishing re vised water rates, sewer rates, tap fe es, system
development fees, administrative fees, service charges and pe nalties and septic a nd commercial
dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities.  Edward Ernste, local home builder;
Ed Melko; J ohn Palmer; Pacomio  Sandoval; J ames Perot Mc Neil; Charlie Giroux; P aul
Frauendienst, President, Southeast Wyoming Homebuilders Assoc., and local general contractor; Joe
Patterson, developer/home builder, Dakota Crossing Subdivision, and B ill Edwards, deve loper,
Saddle Ridge Subdivision, spoke in opposition to increased fees.  Topics discussed included: Ability
to build afforda ble homes; Boa rd of Public Utilities (BOPU) f ees vs. those a ssessed in other
Wyoming communities; whether fee adjustments are justified; use of revenues by BOPU; effect of
increases on individuals on limit ed incomes; burdening tax payers; current economic conditions
relative to residential housing sales; increased costs for food and medical expenses; infrastructure
improvements paid by contractors/developers affiliated with housing developments; water and sewer
tap fees; encouraging efficient “green” building pr actices, and prope rty appraisals affecting the
housing market.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr.
Valdez moved to amend by substitute Exhibit A, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Randy Hays, BOPU
administration, advised the amendment reflects a proposed rate decrease on oil exploration charges
effective July 1, 2011, with other proposed rates to become effective Jan. 1, 2012.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry, Tim Wilson, Director,
BOPU, verified that last year’s rate increase wound up being less than the proposed amount, and
stated if the proposed increases to fees, pursuant to the ordinance under discussion, were reduced,
planned projects would need to be reviewed for further prioritization.  He shared information on
proposed 2012 water and sewer projects; provided information on water and sewer reserve funds,
and re viewed a nticipated re venue a mounts that would be g enerated if the ra te incre ases we re
approved.  Dr. Rinne moved to ame nd by deleting Exhibit C affiliated with the  ordinance (i.e.
Schedule of Tap Fees, System Development Fees, Pump Station Development and Related Fees),
seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Wilson provided information on past and proposed
revenue generated from water tap and system development fees, and stated system development fees
are not used for nor mal operations and maintenanc e; it is set aside  to assist with the costs of
acquiring water rights and supply; water treatment; transmission mains and expansion of the water
and sewer systems.  Mr. Fr auendienst, Mr. Edwards and Mr. Er nste commented on the  costs of
building residential homes and reiterated their concerns with the proposed fee increases.  Topics also
discussed included the current status of available water for the community due to high snow pack
conditions and full reservoirs, and suggestion that watering restrictions be removed which would
generate more  water  sales, re sulting in more r evenue.  Discus sion followed on e asing water
restrictions; t he p roposed a mendment t o r emove E xhibit C;  f ee mo ratorium f or o ne year, and
encouraging development.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Wilson stated, as of March 31, 2011, the estimated
amount of water debt is $43.4 million and sewer debt is $36.9 million.  He explained BOPU could
meet its bond payment obligations without the proposed increases, but projects would need to be re-
evaluated.  Motion to amend by removing Exhibit C carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash and Mr. Brown voting “no”.  Upon further
inquiry, Mr. Wilson reviewed proposed FY2012 water and sewer budgets, amount of BOPU debt,
and funds use d for pr ojects, including amounts that could be utiliz ed from cash reserves.  He
indicated the amount of water reserves is sufficient, but that was not necessarily the same situation
for sewer reserves.  Concerns were expressed regarding removal of Exhibit C, which eliminates an
increase in fees for developers, but not reflecting the same consideration to other users.  Main motion
as twice amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception
of Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez voting “no”.
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:25 p.m.
Ordinance appr opriating money s for the Cit y of Chey enne, Wy oming, f or the va rious
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing the amount of general and

Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez. [Clerk’s
note: Refer to minutes below for additional action on this item]
Ordinance amending Ordinance No. 3897 defining the terms and conditions of employment
for non-uniformed employees of the City of Cheyenne.  John Palmer stated he did not personally
support the proposed $1,000.00 a year pay increase for employees based on his previous comments
during discussion on the ordinances concerning sanitation and BOPU fee increases, and questioned
employee Wellness Program requirements.  Rich Wiederspahn, Human Resources Director, provided
information on employ ee Wellness Prog ram participation compliance  requirements, stating the
information was recently issued to employ ees, and Mayor Kaysen confirmed the program is not
performance based and is not tied to any objectives or goals that must be met by employees.  Mr.
Valdez moved to approve on se cond re ading, seconde d by  Mr. B rown. Mr. B rown ra ised a
procedural question inquiring whether action on the ordinance should be taken based upon the
previous agenda item (re: proposed FY2012 budget appropriation ordinance) failing on second
reading.  Mayor Kaysen called for a 15 minute recess to allow the City Attorney the opportunity to
research the procedural issue.
Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 9:05 p.m.
Mr. Collins moved to reconsider the previous action taken on Agenda Item #12, seconded
by Mr. Pierson.  For the record, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer read the entitlement of the ordinance
proposed for reconsideration as follows: “Ordinance appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing
the amo unt of general a nd sp ecial ta xes a s p art of  the  re venue re quired to  me et th e sa id
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2011, and ending June 30, 2012.”  Ms.
Intlekofer noted for the record that Mr. Collins, as maker of the motion to reconsider, had voted on
the prevailing side relative to previous action taken during the meeting on the ordinance.  It was
noted the purpose of reconsideration was to meet statutory time frame requirements pertaining to
approval of the F Y2012 budg et.  May or Ka ysen c larified a motion to rec onsider re quires an
affirmative simple majority vote to pass.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body.  Mayor Kaysen re-opened discussion by the governing body on the main
motion to approve pertaining to the FY2012 budget appropriation ordinance.  Comments included
potential revisions to the proposed budget pursuant to previous discussion and statements of concern,
and procedural criteria time-wise involving a motion to reconsider.  Main motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
Mayor Kaysen announced discussion on the ordinance involving terms and conditions of
employment for non-uniformed employees was back on the floor.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by
substitute Exhibit A, seconded by Mr. Collins.  M r. Wiederspa hn stated the revised Exhibit A
changes the minimum hourly rate for Grade I entry level employees to $7.25 an hour.  Motion to
amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry regarding the pay
plan schedule and whether wording in Section 4. of the ordinance, pertaining to a proposed salary
compensation increase, was needed, Mr. Wiederspahn e xplained any increase has always been
included for clarification in the terms and conditions annual ordinance, and that the pay schedule
(Exhibit A) is utilized as a salary guideline.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, stated his
opinion that  the wording should be contained in Section 4. of the ordinance.  B arb Dorr, City
Treasurer, advised the pr oposed F Y2012 budg et re flects the increa se, and c oncurred with Mr.
White’s statement that the wording be  included in the compe nsation section (Sec tion 4.) of the
ordinance.  Upon inquiry regarding past salary adjustments, it was advised the adjustments have been
contained in the compensation section of the ordinance, which defines the terms and conditions for
non-uniformed employees f or the a pplicable f iscal y ear.  During  discussion on the employ ee
Wellness Program, Mr. Wiederspahn and Ms. Dorr provided information on blood draws and health
assessment form completion required for program participants.  D iscussion topics included the
Wellness Program; differing ordinance provisions relative to uniformed (Police) vs. non-uniformed
employees; proposed $1,000.00 employee salary increase, and ordinance effective term (i.e. 2012
fiscal year).  Main motion as amended by substitute Exhibit A carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen and Ms. Ash voting “no”.
Ordinance amending Ordinance No. 3898 defining the terms and conditions of employment
for sworn officers of the Cheyenne Police Department.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve on second
reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Valdez moved to amend by substitute ordinance #2 dated
June 8, 2011, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, reviewed amended
language contained in the substitute regarding supplemental income for officers injured in the line
of duty who ar e receiving workers compensation benefits and pr oviding for use of  sick leave ;
circumstances under which the City would provide medical insurance coverage for a spouse of an
officer permanently, totally disabled or who dies in the line of duty , and extension of health ca re
coverage for employee dependents until they are 26 years of age in compliance with the fede ral
health care act.  Upon inquiry, Police Chief Brian Kozak provided information involving officers
who have been injured in the line of duty and have been off work for over a year, explaining officers
in those situations often file for workers compensation benefits and then seek medical retirement.
He stated the amendment would require the officers to claim sick leave time after a year had passed
to reach the maximum supplemental income amount allowed.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.

[CA] Resolution authoriz ing submission of an a pplication to the Wy oming De partment of
Transportation for a Wy oming Rur al Public Transporta tion Progra m Grant for  the purpose of

Resolution amending the City of Cheyenne Annual Action Plans for Fiscal Years 2006, 2007
and 20 08 a nd re allocate fu nding to H abitat f or Hu manity Restore Annex f or a  Com munity
Development Block Grant (CDBG) project.  Josh Nicholas, Director of Development, Habitat for
Humanity of Laramie County, explained they were made aware of available recaptured funding from
information posted on the City’s website, and pursuant to their research on funding availability and
requirements, a grant application was submitted for the Restore project.  Mr. Valdez moved to adopt,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  During discussion on the process utilized for use of recaptured dollars,
Deanne Widauf, Housing  and Community  Development Office, advised the current process for
allocating recaptured funding is not a competitive process as is used for full grant cycle requests,
noting a competitive process is very time consuming and staff intensive, and stated, pursuant to
Council concerns expressed on advising the public of recaptured funds that may be available, the
process may be changed in the future to be similar to full grant cycle requests.  She stated additional
information regarding recaptured funding availability will also be posted on the website.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5281)

Resolution approving  an Outside Use r W ater and Sewe r Service s Ag reement with the
Transportation Commission of Wyoming (new State Tourist Welcome Center).  Mr. Valdez moved
to adopt, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Valdez moved to amend by  substitute, seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Herman Noe, BOPU, advised the Tourist Information Center will connect to the water and
sewer system next to the Swan Ranch Development and commented that in the upcoming months
BOPU expects to receive numerous user agreement requests involving the Swan Ranch Development
area as lots begin to develop.  H e advised the proposed sub stitute resolution removes a clause
indicating BO PU did not need to bring  outside user a greements involving the Swan Ranc h
Development area before City Council for approval; the change will enable each future request to
come before the governing body.  James Perot McNeil expressed concerns with water and mineral
rights in the area and spoke in opposition to the resolution.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Noe stated he was
unaware of any mineral rights affiliated w ith the lots t hat may be deve loped.  Moti on to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  Comment was made r egarding the
governing body’s authority to review these types of user agreements.  Upon inquiry, John Knepper,
Assistant City Attorney, reviewed a brief history of the governing body’s approval of outside user
agreements pursuant to a past resolution that is still in effect; provided information regarding the
governing body’s autho rity to review and a pprove B OPU outside user a greements, and noted
BOPU’s authority to approve outside user agreements pursuant to Wyoming Statutes.  Discussion
followed on the initial intent of the past re solution, as referenced by Mr. Knepper, providing for
review and approval of these types of agreements by the governing body.  Main motion as amended
by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  (#5284)
                                                          
 [CA] Resolution authorizing the Mayor to sign Lease #API-169 Amendment #2 with the United
States of Amer ica (through the United States De partment of Ag riculture, Agricultural Research
Service) for the purpose of constructing fire hydrants near the Board of Public Utilities Round Top

Resolution certifying that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne has agreed on City
projects to be submitted to the State Loan and Investment Board for the 2011 Countywide Consensus
Block Grant funding.  Ci ndy Schneider, Grants Manager, provided information on a proposed
substitute pr ojects list, no ting the  su bstitute re verses th e pr iority or der f or pr ojects c urrently
prioritized as #7 and #9, pursuant to request of the Public Works Department, and indicated some
project names have been changed for clarification.  She explained countywide consensus approval
process requirements i nvolving t he C ity, L aramie C ounty a nd t owns w ithin L aramie C ounty;
referenced a meeting of elected and administrative City, Laramie County and Town representatives
that had been held on May 17  to discuss the amount of funding for each entity; reviewed fundingth
amounts available and the agreed upon funding formula by the entities involved; discussed how the
City’s projects list was developed and prioritized through input by department heads, and noted a
grant match is not required.  Mr . Valdez moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr. Va ldez
moved to amend by substitute projects list, seconded by Mr. Pierson. It was advised those projects
prioritized as #7 (Replacement of Roofs, Garage Doors and Windows in Municipal Bldgs.) and #9
(Street & Alley Facility Expansion) have been reversed in priority order on the substitute lis t.
Comment was made on the  priority  level (#13)  listed for the L incolnway Utility  Corridor
Infrastructure project, and the desire to partner with the state and Downtown Development Authority
to accomplish the project.  Motion to amend by substitute projects list carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body.  Dr. Rinne moved to ame nd the substitute list by changing the
priority order of projects as follows, seconded by Mr. Collins:  Leave #1 (Replace Underground Fuel
Tanks at Sanitation/Cemetery/Lube Shop) and #2 (Pioneer Baseball Field Lighting Safety Upgrade
(including scoreboard), Pioneer Park Concession, Score Booth, Restroom Replacement) as is; change
#3 (Ten ne w (Police De partment) vehicles and a  Server for Came ra System) to #6; chang e #4
(Reconstruct Campstool Rd.) to #5; change #5 (Pressure Management Phase I Construction-BOPU)
to #3; change #6 (New Aerial Fire Truck/partial funding) to #7; change #7 (Replacement of Roofs,
Garage Doors and Windows in Municipal Bldgs.) to #8; change #8 (Cheyenne Jr. League Facility
Replacement: Restrooms, Concessions, Storage and Maintenance Facility) to #9; change #9 (Street
& Alley Facility Expansion) to #10; change #10 ((Dunbar Field Lighting Safety Upgrade/Grounding
Correction (including scoreboard)) to #11; change #11 (Transfer Station Facility Remodel) to #12
change #12 (Holliday Park & Mylar Park Restroom Replacement) to #13; change #13 (Lincolnway
Utility Corridor Infrastructure/New Development) to #4 and reduce the requested funding amount
from $2,000,000.00 to $1,000,000.00, and leave #14 (Windmill Site Infrastructure: Water & Sewer)
as is. Upon re quest, Ca rol Intlekofer, City Cle rk, r eviewed th e pr oposed a mendment c hanges.
Christie DePoorter, Executive Director, Downtown Development Authority (DDA), spoke in support
of moving the Lincolnway Utility Corridor Infrastructure project, which involves the “downtown
hole” (as a result of a fire that occurred several years ago), to a higher priority, from #13 to #4.  She
advised improving the downtown hole has been a priority for downtown development to assist with
enticing interest by private developers.  Noting a discrepancy in the total funding request amount for
priority projects vs. substitute projects on the list affiliated with the motion to amend, Mr. Brown
offered a friendly amendment to the motion to amend on the floor by reducing the funding request
for the Lincolnway Utility Corridor project from $1,000,000.00 to $978,750.00.  Dr. Rinne, as maker
of the motion to amend, and Mr. Collins, as second to the motion, concurred with acceptance of the
friendly amendment to the motion to amend.  James Perot McNeil spoke in fa vor of making the
downtown hole a higher priority.  Discussion included the estimated cost for the utility corridor (just
under $1,000,000.00); obtaining a commitment by a developer who has expressed an interest in
building a hotel near the corridor; potential funding assistance that may be available through the
Wyoming Business Council, Economic Development Joint Powers Board, DDA and other entities
who may be interested in contributing to the corridor project; option of re-prioritizing the projects
list if needed at a  later da te, and c omments in support of moving the cor ridor project under the
priority projects category, instead of substitute projects.  Upon inquiry , Ms. Schneider a dvised a
project must be started within one year of g rant funding approval by SLIB, and that an invoice
evidencing project work has begun must also be submitted within the one year time frame year after
the project has been approved to meet SLIB requirements.  Ms. Schneider verified the countywide
consensus block grant projects list is planned to be submitted to SLIB by June 30; stated review of
the City’s proposed projects list by Laramie County Commissioners is scheduled for its June 21,
2011 meeting; advised there are additional SLIB meetings throughout the year when projects could
be submitted, and reviewed the process that is allowed by SLIB if any projects approved for funding
need to be  re-prioritized.  Discus sion continued on the time fra me in which to obtain a wr itten
commitment by the interested hotel developer relative to grant application deadlines involving the
corridor project; Campstool Road project, and concern with changing the priority of the Pressure
Management Phase I Construction project for BOPU from #5 to #3 and seeking funding through the
consensus block grant process vs. budgeting the City’s $1 million dollar bond obligation payment
to BOPU.  Upon inquiry regarding the reconstruction of Campstool Road project and recent state
legislation,  Mayor Kaysen advised dollars have been set aside by the State to help with county road
development, not roads located within municipal boundaries.  Upon request, Ms. Intlekofer reviewed
the proposed project priority changes to the projects list pursuant to the pending motion to amend,
including the friendly amendment that was offered and accepted.  Motion to amend the substitute
projects list , including the  friendly  am endment, carried.  Voting  “y es” - - all member s of the
governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash voting “no”.  Ms. Ash moved to amend the amended
substitute projects list by striking priority project #3 -- Pressure Management Phase I Construction
(BOPU project) from the list and adjusting the priority numbers of remaining items accordingly,
seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Comment was made that the $1 million dollar bond obligation payment
to the BOPU should be funded in the budget instead of including  the project on the block g rant
projects list.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson and Dr.
Rinne.  Voting “ no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. B rown, Mr. Collin s, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Va ldez and May or
Kaysen.  Comments followed on the importance of the Campstool Road project, and the concept that
the City would own the Lincolnway Utility Corridor Infrastructure, if constructed.  Main motion as
amended by substitute projec ts list , as ame nded, ca rried.  V oting “ yes” - - all member s of the
governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash and Mr. Pierson voting “no”.  (#5286)

2011 at 12:30 p.m. in City Council Chambers for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and
Board of Public Utilities for fiscal year 2012.

and water and oil rights in the area.

MEETING: Budget Hearing/Special Session of the Governing Body | DATE: 2011-06-20 | CLIP_ID: 228 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 228

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
June 20, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne convened in special session at 12:39 p.m. in
Council Ch ambers f or th e p urpose o f a cting u pon th e f iscal y ear 2 012 b udget a ppropriation
ordinance on 3  and final reading.  Those present were:  MAYOR: Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCILrd
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Pierson,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present were: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer; Randy Hays, Administrative Manager, Board of Public Utilities, and Erik Rasmussen,
Deputy City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
An Ordinance appropriating moneys for the City  of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various
purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing the amount of general and
special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for the fiscal year
beginning July 1, 2011, and ending June 30, 2012.  Edward Melko commented on the budget being
approved prematurely due to potential changes in the propose d budget.  Reporting  for F inance
Committee, Mr. Valdez  moved to approve the ordinance on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.
Pierson.  Mr. Valde z moved to amend by  removing funding for an employee salary study in the
amount of $75,000 and $220,000 from the general contingency fund, seconded by Mr. Pierson.
Vicki Nemecek, Assistant Public Works Director, advised of the need to offset sanitation costs if a
separate ordinance proposing a 3.2% sa nitation fees increase does not pass on f inal reading, and
provided information on the status of the Solid Waste fund and estimated increase ($800,000) in
funding that may be needed in fiscal year 2013 to cover such expenses as tipping fees, recycling,
equipment leases, fuel costs, additional areas of coverage due to annexation, etc.  She stated there
are no sanitation emergency funds available.  Claus Johnson spoke in favor of retaining the funding
for the employee organizational, classification and wage study, noting the study would provide a
future roadmap relative to staffing levels, position descriptions and responsibilities, and would assist
with retaining staff.  Mr. Melko commented on the hauling of solid waste to Ault, CO, landfill status,
consolidated landfills, and potential trash to energy technology.  Comments followed regarding the
proposed amendment to remove $220,000 f rom the g eneral contingency fund to offse t loss of
projected re venue thr ough incre ased sa nitation fees a s there w as no F inance Commi ttee
recommendation to approve that separate ordinance still pending before the governing body, and
concerns with a previous employee salary survey that was conducted.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend
by friendly amendment to the motion on the floor to keep funding for the employee study but reduce
the budgeted amount to $45,000 instead of $75,000, noting that Police and Fire Departments can
conduct an in-house salary study for their areas.  Motion died due to lack of a second.  Discussion
followed on the pros and cons of conducting an employee salary, classification and staffing level
study; support for the proposed budget as initially submitted; timing of amendments to the budget
being proposed; budget serving as an estimate and adjusting the budget, if necessary, through the re-
appropriation process; ordinances still pending before the g overning body concerning proposed
sanitation a nd B OPU f ee i ncreases a nd a ffiliated r evenue estimates; f iduciary r esponsibility;
delineating sanitation reve nue and c ost information for the public, a nd previous year’s financial
concerns during budget preparation which resulted in reductions in workforce.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Voting “no” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Brown, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen. Mr. Valdez moved to amend
by removing the estimated revenue amount of $186,000 from BOPU (Board of Public Utilities)
revenue (water) increases and deferring the L ions Park Drive w ater main project  at a projected
expenditure c ost of  $1 74,000, seconded b y Mr . Pi erson.  U pon in quiry, B rad B rooks, B OPU
Operations Maintenance, explained the water main project involves repair of an existing 850 ft.
feeder line that ties in from the west side of the Kiwanis Community House and helps serve Peanut
Pond, ending up at the Airport, and also serves City buildings as well as commercial and residential
customers.  He stated the line has incurred maintenance problems, that the project could probably
be delayed but it is  always unknown how long a line that has experienced problems will las t.
Comment was made that the amendment is due to the potential scenario that the BOPU fees increase
ordinance may not be approved on final reading at the June 27  City Council meeting.  Discussionth
included Finance Committee recommendations regarding the pending sanitation and BOPU proposed
fees increase ordinances and timing of the ordinances for review and action by the governing body;
estimated revenues meeting estimated expenses for the budget, and clarification that the proposed
amendment concerns the BOPU water fund.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles,
Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez. Voting “no” - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash,
Mr. Brown and Mayor Kaysen.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend as follows: Include funding in the
amount of $25,000 to support Arts in Public Places (AIPP); cut the Fire Dept. clothing allowance
from $50,000 to $25,000; cut funding for a Planner II full-time position to a part-time position to
$33,546; with additional funds add $15,000 to Parks and Recreation Dept. for vandalism costs;
$18,546 to Police Dept. with $5,000 for access to crime reports.com; $1,000 to Parks and Recreation
for safety gear; $210,000 for Parking/Special Projects to upgrade the telecommunications system,
and remaining balance to go into g eneral fund re serves, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Motion failed.
Voting “yes” - Ms. Broyles.  Voting  “no” - - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. B rown, Mr. Collins, Mr.
Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Mr. Spiker moved to amend to
remove $135,000 in BOPU sewer revenues and remove $130,000 from the BOPU expense side for
the project to replace manholes, deferring the project to a future fiscal year, seconded by Mr. Pierson.
Comments followed regarding the need to increase the fees by 3.2% for the sewer operating fund to
keep it in as good a shape as the water fund.  Discussion followed regarding water historical volume
and reserve statistics; current watering restrictions and possible modification by BOPU Board to ease
watering restrictions; sales of excess water; necessity for revenue increases for the sewer fund, and
funding the manhole pr oject in the fisca l year 2012 budget to place less stress on future  funding
needs.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Voting “no”- - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, and Mayor Kaysen.  Upon
inquiry, Captain Mitch Maxwell, P olice Department, provided information available regarding
personnel salary line items and funding  amounts.  Mayor Kaysen advised the police department
active staffing level is down due to officers in the military, receiving training at the police academy,
and medical related leave, and that work shifts are being adjusted to 12 hour shifts.  Upon inquiry,
Fire Chief J ames Martin provided information on personne l salary line items and revie wed the
necessity for an overtime budget to cover expenses for response to fire incidents, citing the Hitching
Post fire as an example, and staff coverage due to firefighters out on medical leave.  Upon inquiry,
Ms. Dorr reviewed the proposed increase in t he treasurer’s budget relative to t he Deputy City
Treasurer part-time position being proposed for full-time, and explained the allocation to the budget
line item titled “Attention Homes” includes monies budgeted for $250,000 to cover all of the human
services agencies not a specific payment to Attention Homes.  It was noted the title of the line item
would be changed so it does not appear that one agency was receiving all of the funding.  Ms. Ash,
seconded by Mr. Allen, moved to strike $582,000 in wage increases for employees; $50,000 for 2
vehicles for Planning/Development; $67,097 for a Planner II position in Planning/Development;
$5,000 for Engineering Training; $5,755 for a part-time position for Forestry; $20,000 for Aquatics
lifeguard pay; $16,590 for a part-time golf position; $12,000 for Police aircards; $17,600 for 2 part-
time street striping positions for Traffic; $1,500 for dues/subscriptions, $2,500 for attorney fees,
$1,000 for training budget, and $8,731 for professional services, all from the Attorney’s budget;
increase in m aintenance in C lerk bu dget by $1 ,100; in crease de pot/downtown ma intenance in
Parking/Special Projects budget by $13,000; cut $18,654 in contingency funds; cut $32,000 in Civic
Center events and activities; add $1,000,000 to BOPU’s budget and $12,770 to Parks and Recreation
budget toward a par t-time position at Botanic Ga rdens (Children’s Village); strike $70,000 for
curtains from the Civic Center budget and $33,090 for a police officer position from a 1 year position
to a 6 month position.  Ms. Dorr clarified the Civic Center events and activities line item is contained
in a non-general fund budget and stated something else would need to be stricken in the Civic Center
budget to effect the proposals suggested for change involving that area’s line items.  Ms. Ash revised
her motion to amend, with concurrence by Mr. Allen as second, to strike $32,000 from the Civic
Center general funding instead of f rom events and activities.  Discussion followed reg arding the
budget review process and pros and cons for the proposed amendment and the budget areas involved.
Topics included: Employee wage increases; funding of the $1,000,000 bond payment the City owes
BOPU through the general fund and not from reserves or potential SLIB funding; level of budget
reserves; current economic conditions; part-time staff position at the Children’s Village; DDA tax
incremental financing (TIF) payment; need for new telecommunications (phone) system, and health
insurance costs.  Upon query and with no objection raised from Council members, Mayor Kaysen
provided information on the proposed amendment areas regarding the last employee wage increase
(Jan. 1, 2009); priority items that were included for funding in the submitted budget as identified by
department heads to mee t obligations to provide se rvices; $1,000,000 BOP U bond obliga tion
payment due in December 2011; status of telecommunications system, and anticipated current fiscal
year-end unexpended general fund amounts.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Dorr explained the $70,000 request
for curtains for the Civic Center will be paid through the general fund, and if $32,000 is cut in Civic
Center non-general fund expenditures then the same amount would need to be cut in revenues.  She
reviewed end of year encumbrances and the first reappropriation of the fiscal year 2012 budget
process to address unexpended general fund budget amounts from the current fiscal year.  Motion
to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash and Mr. Collins.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Broyles,
Mr. B rown, Mr. Pierson, D r. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and May or Ka ysen.  Discussion
followed regarding end of year projects and encumbrances; SLIB grant application and potential
funding time frames, and increasing the funding level from 1/3 to 1/2 for the Assistant Education
Director for the Botanic Gardens Children’s Village position and adding a part-time position at the
Children’s Village.  Rick Parish, Director of Parks and Recreation, provided information on funding
amounts for the full-time position at the Children’s Village relative to the difference in cost between
single health insurance vs family insurance coverage, and funding for a part-time position to provide
for Sunday hours at the Children’s Village.  Ms. Dorr advised the proposed budget, as submitted,
includes 1/3 funding toward the Assistant Education Director full-time position and to fully fund the
position $26,500 would be needed to make up the difference.  Dr. Rinne moved to fully fund the
Assistant Education Director position for the Children’s Village, utilizing the $30,000 excess in the
proposed budget due to a previously approved amendment made earlier in the meeting, seconded by
Mr. Allen.  Mr. Collins offered a friendly amendment to the motion to fund as much as possible the
part-time summer position for the Children’ s Village and utilizing any  remaining funds for the
Assistant Director position, with concurrence by Dr. Rinne as maker of the motion and Mr. Allen
as second.  Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 2:40 pm.  Dr.
Rinne announced, following discussion during the recess with representatives of the Botanic Gardens
Foundation Board present at the meeting, that the offer of the friendly amendment made prior to the
recess would not be accepted, with Mr. Allen concurring.  It was noted the Foundation would prefer
the City approve full funding  for the f ull-time Assistant Education Direc tor with any remaining
amount available  from the $30,000 g oing towa rd the pa rt-time position.  Com ments fo llowed
regarding funding of the Children’s Village positions by the City vs. the Foundation Board.  Pursuant
to the additional information, Dr. Rinne clarified the intent of the motion on the floor was to fully
fund the Assistant Education Director position first with the $30,000 amount, and fund the summer
part-time position as much as possible with the remaining balance from the $30,000.  Motion to
amend, as clarified, carried. Voting “yes” -- all members  of the governing body.  Upon inquiry
regarding money remaining in the g eneral contingency fund, Ms.  Dorr advised the a mount was
approximately $156,000.  Ms. Broyles moved to remove $25,000 in contingency funds and place it
in the Mayor’s budget for Arts in Public Places (AIPP) projects, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Ms. Dorr
advised the funding should be placed in the currently utilized fund instead of the Mayor’s budget to
enable funds to be ca rried over from year to year.  Ms. Broy les revised her motion to place  the
$25,000 into the budget fund suggested by Ms. Dorr, with concurrence by Mr. Valdez as second to
the mo tion.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Dorr stated the current balance remaining in the AIPP fund is
$34,616.00 a nd that  amount ha s b een c arried o ver i n th e pr oposed f iscal y ear 2 012 b udget.
Discussion followed on current available funding for AIPP projects; past funding amounts for AIPP;
AIPP Committee work and recommendations regarding community artwork, and no additional
funding for artwork projects being included in the proposed budget as submitted.  Motion to amend
failed (d ue to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and
Mayor Kaysen.  Votin g “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Br own, Mr. Pierson and Mr . Spiker.
Discussion continued and included possibly holding in abeyance some expenditure areas, such as
the $1,000,000 bond payment for BOPU, proposed new staffing positions, and vehicles, until a
decision is received pursuant to an application for funding through SLIB for a BOPU project; amount
of reserves, and $1,000 employ ee salary increase.  U pon inquiry, Ms. Dorr a dvised the funding
requested for 6 new positions totaled $333,500.00, and Mayor Kaysen reviewed the type and number
of proposed new positions, which includes the full-time position approved by amendment for the
Children’s Village, that would now total 7 positions.  Main motion as amended three times carried.
Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor
Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash and Mr. Collins.  (#3923)
There being no further  business previously  noted for the  special session, May or Kaysen
adjourned the meeting at 3:02 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
