MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-06-27 | CLIP_ID: 230 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 230

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
June 27, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker
and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Mr. Allen.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mayor Kaysen announced agenda item #17 was inadvertently
placed on the Consent Ag enda during prepa ration of the a genda, a nd, there fore, it was being
removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received
by Mr. Collins and Ms. Ash, agenda item #20 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon request
by Ms. Ash, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins and Mr. Brown, agenda item #16 was
removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Spiker moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  M otion carried. Voting “yes” -- all member s of the g overning body
present.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Governing Body on J une 13, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] Minutes from Special Session Meeting of the Governing Body on June 20, 2011.  Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Collins each declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to
vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services and the Bicycle Station respectively, and vacated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Collins returned to Council
Chambers. 
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive input and comments on a restaurant liquor
license application filed with the Cit y Clerk's Office for Capiro Ambriento, L LC d/b/a Capiro
Ambriento, 3617 E. L incolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Comments wer e made welcoming the
owners of the new restaurant to the community.  There being no further comments and purpose of
the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.  The item was referred to Finance
Committee.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive input and comments on a restaurant liquor
license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Morris House Enterprises, Inc. d/b/a Morris
House Bistro, 2114 Warren Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Dameione Cameron, applicant, reviewed
the history of the restaurant’s location, explaining it is the former Esther Hobart Morris house, and
provided information on restaurant hours and menu items involving southern country cuisine. There
being no further comments and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the
hearing.  The item was referred to Finance Committee.
Ordinance amending the City of Cheyenne’s Sanitation Fee Schedule. Reporting for Finance
Committee, Mr. Valdez stated there was no recommendation on the item from the Committee.  Mr.
Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Brown moved
to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director,
advised the substitute removes the proposed 3.2% increase in commercial and residential solid waste
collection fees, and combines the residential automated collection and recycle services into one fee
with the rate s remaining the same.   He also reviewed various areas included in the substitute,
including roll-off container overweight/overload fees; transfer station fees for drop-off recyclable
items; commercial a nd residential r ecycling and y ard waste container fees; reduced rates at the
landfill for commercial clean load recyclable materials, charges per ton for unsecured loads at the
landfill, and elimination of free pickup of bags left outside of containers.  Frank Cole, developer,
reviewed a past circumstance involving inconsistency in assessment of fees for an unsecured load.
Upon inquiry, Mr. Elias confirmed the substitute eliminates the proposed 3.2% increase in fees, and
provided information on fees related to recyclables brought to the transfer station, secured loads,
notification procedures, unloading cardboard containers, yard waste container retrieval and cleaning
fees, and staff services and costs.  Comments followed on the proposed substitute.  Motion to amend
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne
voting “no”.  Concern was raised on assessing a fee for dropping off recyclable items at the transfer
station.  Main motion as amended by  substitut e ca rried.  V oting “ yes” - - all member s of the
governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne a nd Mr. Valde z voting “no.”  ( #3924)
Mayor Kaysen advised the sanitation fee schedule will be posted on the City’s website.
Ordinance approving and establishin g revised water rates, sewer rates, tap fee s, system
development fees, administrative fees, service charges and pe nalties and septic a nd commercial
dump rates, a s rec ommended by  the B oard of  Public Utili ties.  Charli e Giroux ex pressed
appreciation to City Council for its consideration in not increasing rates. Mr. Valdez moved to
approve on t hird and final reading, seconded by Mr. B rown.  Mr. V aldez moved to amend by
removing Section 4, and re lated Exhibit A, involving wa ter rates, and re -numbering remaining
sections, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry regarding the Board of Public Utilities’ review of
watering re strictions a nd po ssible re vision to t he re strictions wh ich c ould p otentially g enerate
revenue through additional water sales, Clint Bassett, BOPU Water Conservation Specialist, advised
the Board has requested a recommendation be provided from the Water Conservation Committee.
He stated the Committee would be meeting at noon on Thurs., June 30, at the Laramie County
Library Windflower Room.  It was confirmed the amendment removes proposed fee increases to
water rates with proposed sewer rates remaining intact.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” --
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr.
Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Main motion as amended failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. Pierson, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez voting “no”. [Clerk’s note: Main motion for an ordinance on third and final
reading requires six affirmative votes to pass.]
Ordinance amending Ordinance No. 3897 defining the terms and conditions of employment
for non-uniformed employees of the City of Cheyenne.   Mr. Valdez moved to approve on third and

Ordinance amending Ordinance No. 3898 defining the terms and conditions of employment

Ordinance amending Title 17, Z oning, Chapter 17.08, Z oning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne City  Map of the Che yenne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from CB Community Business District to LI Light Industrial District for Lots 1 & 2,

Third Filing , Chey enne, Wy oming ( located south of a nd adjac ent to Venture  Drive, e ast of
Campstool Road).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on
second reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon inquiry regarding past re-zoning actions involving the
property, Jim Flesher, Planning/Development office, provided information on uses allowed, some
involving board approval, under CB and LI zone designations.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.   
Ordinance amending Title 17, Z oning, Chapter 1 7.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne Ci ty Map of the Che yenne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from CB Community Business District to LI Light Industrial District for Lot 1, Block
1, Hutchins Corner, 2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner at the curve
of Hutchins Drive, south of Campstool Road).  Mr. Collins moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon inquiry regarding allowable building designs in a LI zone, Jim Flesher,
Planning/Development office, advised if the zone change was approved current criteria for an LI
zone would apply.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with
the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.
Ordinance creating Section 17.116.155, Special Use Permit: Oil and Gas Support Services,
of Chapter 17.116, Design and Use Standards Generally, of Title 17, Zoning, of the Cheyenne City
Code.  Mr. Collins moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Mr. Collins moved
to amend Section E. 4. by changing 18 months to 12 months and by removing the sentence “Any
application that has not established the Special Use within 1 year shall be void,” seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  Brad Emmons, AVI, commented that a longer term (i.e. 18 months) should be considered
due to  the lengthy time frame involving an applicant’s site plan process.  Donald James Perot
McNeil suggested the governing body carefully consider the permit process for correctness.  Upon
inquiry, Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, explained the amendment would provide for the
application to be valid for up to 12 months with the ability to seek a 6 month extension, and John
Knepper, Assistant City  Attorney, provided infor mation regarding permit time fra me, extension
period and permit approval process.  Discussion followed on the permit time frame and permit
application process.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body
present.  Upon inquiry  regarding outside storage and sc reening of adjacent public rig hts-of-way
contained in Section B. 3. of the ordinance, Mr. Ashby provided information on what types of areas
would be involved in screening requirements; definition of oil and gas services; building permits;
code enforcement; site plan public notification requirements; landscaping, and what might constitute
“sufficient height” relative to screening requirements and certain types of equipment or supplies.
In response to inquiry pertaining to a specific site that may involve oil and gas support services, Mr.
Ashby stated he was not aware of any discussion regarding placement of a helicopter pad.  Upon
inquiry, May or Ka ysen advised oil supply  companies a nd contac ts from local developers a nd
landowners, principally in the eastern Cheyenne area, had requested the ordinance.  Upon inquiry,
Dan White, City Attorney, advised he was not aware of any exceptions in nu isance ordinances
involving certain effects, such as odors, pa rticulate matters, etc., a nd indicated if some unusual
activity occurs, it could be determined to be a nuisance.  Mr. Ashby further clarified uses emitting
noise and light pollution would require special board of adjustment approval and a hearing process
pursuant to the zoning code.  Comments included concern with undesirable aesthetic conditions such
as possible junk vehicles, tires, and cleanliness of the property, and support for oil and gas activities.
Ms. Ash moved to amend Section B. 4. to provide for a revised definition of maintenance to read
“Maintenance is d efined a s a ny a ctivity related to  up keep o f vehicles o r equipment t hat ha s a
potential of causing the discharge of chemicals, lubricants, or other hazardous substances onto the
ground,” seconded by Mr. Collins, as referenced in Mr. Ashby’s memo dated June 27, 2011.  Mr.
McNeil cautioned the governing body to make good amendments, and expressed his concern with
the fracking process.  Upon reque st, Carol I ntlekofer, City Clerk, re iterated the wording of the
pending motion to amend.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body pr esent.  Pur suant t o in quiry re garding wo rding c ontained in the or dinance re lative to
restrooms, Mr. Ashby advised he would further review the topic.  Concern was raised regarding the
height of screening and buffering requirements, particularly due to topography conditions.  Main
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present with the
exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.

Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for Ca hill Park

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-07-11 | CLIP_ID: 232 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 232

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
July 11, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
 Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins (arriving at 6:07 p.m.), Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Mr. Allen.  Also present: Kristy Anderson, Deputy City Clerk; Barb
Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Valdez, and pursuant to support received by Ms. Broyles
and Dr. Rinne, agenda items # 25 (a) a nd (b) were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Brown
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried. Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 27, 2011.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to appr ove payment of vouche rs as pre sented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for applications for two
available retail liquor licenses filed with the City Clerk's Office by One East Sixteenth, LLC d/b/a TBD,
1601 Central Avenue; Cheyenne Depot Museum, Inc. d/b/a Cheyenne Depot Museum, 121 West 15th
Street; Syvn, Inc. d/b/a TBD, 218 West 17th Street; MTM, Inc. d/b/a The Cowboy Grill, (address not
yet issued) to be located off Powderhouse Road between Spirit Lane and Gardenia Drive in The Pointe
Plaza, and Let’er Buck Investment Group, LLC d/b/a Steamboat’s, (address not yet issued) to be located
off Gardenia Drive between Yellowstone Road and El Camino Real in Cathedral Commons.  Mayor
Kaysen announced the application for Syvn, Inc. was being withdrawn from the agenda at the request
of the applicant.  Owen Groshart, partner, Mingles Bar, and Wally Reiman spoke in opposition to the
Cheyenne Depot Museum’s application, expressing concerns with a City sponsored entity competing
with other businesses and proposed liquor license operations not fully utilizing a retail type license to
its full capacity.  James Bowers, Executive Director, Cheyenne Depot Museum (Depot), explained the
Depot is a non-profit agency that receives no City, state or federal funding; the retail liquor license will
allow them to offer services to those people interested in renting their facilities for events and activities;
that the Depot is not trying to compete with downtown businesses, and that the Depot is a private entity
leasing the building from the City. There being no further comments or discussion and purpose of the
hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the applications to the Finance
Committee.
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and Section
17.08.040 Cheyenne City Map of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification from
CB Community Business District to LI Light Industrial District for Lots 1 & 2, Block 1; Lots 1 & 2,
Block 2 ; L ots 3  & 4 , 6 -10, Bl ock 3 ; an d L ots 1 -7, Bl ock 4 , Campstool Addition, T hird Fi ling,
Cheyenne, Wyoming ( located south of a nd adjac ent to Venture  Drive, e ast of Campstool Road).
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final reading,
seconded by Ms. Ash. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
(#3927) 
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and Section
17.08.040 Cheyenne City Map of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification from
CB Community Business District to LI Light Industrial District for Lot 1, Block 1, Hutchins Corner,
2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner at the curve of Hutchins Drive, south
of Campstool Road).  Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present. (#3928)
Ordinance creating Section 17.116.155, Special Use Permit: Oil and Gas Support Services, of
Chapter 17.116, Design and Use Standards Generally, of Title 17, Zoning, of the Cheyenne City Code.
Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon inquiry regarding
outside storage and screening of adjacent public rights-of-way contained in Section B.3. of the
ordinance, Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, explained the purpose of this section is to provide
a buffer from other properties and public rights-of-way with some flexibility in the screening area.  He
provided information on what ty pes of e quipment would not be in compliance w ith the heig ht and
screening requirements, and reviewed the definition for storage and types of equipment to be stored
under the permit. He further explained the ordinance does not prohibit re-application for a special use
permit after expiration of the permit following the 2 year time frame.  Comments followed regarding
concerns with  what mig ht constitute “sufficie nt heig ht” re lative to scre ening require ments for
equipment.  Mr. Collins moved to amend Section B.3., to remove the language “of sufficient height to
fully screen all stored materials,” seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Discussion continued on the importance of
some screening requirements as the businesses are located in the eastern gateway into the City.  Motion
to amend carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr.
Brown voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present. (#3929)
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and Section
17.08.040 Cheyenne City Map of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification from
CB Community Business District (Conditional) to LI Light Industrial District for Lots 2 & 3, Block 1,
Hutchins Corner, 2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located west of and adjacent to Hutchins Drive,
south of Campstool Road) -- referred to Public Services Committee.
Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat for Niobrara
Energy Park, a replat of a portion of Tracts 6, 17 and 18, Clear View Tracts, and a portion of the N½
of Section 9, T.13N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (located west of S. College Drive
(behind Lighthouse Baptist Church), north of Laramie County Community College). Brad Emmons,
AVI, agent for applicant, reviewed items they were requesting for changes in the affiliated Development
Agreement as outlined in a memo to the g overning body dated July 11, 2011.  He state d requested
changes include: Removing Condition #15, which requires construction of Allison Road to the west plat
boundary of the property; providing overhead power lines instead of underground lines as this is already
in place for the area and placing underground power lines would be much more costly; changing the
Resolution to di rect the Mayor and City Clerk to sign the D evelopment Agreement, and ame nding
several technic al/clerical i tems in the Deve lopment Ag reement.  L isa Paffor d, Building  and
Development Office, added that staff agrees with the content of the changes submitted and also agrees
with a 2 week postponement in order to make changes accordingly.  Ms. Pafford submitted a letter from
City Engineer Doug Vetter, regarding the Allison Road extension, that was provided to her before the
meeting.  Brad Graham, developer of project and member of Coll-Ind, LLC, commented on the project,
stating they are working with the City and County (re: Development Agreement issues) and that a n
application for a  Business Ready grant to assist with the project has been submitted.  He clarified he
is not opposed to extending Allison Road but the County would like to cul-de-sac the road; reviewed
the recommendation from Cheyenne Light, Fuel and Power with regard to overhead power lines, and
noted the intent of the pr operty location is t o serve as a site to loca te oil company  equipment and
supplies.  Mr. Collins moved to adopt with staff recommendations #1 through19, seconded by Ms. Ash.
Mr. Spiker moved to amend the Development Agreement, seconded by Mr. Collins, with 4 technical
items as follows: Section 3.a. - add the word “concurrently” between the words “built and “with” and
the word “road” between the words “of” and “infrastructure”; Section 3.c. - add the words “by legal
procedures of the entity with jurisdiction” after the word “vacated”; Section 5.c. -  remove the words
“Fire Department”; add a new Section 4.c. - “Lots adjacent to the existing school parcel will be required
to place a 3 ft. landscape berm with a 4:1 slope with a 6 ft. privacy fence placed on the top of the berm.
Landscaping will also be placed along the berm.”.  Mr. Spiker raised a point of order regarding the
motion to amend with May or Kaysen clarifying the motion on the flo or.  Motion to amend car ried.
Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Mr. Spiker moved to amend that the Mayor
has the authority to s ign the Development Agreement and changing the resol ution to re flect this,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
Mr. Spiker moved to amend Condition #15 to extend the right-of-way to be set aside for the west
boundary with the developer having the ability to create a cul-de-sac 400 ft. short of the west boundary
as recommended by the County, seconded by Mr. Brown.  M r. Emmons noted the right-of-way is
already set to be platted for extension to the west boundary and is a temporary easement for a cul-de-
sac.  Mr. Spiker stated if the right-of-way is to be platted he would withdraw his motion.  Mr. Brown,
as second to the motion, concurred with the motion being withdrawn. Mayor Kaysen announced the
motion was withdrawn.  Mr. Spiker  moved to amend to re move Condition #15, seconde d by Mr.
Brown.  U pon in quiry, M r. Em mons e xplained b uilding of  the  c ul-de-sac in t he ri ght-of-way in
relationship to potential future extension of the road.  F urther comments followed on connectivity,
future road extension and reservation of the road right-of-way.  Motion to amend to remove Condition
#15 carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Emmons
discussed overhead power lines vs. underground lines, provisions of the Development Agreement, and
requested an amendment to allow for ornamental street trees to be planted if overhead lines are allowed.
Mr. Spiker moved to amend to allow overhead power lines and ornamental street trees, seconded by Mr.
Collins.  Ms. Pafford advised that staff will proceed  with changes pursuant to the amendments with
final review by the city attorney.  It was confirmed neither the developer nor staff felt postponement of
the item was needed.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
Upon inquiry, Mr. Emmons clarified that overhead power lines will be planned; urban standards for
curb and gutter will be met, and, pursuant to the Development Agreement, a greenway path is also
planned.  Comment was made  regarding not having  overhead power lines as a standa rd for future
developments.  Main motion as ame nded four times carr ied.  V oting “yes” -- all member s of the
governing body present. (#5295)

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
July 25, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Absent: Dr. Rinne. Also present: Carol I ntlekofer, City Clerk; Ba rb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Da n
White, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Brown
and Ms. Ash, agenda items #13 and 20(a) were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by
Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins and Ms. Ash, agenda item #9 was removed
from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Collins  moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 11, 2011.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Service s and vac ated Council Chamber s.  Mr. Allen moved to  approve payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Dire ctor,
reviewed a voucher payable to Circle-S Aviation, LLC ($26,525.00) for aerial weed spraying services
at Belvoir Ranch and explained an annual spraying program for noxious weeds has been implemented,
particularly to control larkspur, which is poisonous to cattle.  He provided additional information on
repair/replacement costs of well (water) pumps.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council C hambers.  Following  announcement of the vote, Mr . Brown
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Ka ysen o pened a public hear ing to r eceive comments and input for  a tra nsfer of
ownership for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Cadillac Ranch
Bar, Inc. d/b/a Cadillac Ranch Bar, 3307 Nationway, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  (License currently held by
Cheyenne OTB, LLC d/b/a Wyoming OTB, 3307 Nationway, Cheyenne, Wyoming.)  Upon inquiry,
Kevin Beers, applicant, reviewed tentative plans for the  business and stated the lice nse location for
operation will not be changing.  There being no further comments or input, Mayor Kaysen advised the
purpose of the hearing had been met and closed the hearing.  The application was referred to Finance
Committee.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public mee ting for the purpose of receiving public comment on the
submission of a Se ction 5307 federal grant to fund tra nsportation services by Cheyenne Transit for
fiscal year 2012.  J oe Dougherty, Director, Cheyenne Transit Program, clarified this is an annua l
submission request for federal grant funding and the requested amount is $744,355.00.  Mr. Dougherty
referenced a memorandum dated July 25, 2011 that he had submitted to members of the governing body
regarding the grant request, noting la st year’s requested amount was $807, 660.00.  There  being no
further comments or input, Mayor Kaysen advised the purpose of the hearing had been met and closed
the hearing.

Investment Group, LLC d/b/a Steamboat’s (address not yet issued) to be located off Gardenia Drive
between Yellowstone Road a nd El Camino Real in Cathedral  Commons.  J ames Yates, managing
partner, Let’er Buck Investment G roup, LLC, d isplayed a n a rchitectural d rawing o f th e p roposed
building to be built and provided information on plans for the Wyoming-themed restaurant, stating the
project is “shovel ready”.  He stated a retail type liquor license was important to be able to provide food
and alcohol catering services, and indicated, if the license is approved, they would be willing to report
to the governing body on a quarterly basis with regard to construction progress.  Bill Murray, partner,
One East Sixteenth, LLC, clarified their plans are to develop the building into a restaurant environment
and it was not their de sire to hold the liquor license, if  approved, as a future investment.  Mr. Ya tes
expressed appr eciation of One East Six teenth’s pr oject and the  develope rs’ c ommitment to the
downtown area.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve one license to Let’er Buck Investment Group, LLC d/b/a
Steamboat’s, with the condition that construction of the establishment must begin within 12 months
from approval of the license or the license will revert to the City, and one license to One East Sixteenth,
LLC d/b/a TBD [to be determined], with the condition that a signed lease agreement with a restaurant
establishment to operate within the building must be sig ned within 12 mont hs from approval of the
license or the license will revert back to the City, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Discussion included retail
liquor licenses as economic development tools; inclusion of reasonable conditions with the motion to
approve, and factors considered by Finance Committee members in making their recommendation on
recipients of the licenses, which included support for a license for the downtown area, a Wyoming-
themed restaurant and operational utilization of the licenses in a timely manner.  Upon request, Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk, re-stated the motion on the floor for clarification.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body present.

§15-1-112 (b).  Estimated proposal value is $7.2 million dollars.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held August 8, 2011 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to gather public comment in regards
to the City of Cheyenne’s intention to make application to the Wyoming Business Council, Business
Ready Community Grant and Loan Program, for funding through the Downtown Development Grant
Program for funding assistance to provide a utility corridor to encourage development of downtown
business space.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 6:48 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 August 2, 2011

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
August 8, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Absent: Mr. Collins. Also present: Carol I ntlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan
White, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designa tion of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Allen, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Pierson
and Ms. Ash, agenda items #10 and 15 (e) were removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by
Mr. Brown, and pursuant to support re ceived by Dr. Rinne and M r. Allen, agenda item #12 was
removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Brown  moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 25, 2011.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to radio
station KYOY FM and vacated Council Chambers. Mr. Allen moved to approve payment of vouchers
as presented, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry , Todd Fe ezer, Assistant Dire ctor, Parks and
Recreation, provided infor mation pertaining  to a vouche r pay able to L a F amilia ($750.00) f or
advertising services for the Superday event.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all membe rs of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following  announcement of the vote, Mr . Valdez
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input on the proposed sale of
real p roperty owned by the City of Cheyenne, described generally as Lot 2, Block 1, Cahill P ark
Addition (located at the southeast corner of Windmill Road and Dell Range Boulevard) to Menard, Inc.
which would generate economic development benefits for the City of Cheyenne pursuant to Wyo. Stat.
§15-1-112 ( b). Estima ted proposal value is $7.2 million doll ars. Ma tt Ashby , Planning Ser vices
Director, reviewed time frames regarding governing body actions, public notices and the negotiation
process involving the proposed sale.  He provided information on valuation of the property; proposed
purchase price and general terms of the proposed sale, which will include a written purchase and sales
agreement and a development agreement; public infrastructure to be constructed of benefit to the City,
and other items discussed during the negotiation process.  He advised discussions have recently taken
place involving subsequent refinements to some elements affiliated with the agreements, including items
involving infra structure and naming  rig hts for a f uture a thletic fie ld, stating Mena rd is no longe r
interested in naming rights.  Mr. Ashby provided an explanation on assessment of the current valuation
of t he property since a  2006 proper ty appra isal was conduc ted, and non- comparable land sa les
circumstances affecting the availability of an updated formal appraisal, which had been requested.  He
also provided information on developer costs, such as community development fees and construction,
and potential community economic impact relative to the opening of a Menard business in the City.
Donald James Perrott Macneil questioned the proposed use for the land as he thought the government
had stipulated the land was to be used for park land, and spoke in opposition to sale of the property.
Dan White, City Attorney, advised the Veterans Administration (VA) did have a restriction on the land
when initially conveyed to the City, but in 2007 the VA provided a release of restrictions on the use and
as a result of this document the City can now make use of the property which is subject to approval by
the VA administration, noting the VA has provided approval for the intended use affiliated with the
proposed sa le to Menar d.  Fr ank Cole, C ole Shopping Cente r, expressed c oncern that the land’s
valuation has bee n estimated too low, and stated, sinc e ther e have  been c hanges in the propose d
valuation, re-advertising with current numbers should be done.  He  cited ex amples of other large
commercial property developers who have assisted with public infrastructure for the City in conjunction
with development projects, and discussed expenses to buyers vs. developers; lack of submittal of a site
plan to date, and leaving the property as a public park area.  Delmar Stone spoke about the past and
future “vision” for the parcel, which should remain public park and open space; commented on the
growing community need for open space, noting the parcel has many recreational facilities and uses
nearby; expressed concern that “vision” is being clouded by dollar signs, and urged reconsideration of
any sale of the property.  Edward Melko spoke in opposition to the proposed sale; questioned whether
other City park lands were set aside in trade for this parcel as affiliated with federal d esignations;
expressed concern with increased traffic and expansion of the are a’s use in a commercial way, and
suggested other sites he felt would be better suited for the Menard business.  Upon inquiry, Todd Feezer,
Assistant Director, Parks and Recreation, advised there were two different subjects with regard to
release of restrictions on the land, one was the restriction on use which was lifted by the VA and the
other dealt with L and and Water  Conservation (LCW) funding grants in which de signation of the
property was tra ded for other City  open spac e properties based on appra ised value.  H e provided
information on the properties involved in the trade designation under LCW provisions and commented
on possible future plans for the traded lands relative to recreational purposes.  Discussion included the
following topics: Past proposed commerc ial development (i.e. hotel, r estaurant, retail) plans f or the
parcel; time frame allowed for accepting requests for proposals relative to other potential intere sted
parties; comments in support as well as opposition to sale of the land; holding off on a sale at this time
due to depressed economic conditions and prope rty values; acquiring additional proposals; positive
location of the Menard business for east Cheyenne residents; whether a business of this type would
generate additional economic benefits to the City, and a parking variance for the proposed development,
requested by Menard, that was recently approved by the Board of Adjustment. Upon inquiry, Mr. Ashby
provided information on the parking  variance for reduced number of spa ces that was re quested by
Menard’s, and a dvised the development would be subject to the adopte d larg e sca le commer cial
development regulations which address such items as building construction elements, landscaping,
screening and articulation of the building.  U pon query, Mr. Ashby confirmed a representative from
Menard was unable to be in attendance at the meeting but will be present at the next Finance Committee
and City Council meeting, and advised naming rights to a possible future athletic field were removed
[from draft agreement provisions] pursuant to a request from Menard.  There being no further comments
or input, Mayor Kaysen advised the purpose of the hearing had been met and closed the hearing.  Mayor
Kaysen advised the agenda item (re: Resolution determining the sale of real property with Menard, Inc.)
affiliated with the public hearing had been referred to Finance Committee for review/discussion, and
the Committee meeting will be held Aug. 15, 2011 at 11:00 a.m., Room 122, Municipal Building, and
was open to the public.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to gather public comment and input in regards to the City
of Cheyenne’s intention to m ake a pplication to the Wy oming B usiness C ouncil, Business Rea dy
Community Grant and Loan Program, for funding through the Downtown Development Grant Program
for funding assistance to provide a utility corridor to encourage development of downtown business
space.  Cindy Schneider, Grants Manager, provided information on the proposed project affiliated with
the grant application, explaining the utility corridor is really a service corridor, approximately 22 ft.
wide, that will include elevators, stairwells, utility easements, conference rooms and an atrium for use
by the downtown and the public.  She noted the cor ridor will assist in encoura ging development of
adjacent properties, and the project will act as a conduit to the vision of a vibrant downtown a rea by
generating added activity and commerce, and sales tax revenue.  Ms. Schneider advised if awarded the
$1,000,000 grant it will fund half of the corridor project, noting additional funding will come from the
recently approved State Loan and Investment Board (SLIB) Consensus Block Grant funding and the
Economic Development Joint Powers Board, which will be contingent upon receiving the grant.  She
reviewed the scheduling time frame for submittal of the grant.  Tom Lyndsey spoke in favor of doing
something with the vacant 2006 down town fire site (site of the propose d project) and in support of
private developers building infrastructure in the a djacent area, hopefully a hotel with under ground
parking and a restaurant; suggested the City give the 22 ft. City-owned corridor area to a p rivate
developer as an incentive, and commented that “filling the hole” is a priority.  Ed Melko stated he was
not particularly in favor of the project, but supports the application being submitted at this time, and
questioned why a fence couldn’t be put in place to block the hole from public vision.  There being no
further comments or input, Mayor Kaysen advised the purpose of the hearing had been met and closed
the hearing.

Intlekofer read the following:  Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held August 22, 2011, at 6:00
p.m. in Ci ty Council Chambers, 2101 O’N eil Ave., Chey enne, Wy oming, to dete rmine whe ther
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land being
situated in portions of Sections 14 and 23, Township 13 North, Range 67 West, 6th P.M., Laramie
County, Wyoming, (generally located between Interstate 25 and Clear Creek Parkway, South of West
College Drive).
Edward Melko questioned the recent extension of the lease agreement with Post 6 baseball with
regard to an extension being approved by the Mayor vs. City Council, and whether the Mayor had the
authority to approve the extension.  City Attorney Dan White advised the lease agreement requires a
minimum of 60 days notice of termination of the lease, and expressed his opinion that under the scope
of the re solution that had pr eviously been a dopted (i.e.: a uthorizing the May or to issue a notice of
termination), that more time could be extended by the Mayor.  He indicated the parties were close to
reaching resolution of matters involved and that it was appropriate under the circumstances to extend
the termination date.  He stated a letter to Post 6 was issued by Mayor Kaysen in June, advising them
the lease would be terminated within 60 days from the date of the letter, and advised in the meantime
the City and Post 6 have continued negotiations and in a good faith effort the City has extended the lease
termination date for another 60 days.  Mayor Kaysen affirmed that Post 6 was negotiating in good faith.
Donald Ja mes Pe rrott Mac neil s poke in s upport of  re solving le ase a greement i ssues.  Com ments
followed regarding the Mayor’s decision on a 60 day extension.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 7:31  p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
August 22, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Absent: Mr. Collins. Also present: Carol I ntlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan
White, City Attorney. The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.) Mr. Brown moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 8, 2011.  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Service s and vacated Council Chamber s. Mr. Allen moved to appr ove payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body  prese nt in Counc il Chambers. F ollowing a nnouncement of the vote, Mr. B rown
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Ka ysen opene d a public hea ring to determine wh ether compliance of  annexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for the property described as a parcel of land being
situated in portions of Sections 14 and 23, T.13N., R.67W., 6th P .M., Laramie County, Wyoming
(generally located between Interstate 25 and Clear Creek Parkway, South of West College Drive).  Jim
Flesher, Urban Planning office, reviewed requirements and compliance of conditions contained in Wyo.
Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i)
through (vi).  Donn Edmunds expressed concerns with City fire and police manpower coverage for the
proposed area to be annexed.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing
having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
Resolution determining that the sale of real property owned by the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming
would benef it the economic de velopment of the  City pursuant to Wy o. Stat. § 15-1-112(b) a nd
authorizing the Mayor to sign a Purchase and Sale Agreement and a Development Agreement with
Menard, Inc. for the sale of  23.87 acres of City -owned land platted as L ot 2, Block 1, Cahill Park
Addition (l ocated at  t he s outheast co rner o f t he i ntersection o f W indmill R oad an d De ll R ange
Boulevard, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Frank Cole, Cole Shopping Center, spoke in opposition to the City
selling the vacant park land, noting uses in the area for recreational purposes and commenting that the
proposed purchasing offer amount was not high enough.  Barbara Stafford, Friends of Botanic Gardens
Foundation Board, referenced a document (on file with City Clerk’s office) submitted earlier in the day
to governing body members, and spoke about the assistance provided by the Foundation Board in
acquiring release of property use restrictions through the U.S. Dept. of Veterans Affairs, and in support
of utilizing funds received from the property sale to upgrade and expand the conservatory at Botanic
Gardens. Scott Nuttelman, Menar d, I nc., re quested support for  the p roposed sale a nd discussed
Menard’s desire to expand its operations to serve the Cheyenne area and the company’s development
plans and construction season timing issues.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Nuttelman provided information on
evaluation of other site location options they had researched within the community, and advised the
Windmill site provides for the ne cessary infrastructure for utilities and more  general access to the
community with room for e xpansion of other  retail businesses.  During discussion, Mr. Nuttelman
explained they did review the Swan Ranch development area but considered it more of an industrial site,
which might be viable for a potential manufacturing and distribution facility, than a commercial retail
site; reviewed the location of Menard stores in the region, and potential opportunity to expand into other
western states in the future.  In response to Mr. Nuttelman’s comments involving evaluation of other
property sites in the area, Mr. Cole advised that he was contacted by Menard’s but at the time of the
inquiry he did not have a ny property available for r etail development in the acr eage size Menar d’s
desired.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Mr.
Valdez moved to pos tpone to Sept. 12, 2011, seconde d by Mr. Pierson.  Mr. Nuttelman sug gested
approving t he sale with conditions to resolve any  concerns the Council may  have, a nd advised a
postponement may  aff ect the c ompany’s time fr ame to beg in construction.  I t was noted the
recommendation to postpone made at Finance Committee was to allow time for review and clarification
of confidential information provided by the City Attorney.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney,
provided information on the  attorney-client confidential memorandum he had issued to governing body
members involving a potential property interest claim in the parcel involving the Laramie County
Commissioners.  H e explained since providing the m emorandum to Council members, the County
Commissioners have asserted a property interest claim and explained this issue needs to be resolved
prior to acting on the resolution.  Discussion included the purpose of postponement, which would allow
time to f urther r eview t he City  At torney’s me morandum a nd to p rovide tim e fo r t he Cou nty
Commissioners to meet to make a final decision on their proposed intere st in the property .  Mayor
Kaysen advised, pursuant to communication with County Commission Chair Diane Humphrey, that
the Commissioners have not made a final decision yet on their claim of interest in the property and that
it is still under review, and stated the Commissioners plan to take this up at their Sept. 6th meeting.
Mayor Kaysen recognized County Commissioner Troy Thompson in attendance at the meeting, who
confirmed the information shared by the Mayor was correct.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Ash voting “no”.

R.67W., 6  P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located between Interstate 25 and Clear Creekth

Collins).

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-09-12 | CLIP_ID: 242 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 242

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
September 12, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: COUNCIL PRESIDENT - Dr. R inne; COUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson (arriving at 6:08 p.m.), Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Mr. Collins and Mayor Kaysen. Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City
Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  In the absence of Mayor Kaysen,
Council President Rinne, Acting  Mayor, chaired the meeting. [City Clerk’s note: Council President
Rinne, as Acting Mayor and meeting chair, exercised his option to vote or not vote, as entitled by state
statute, on agenda items before the governing body for action.]  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Brown moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting  “yes” -- all member s of the g overning body present with the exception of
President Rinne who did not vote.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on August 22, 2011.  M otion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
 Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.
Motion carried.  Voting  “yes” -- all membe rs of the g overning body present with the exception of
President Rinne who did not vote.
President Rinne opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a transfer of location
for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Cloud 9, Inc. d/b/a Cloud 9
Liquorland, 1958 Dell Range Boulevard (Suite C), Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Tom Kingham, owner of
Cloud 9, Inc., explained the current status of the liquor license, which has been “parked” and not in
operation, and his request to relocate the license to open a package liquor store only business  within
a vacated building area in the strip mall in the Sam’s Club south out parcel area.  There being no further
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, President Rinne closed the hearing and
referred the application to the Finance Committee.
An Ordinance amending Title 17, Z oning, Chapter 17.08, Z oning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne Cit y Map of the Chey enne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from MR-1 Medium Density  Residential Establish ed and P Public Districts to P UD
Planned Unit Development District for Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, and Lot 1, Block 2, Hospital Center, 1st
Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between E. 22nd and E. 25th Streets, east
of Warren and we st of Evans Avenues) -- r eferred to Publi c Services Committ ee (sponsor - Mr.
Collins).
An Ordinance amending Title 17, Z oning, Chapter 17.08, Z oning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Cheyenne City  Map of the Che yenne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from A-2 County Agricultural District to HI Heavy Industrial District for a portion of
Section 1, T.13N., R.66W., 6  P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming,  also to be known as Lot 1, Block 1, GDCth
Subdivision (located south of I nterstate 80, west of Campstool Road) -- referred to Public Services
Committee (sponsor - Mr. Collins).
An Ordinance amending Section 5.44.030(C), Contrac tor Licensing Board - Me mbership -
Term, of Chapter 5.44, Contractors, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, relating to the removal of certain City officials as ex-officio members of
the Contractor Licensing Board -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Mr. Pierson). 
An Ordinance amending Section 15.04.020(A), Building Code Board of Appeals, of Chapter
15.04, Gene ral Provisions, of Title 15, Building s and Construction,  of the Code of  the City  of
Cheyenne, Wyoming, relating to the r emoval of certain City officials as ex-officio member s of the
Building Code Board of Appeals -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor - Mr. Pierson). 
Resolution determining that the sale of real property owned by the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming
would benef it the economic development of  the City  pursuant to Wy o. Stat. § 15-1-112(b) a nd
authorizing the Mayor to sign a Purchase and Sale Agreement and a Development Agreement with
Menard, Inc. for the sale of 23.87 acres of City-owned land platted as Lot 2, Block 1, Cahill Park
Addition (l ocated at  t he s outheast co rner o f t he i ntersection o f W indmill R oad an d De ll R ange
Boulevard, Cheyenne, Wyoming) (Postponed from 08/22/2011).  Scott Nutte lman, Menard, I nc.,
requested a two week postponement as they are still working with staff members and the County to
finalize the Development Agreement and Purchase and Sale agreement, and the pending release of
claim of prope rty interest pre viously asserted by the Co unty.  In addition, he noted that g iven the
magnitude of the proposed sale, and as there were three members of the governing body absent at the
meeting, final consideration of the resolution by the entire Council was preferable.  Reporting for
Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Ms.  Broyles moved to
postpone for two weeks (to Sept. 26, 2011), seconded by Mr. Brown. [City Clerk note: Mr. Pierson
joined the meeting.] Comments included reference to a recent City Attorney memo requesting a two
week postponement, and postponement enabling more members of the governing body to be present
to vote on the resolution.  Motion to postpone failed (due to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Ash, Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles and Mr. Pierson.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and President
Rinne.  Discussion on the main motion included the following topics: Valuation of the property relative
to proposed purchase amount and the City’s return on investment; proposed Menard business that would
be located off one of the busiest streets (i.e.: Dell Range Blvd.) in the state; current per square foot value
of properties being developed for industrial vs. commercial use; elements affiliated with economic
development issues, including  local g overnment’s role re lative to business competi tion; Menard’s
project value to the community; retaining land for additional park, athletic and/or open space purposes;
Menard site plan relative to the number of parking spaces and effect on Cahill Park events and sports
activities; property as a long-term City asset; concern with marketing process involving proposed sale
of the property; current economic market conditions affecting property values; community need for a
girl’s softball field; budgetary concerns involving parks and recreation facilities and maintenance issues,
and comparison issues involving economic development incentives for the “downtown hole” (fire site)
vs. the prop osed Menard’s project.  During  discussion, and in response to inquiries, Rick Parish,
Director of Parks and Recreation, reviewed the history time frame of when the property was initially
proposed to be sold; explained removal of the land use restriction by the Veterans Administration and
Land and Water Conservation Section 6(f) boundary designation changes involving the property and
other parks and recreation areas, noting the sale will assist with financial funding for enhancements,
including remodeling and refurbishing, of existing parks and recreational facilities within the City.  He
provided information on property appraisals; reviewed and discussed a list of department projects and
needs tha t could benef it from the money  rec eived from the sale  of the prope rty, and explained
infrastructure that Menard’s project would provide at the site, including a Greenway tunnel, parking lot,
sidewalk and bike path landscaped with trees, and an approach for a possible future rec center in the
vicinity as well as installation of water and sewer access to the area.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Parish
reviewed estimated parking spaces that will be available through the Menard development that could
potentially provide ove rflow public pa rking for ne arby athletic fa cilities.  Motion t o adopt failed.
Voting “yes” -- Ms. Ash and Mr. Pier son.  Voting “ no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. B rown, Ms. Broyles, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  President Rinne did not vote.
Resolution determining that conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist
for the property described as a parcel of land being situated in portions of Sections 14 and 23, T.13N.,
R.67W., 6  P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located between Interstate 25 and Clear Creekth
Parkway, south of West College Drive).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown moved

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-09-26 | CLIP_ID: 244 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 244

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
September 26, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR: Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Brown
and Ms. Ash, agenda item #24(c) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  M r. Collins moved to
approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 12, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Service s, and vac ated Council Chambers.  Mr. V aldez moved to approve  payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Upon inquiry, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, provided
information on the following vouchers payable to:  Hirst Applegate law firm, outside legal fe es
affiliated with a hearing scheduled before the Fire Civil Service Commission; Powers, Depot elevator
repair, and Schindler E levator Corporation, Depot elevator preventive maintenance services.  Upon
inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, advised a voucher payable to VISA involved expenses for
two employees to tour a construction and demolition recycling system in Boston, noting the processing
system is partially identical to what is proposed at the Transfer Station.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Ka ysen opene d a public hea ring to rec eive comments and input for a  transfe r of
ownership for a r etail liquor license a pplication filed with the City  Clerk’s Of fice for Concor d
Neighborhood Corporation d/b/a Applebee’ s Neighborhood Grill and B ar, 1401 D ell Range Blvd.,
Cheyenne, Wyoming (License currently held by Wild West Apple Ventures Ltd., LLC d/b/a Applebee’s
Neighborhood Grill and Bar, 1401 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Mylon Skala, Director of
Operations, Applebee’s Concord Neighborhood Corporation, clarified Concord purchased the business
from the current owner and they will continue operating in the same manner.  There being no further
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and
referred the application to Finance Committee.
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and Section
17.08.040 Cheyenne City Map of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification from
MR-1 M edium D ensity Re sidential E stablished a nd P Pub lic Di stricts t o PU D Pl anned U nit
Development District for Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, and Lot 1, Block 2, Hospital Center, 1st Filing, City
of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between E. 22nd and E. 25th Streets, east of Warren
and west of Evans Avenues).  Jan Nelson-Schroll spoke in opposition to the ordinance.  Comments
included concerns with incr eased traffic, ambulance access, flooding, downtown area congestion,
effects on the neighborhood, helicopter noise, and the need for additional planning and  community
involvement.  S unny Kaste spoke in favor of  the hospital expansion plans, noti ng the development
would be good for the neighborhood and stating she had no concerns with higher traffic or noise from
helicopters.  Reporting  for Pub lic Services Comm ittee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on sec ond
reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.   Mr. Collins explained that a r ecommendation to include Planning
Commission Condition #1 was included with the motion at Finance Committee, however, since that
meeting it was noticed that the Condition wording  was contained within the ordinance (requiring an
amendment).  Mr. Collins moved to amend by incorporating Planning Commission Condition #1 by
striking current language under Section I -6, bullet point #3, on pag e 6 of 10 of  the ordinanc e and
replacing with  lang uage that re ads: "When provided, surf ace storm water detention ar eas shall be
developed as public greens."  Ms. Ash seconded the motion to amend.  Comment followed on are a
drainage/flooding a lleviation a nd di version plans. M otion to a mend c arried.  Vo ting " yes" --  a ll
members of the govering body.  Discussion followed on provisions of the PUD ordinance relative to
the model that had previously been reviewed; structure height restrictions; buildings being articulated
back 10 ft. in distance, and current Capitol Heights Overlay District (zoning) restrictions.  David Land,
Davis Pa rtnership Ar chitects, De nver, CO , a dvised ther e wa s a  lot  of  dis cussion o n w hether t o
incorporate a "step back" in the PUD zoning ordinance and explained it will be addressed in the design
and is not contained within the ordinance.  He stated the cancer center is being designed as a two-story
building that cannot be extended vertically, and the parking garage is being designed so it can expand
2 additional levels, which is under the height requirement, and provided information on the "step back"
issue.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing body.
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and Section
17.08.040 Cheyenne City Map of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification from
A-2 County Agricultural District to HI Heavy Industrial District for a  portion of Section 1, T.13N.,
R.66W., 6  P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming,  also to be known as Lot 1, Block 1, GDC Subdivision (locatedth
south of Interstate 80, west of Campstool Road.  Mayor Kaysen declared a conflict of interest (family
financial interest) for this agenda item as well as agenda item #23(a) (Preliminary Plat); relinquished
duties of the chair to Council President Rinne, and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Collins moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon inquiry, Jon Narva, Cheyenne Fire and Rescue
Dept., stated they are waiting for the result of an ISO assessment study which will determine whether
the area would be considered an underserved area, and noted the new fire training center was built to
accommodate use as a fire station for the area, but it is not staffed at the current time to provide fire
response services.  Comment followed regarding the potential need for additional fire response coverage
and staffing for this area as well as the Swan Ranch area and consideration of same should be addressed
at budg et time.  Upon inquiry , J im Flesche r, Planning  and De velopment, advised the  amount of
community facility fees to be assessed for the area is approximately  $450,000.00, and Rick Parish,
Parks and Recreation Director, advised at the present time there are no plans for use of the community
facility fees.  He noted a request for park land in the JL Ranch Subdivision area has been requested and
further review will be conducted when the fees have been received.  Motion carried.  Voting "yes" --
all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mayor Kaysen returned to Council Chambers with Council President Rinne returning duties of the chair
to Mayor Kaysen. 
Ordinance amending Section 5.44.030(C), Contractor Licensing Board - Membership - Term,
of Chapter 5.44, Contractors, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of
Cheyenne, Wyoming, relating to the remova l of certain City officials as ex-officio membe rs of the
Contractor Licensing Board.  Mr. Collins moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.
Mr. Collins mo ved to  a mend by su bstitute, s econded b y Ms.  As h.  I t w as a dvised th e su bstitute
addresses lang uage cla rification.  Moti on to amend ca rried.  V oting " yes" -- all members of  the
governing body.  Upon inquiry, Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, advised the purpose of the
ordinance is to bring  City Code up-to-da te with curr ent state sta tute provisions.  Main motion as
amended carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing body.
Ordinance amending Section 15.04.020(A), Building Code Board of Appeals, of Chapter 15.04,
General Provisions, of Title 15, Building s and Construction, of the Code of the  City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, relating to the removal of certain City officials as ex-officio members of the Building Code
Board of Appeals.  M ayor Kaysen noted, similar to the previous agenda item, the purpose of the
ordinance is to also bring City Code up-to-date with state statute provisions.  Mr. Collins moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Motion carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the
governing body.
Ordinance annexing to t he City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated in

between Interstate 25 and Clear Creek Parkway, south of West College Drive) -- referred to Public

[CA] Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Frontier North, 3rd

(located at the northwest corner of the intersection of Frontier Mall Drive and Prairie Avenue.  Motion
