[CA] Resolution authoriz ing submission of an a pplication to the United S tates Department of
Treasury, for funding through the State Small Business Credit Initiative to increase access to capital in
Cheyenne, Wyoming and surrounding area.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5317)
Resolution authorizing the City of Cheyenne, through the City Attorney’s Office, to commence
condemnation proceedings to obtain real property located at 1900 Converse Avenue necessary for the
completion of the East Pershing Boulevard Reconstruction Project (Phase 4) (2003-2006, 2007-2010
1% Sales Tax).  Alex Davison, attorney, Patton and Davison law firm, representative for the property
owner, expressed his reservations with regard to the structure of the resolution as drafted, and provided
background information involving the property, which was initially acquired to construct a Hollywood
Video business, and communication time frame between the parties involving the property acquisition
issue.  He explained since closure of the Hollywood Video store it has been difficult for the property
owner to lease/rent the property as it is not a favorable location for any new tenants at this time, noting
the proposed round-about project plans involve part of the prope rty.  He a dvised the City had been
approached with regard to c onsideration of purchasing the entire property but the City’s response
indicated it is o nly interested in a portion of the parking area.  Mr. Davison discussed  p roblems
affiliated with the removal of a portion of the parking area, which would limit the type of use for the
building due to reduced parking spaces, and commented on concerns with ingress/egress to the property;
potential zoning variance that may be required due to MUB zoning requirements; traffic patterns; lack
of sizeable access for delivery trucks, and the difficulty in assessing an appraised value of the real
property portion the City wishes to acquire for the reconstruction project.  He noted a property appraisal
has been requested and the information will be made available when received, and that negotiations are
continuing with his client and City staff.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Davison provided  information in response
to questions raised, including the following topics:  Estimated appraised value of the entire property;
potential zoning issues; square footage of the property vs. the area the City desires for the reconstruction
project; monetary amount of the City ’s offer to purcha se a por tion of the proper ty; potential future
commercial use of the building; study of the property’s parking space configuration and engineering
costs; consideration of the City purchasing the entire property, and estimated time frame in which to
receive property appraisal information.  Comments were made regarding the City’s past acquisition of
properties north of the area in c onjunction with the intersection reconstruction plans, and property
acquisition for the Norris Viaduct project; need for a realistic cost estimate and additional information
relative to mitigation for damages; reduction in parking spaces, and expenses that have been incurred
by the prope rty owner.  During  comments, Mr. Brown raised a point of orde r that discussion was
occurring by governing body members without a motion on the floor.  Mayor Kaysen confirmed that
procedurally the opportunity for discussion and comments by the public was still in order.  John Palmer
spoke in opposition to the use of eminent domain proceedings; suggested any “leg work burden” to
provide information should be the City’s responsibility and not the property owner; commented on  the
proposed round-about project and its effect on the property, and stated his belief the City should buy
the entire property.  M r. Valdez moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Brown. Upon inquiry regarding
existing and future property zoning compliance requirements,  Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director,
advised they have not reviewed the site with respect to c urrent zoning regulations and impacts in
conjunction with the construction of a round-about and any grandfathered/non-conforming uses, and
the issues would be researched with information provided t o the governing body prior to the next
meeting.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, reviewed provisions generally relative to Wyoming
Statutes involving condemnation, “just compensation” and the process of eminent domain, and advised
the City has to make a good faith offer before proceeding with eminent domain.  He briefly reviewed
time frames and stated the City has made a good faith offer in an attempt to acquire the property needed
for the project, and clarified the resolution authorizes moving forward with eminent domain proceedings
if negotiations are not fruitful and that adoption of the resolution does not mean the  City will file a
condemnation action, nor would it mean discussion with the p roperty owner would stop.  He stated
negotiations will continue and that the resolution is needed to meet the schedule to commence project
construction next spring.  Upon inquiry concerning the 6  Whereas clause contained in the resolution,th
Nathan Beauheim, Asst. City Engineer, provided information on past platting of the lots comprising the
property, explaining it has been combined into a single lot, and the parcels the City is attempting to
acquire.  Upon further query, he clarified the two homes purchased by the City several years ago at the
corner of Pershing and Converse were acquired knowing the intersection was going to be reconfigured,
however, it was not certain what the configuration would be at that time.  He stated when design of the
intersection commenced, the consultant was told to take full advantage of the property located in the
intersection along with the land on the northeast corner where the detention pond is located and right-of-
way that the City owns.  He advised even with the acquisition of the land related to the resolution, the
City still needs to acquire two additional pieces of property for the round-about configuration.  Mr.
White c larified if  the  re solution wa s a dopted it  wo uld a uthorize the Mayor to p roceed w ith
condemnation, if necessary, to ensure the property can be acquired to meet the construction schedule,
and confirmed that no further condemnation legislation would come before the governing body.  Upon
inquiry, Mr. Beauheim stated a single lane (vs. a double lane) round-about would not have adequate
capacity to handle the current, nor future, traffic level in the area; provided information on the City’s
monetary offe r to ac quire the la nd, and provide d information on prop erty owner notification
requirements and desired project construction time frame.  Further comments followed on possibility
of the City acquiring the entire property, not just the area required for the reconstruction project and
assessed value of the pr operty.  Mr. Davison a dvised property value information will hopefully  be
received within the next 1 to 1½  months.  Mr. Pierson moved to postpone action on the resolution for
45 days (to Nov. 14, 2011), seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Beauheim explained time frame concerns
if action is postponed, including construction scheduling for the round-about project as well as other
scheduled work in the area.  Mr. Davison spoke in favor of the motion to postpone, which will allow
time for further discussion and to  receive the property appraisal.  Discussion included the propose d
construction t ime fra me; previous neg otiations between the  City , WYDOT and prope rty owner ;
diminished parking spaces that may affect the property owner’s ability to lease the property to future
tenants; possible purchase of the e ntire property by the City and concerns with a pote ntial eminent
domain/condemnation a ction.  D uring d iscussion M r. B eauheim p rovided in formation o n the
construction season schedule; estimated $7 mill ion dollars project cost; status of the de sign for the
project ( approximately 99 % complete), a nd e stimated c ontractor bidd ing tim e frame.  M r. Whit e
reviewed state law relative to eminent domain; discussed elements involved in “just compensation”,
and advised passage of the resolution does not end property acquisition negotiations.  M r. Pierson
withdrew his motion to postpone for 45 days with concurrence by Ms. Broyles as second to the motion.
Upon inquiry, Mr. Ashby advised an analysis of zoning and site requirements could be conducted with
regard to potential property use and any variance request procedures. Discussion continued on the City’s
proposed compensation offer; need for property appraisal information, possible zoning variances related
to future use of the property not affiliated with the area the City wishes to acquire, and support for and
against the proposed r esolution.  In response to query concerning when a property appraisal for the
entire property might be received, Mr. Davison advised an appraisal could probably be acquired in less
than 30 days, but an appra isal for just the portion the City  wants is more diff icult due to unknown
variances that may be needed that would af fect the value.  Ms. B royles moved to postpone for two
weeks (to Oct. 10, 2011), seconded by Ms. Ash.  Comments followed both for and against the motion
to postpone.  Upon inquiry regarding project cost and construction schedule, Mr. Beauheim provided
information on estimated project costs, work involving the Board of Public Utilities, and the West
Pershing construction schedule.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Mr.
Collins and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Brown, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker
and Mr. Valdez.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Beauheim verified there is $7 million dollars in their budget for the
project.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by striking the 6  Whereas clause in the resolution, seconded byth
Mr. Collins.  M r. Beauheim explained state statute requires the detail included in the pr operty legal
description.  Mr. White stated his concern, from a legal standpoint, if the clause were removed.  Dr.
Rinne withdrew his motion to amend with concurrence by Mr. Collins as second to the motion.  Upon
further inquiry, Mr. White reviewed the purpose of an eminent domain action, advising condemnation
proceedings can only involve land necessary for the project, and stated adoption of the resolution does
not preclude continuing negotiations with the property owner.  Main motion to adopt the resolution
failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no”-- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Ms.
Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:45 p.m.

Professional Servic es Ag reement betwee n the City  of Chey enne, Me tropolitan Planning

residential properties off Pershing in conjunction with the future Pershing/19th Str eet/Converse
construction project.

and suggested consideration to have City Council members speak first, then the public.

involving the sale of real property to Menard, Inc. (Re: Motion to adopt failed at the Sept. 12, 2011 City
Council meeting) and their reasons why neither would be making a motion to reconsider the resolution
at the current meeting.  It was noted that staff needs to continue with efforts to resolve the title issue.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-10-10 | CLIP_ID: 246 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 246

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
October 10, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR: Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Va ldez.  Absent: Mr.
Brown and Mr. Pierson.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan
White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Allen, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins
and Ms. Ash, agenda item #23(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Valdez,
and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins and Ms. Ash, agenda item #22(d) was removed from
the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Valdez  moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by
Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 26, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Radio
Station KYOY, and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Allen moved to approve payment of vouchers as
presented, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Upon inquiry, information was provided regarding a vouche r
payable to TDSI - The Design Studio, Inc. (services for design work for the proposed utility corridor
for in-fill development in the downtown ar ea.)  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body present in Council C hambers.  Following  announcement of the vote, Mr. Va ldez
returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input to determine whether
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land being
situated at 5618 Townsend Place and described as the north 170 feet of Tract 6, Briney Tracts, Laramie
County, Wyoming (generally located at the southwe st corner of Townse nd Place a nd east Car lson
Street).  Jim Flesher, Planning Services Dept., reviewed requirements and compliance of conditions
contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information indicating compliance with Wyo.
Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi).  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing
having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and Section
17.08.040 Cheyenne City Map of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification from
MR-1 Me dium D ensity Re sidential E stablished a nd P Pub lic Di stricts t o PU D Pl anned U nit
Development District for Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, and Lot 1, Block 2, Hospital Center, 1st Filing, City
of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located between E. 22nd and E. 25th Streets, east of Warren
and west of Evans Avenues).  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve
on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of
the governing body present. (#3931)
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and Section
17.08.040 Cheyenne City Map of the Cheyenne City Code, by changing the zoning classification from
A-2 County Agricultural District to HI Heavy Industrial District for a  portion of Section 1, T.13N.,
R.66W., 6  P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming,  also to be known as Lot 1, Block 1, GDC Subdivision (locatedth
south of Interstate 80, west of Campstool Road.  Mayor Kaysen declared a conflict of interest (family
financial interest); relinquished duties of the chair to Council President Rinne, and vacated Council
Chambers.  Edith Cook expressed concerns with the location of the new gas power plant and questioned
whether alternative locations had been reviewed.  Upon inquiry, Jason Hartman, Black Hills Corp.,
advised no other sites were proposed at this time.  Ms. Ash moved to approve on third and final reading,
seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry regarding concern with fire response coverage for the area, Mr.
Hartman reported they are just beginning the conceptual design and would be willing to discuss the
issue with the City.  Ms. Cook's statements were referenced during comments encouraging members
of the public to speak on items of concern to them at Council meetings.  Motion carried.  Voting "yes" --
all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mayor Kaysen returned to Council Chambers with Council President Rinne returning duties of the chair
to Mayor Kaysen. (#3932)
Ordinance amending Sections 5.44.010, 5.44.020, 5.44.030, 5.44.040, 5.44.050 of Chapter 5.44,
Contractors, of Title 5, Business L icenses and Regulations, of the Code of the  City of Chey enne,
Wyoming.  Ms. Ash moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion
carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing body present. (#3933)
Ordinance amending Section 15.04.020(A), Building Code Board of Appeals, of Chapter 15.04,
General Provisions, of Title 15, Building s and Construction, of the Code of  the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming, relating to the removal of certain City officials as ex-officio members of the Building Code
Board of Appeals.  Ms. Ash moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.
Motion carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing body present. (#3934)
Ordinance annexing to t he City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated in

explained they monitor the revenue monthly and if projections are less than anticipated they may have

Transfer of ownership for a retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office
for Concord Neighborhood Corporation d/b/a Applebee’s Neighborhood Grill and Bar, 1401 Dell

d/b/a Applebee’s Neighborhood Grill and Bar, 1401 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Mr.
Valdez moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of
the governing body present.
 
Under other business:
James Perrott Macneil commented in opposition to the Cit y’s actions involving annexation,
development and/or activities involving the Belvoir Ranch and other properties he alleged that he owns.
Ms. Broyles stated that at the Septe mber 26  City Council meeting, the motion to adopt theth
resolution involving condemnation proceedings to obtain property located at 1900 Converse Ave. for
the East Pershing Boulevard Reconstruction Project failed and, as she had voted on the prevailing side,
moved to rec onsider the vote on the  res olution, seconded by  Mr. Collins.  Upon request, Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk, read the resolution entitlement as follows:  Resolution authorizing the City of
Cheyenne, through the City Attorney’s Office, to commence condemnation proceedings to obtain real
property located at 1900 Converse Avenue necessary for the completion of the East Pershing Boulevard
Reconstruction Project (Phase 4) (2003-2006, 2007-2010 1% Sales Tax).  Ms. Broyles explained that
subsequent to the September 26   meeting, additional information affiliated with the resolution hadth
been received, and based upon this updated information she was requesting support for the motion to
reconsider to allow for further discussion.  John Patton, Patton and Davison law firm, representing the
owner of the private property involved in the proposed resolution, advised that attorney Alex Davison,
who has been involved in this matter with the City on behalf of the property owner, was unable to be
in attendance and that he was instead present to represent their client.  Mr. Patton, noting they were not
opposed to the round-about project itself, spoke in opposition to the motion to reconsider and expressed
concerns that approval of the resolution would result in a “fixed course” with only one option left; that
being the taking of the sliver of land the City wishes to acquire without addressing the resulting impact
effects i nvolving the  re maining pr operty a rea the  City do es n ot w ish to a cquire.  H is c omments
included concerns regarding the potential limited use of the re maining property due to zoning and
development requirements; access, reduced parking and ground contamination issues, and inability of
the property owner to rent/lease the property at the current time.  He indicated it was their desire to
resolve the matter without the use of the resolution, which may result in litigation.  Upon inquiry
regarding whether the City could move forward with negotiations if the resolution were passed, Dan
White, City Attorney, stated that his basic understanding of eminent domain law is that if the resolution
was passed the City would have the right to file a complaint to take 10,000 sq. ft. of property (for the
reconstruction project) and could move for a right of entry hearing, which could enable the City to take
possession of the property and begin construction before final resolution of the amount that the City
would owe the property owner.  Mr. White added that, in his view, passage of the resolution would not
preclude City  staff f rom continuing  neg otiations with t he attorne ys and prope rty owner , noting
discussions could potentially involve the full range of issues that may need to be discussed to,
hopefully, arrive at some agreement.  Mr. Patton responded that they wish to seek the City Council’s
perspective, noting that City staff’s position would probably not change.  Comment followed on land
contamination and potential ADA issues involving  the prope rty the City  does not requir e for the
project.  Motion to reconsider carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne
and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Ms. Broyles moved to adopt
the resolution, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Patton reiterated, referencing his previous comments, his
opposition to the resolution.  James Perrott Macneil spoke against the eminent domain proceedings and
stated, in his opinion, the City should buy the property from Mr. Cole and tear down the building.
Mayor Kaysen clarified that the pr operty involved in the resolution was not owne d by Mr. Cole.
Comment of concern was expressed regarding reconsideration of the resolution without the public’s
awareness, and allowing Mr. Davison the opportunity to be present prior to taking a vote.  Mr. Spiker
moved to postpone for two weeks (to Oct. 24, 2011), seconded by Mr. Allen.  Comments in support
of postponement followed.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present.  Mayor Kaysen announced the item would be reviewed at the next Finance Committee
meeting on Oct. 17, 2011.
Ms. Ash commented on federal ARRA (American Recovery and Reinvestment Act) funding
and inquired whether, due to the federal Tydings amendment, any previous ARRA funds that may have
been received by WYDOT could  be used f or the Pershing  projects.  Doug  Vetter, City Engineer,
advised he was not aware of any additional WYDOT funding that may be available under the ARRA.
He explained the two projects (East and West Pershing projects) involve STP (Surface Transportation
Program) urban funding and stated his belief that the STP amount to be received is a fixed amount at
this point.  Mayor Kaysen advised staff will follow-up with WYDOT to inquire whether any ARRA
federal funding might be available.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 7:00 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
 
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 October 18, 2011

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-10-24 | CLIP_ID: 248 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 248

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
October 24, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR: Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Pierson
and Ms. Ash, agenda item #19(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Ms. Ash,
and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #18(a) was removed from
the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Collins  moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by
Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Reg ular Meeting of the Gover ning Body on October  10, 2011.  Mo tion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Ameri-
Tech HVAC Services, and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Valde z  moved to approve pa yment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  U pon inquiry, Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation
Director, provided information regarding a voucher payable to AVI Engineering for design of a sewer
system at Prairie View Golf Course.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown returned to
Council Chambers.
Ordinance annexing to the  City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated in
portions of Sections 14 and 23, T.13N., R.67W., 6 P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (generally locatedth
between Interstate 25 and Clear Creek Parkway, south of West College Drive).  Reporting for Public
Services Committee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting "yes" -- all members of the governing body. (#3935)
Ordinance amending certain Chapters and Sections of Title 17, Zoning, of the Cheyenne City
Code by Repealing and Re-enacting Section 17.80.080, Signs, of Chapter 17.80, P Public District.  Mr.
Collins moved to approve  on second  reading, seconded by Ms. Broy les.  Discussion followed on
language in Section 17.80.080 B. 5. b., indicating the maximum number of times (3) a message on the
electronic message center can change within a 24-hour period and whether this would be adequate
relative to a proposed new electronic bill board for the Civic Center.  Mr. Collins moved to amend by
replacing the lang uage in Section 17.80.080 B . 5. b. that states “Me ssage displays shall change a
maximum of 3 times per 24-hour period” to instead read “Message displays shall change a minimum
of every 8 seconds,” seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Comments followed on other limitations contained in
subsection 5., and potenti al requests in the future f or a similar messa ge display timing cha nge for
commercially zon ed area s.  Upon inquiry , Matt Ashby , Planning Ser vices Dire ctor, provide d
information on the timing of digital message changes relative to a concern involving driver distraction,
and confirmed the proposed ordinance only applies to those districts zoned as P - Public. Discussion
included the timing of display message changes; potential future effect of the amendment involving
commercially zoned districts; new e lectronic sign f or the Civic Center, a nd conce rn with driver
distraction if multiple signs are located in the same vicinity.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of  the g overning body with the exception of Mr. Allen a nd Dr. Rinne voting  “no”.
Comments followed regarding a concern with setting a precedent pursuant to the amended language and
potential future similar requests for commercial districts, and required distances (feet) from the right-of-
way (ROW) for signs as listed in Table 1. contained in the proposed ordinance.  Dr. Rinne moved to
amend Table 1. to delete the two rows in the table labeled as 5 or less (feet) and 5-10 (feet) from the
right-of-way, seconded by Mr. Allen.  Discussion followed regarding distance of signs from the ROW
and location of the sign that’s proposed for the Civic Center.  Upon inquiry regarding ROW distance
of the Civic Center sign, Dru Rohla, Civic Center Director, estimated the current Civic Center marquee,
which is where the new sign will be located,  is proba bly approximately 10' or more away from the
ROW, and Mr. Ashby provided information on how ROW distance is typically measured and that they
would measure the ROW distance for the Civic Center sig n prior to final re ading of the ordina nce.
Discussion continued on effect of the previously approved amendment, further review for clarification
of the intent of the proposed amendment, and whether there were any known signs within the P Public
district that may conflict with the proposed amendment involving ROW distance.  Motion to amend
failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “yes”.
Main motion as ame nded ca rried.  V oting “ yes” - - all member s of the g overning body with the
exception of Mr. Allen and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.
[CA] Resolution authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Saddle Ridge, Third
Filing, a replat of Lots 2-5, Block 2, Saddle Ridge Subdivision, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the
northeast corner at the curve of Gunsmoke Road and Countryside Avenue, east of Saddle Ridge Trail).
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5322) 
Resolution determining that conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist
for the property described as a parcel of land being situated at 5618 Townsend Place and described as
the north 170 fe et of Tr act 6, B riney Tracts, Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located a t the
southwest corner of Townsend Place and east Carlson Street.  Mr. Collins moved to adopt, seconded
by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5323)
Resolution authorizing the City of Cheyenne, through the City Attorney’s Office, to commence
condemnation proceedings to obtain real property located at 1900 Converse Avenue necessary for the
completion of the East Pershing Boulevard Reconstruction Project (Phase 4) (2003-2006, 2007-2010
1% Sales Tax).(Postponed from Oct. 10, 2011).  Nathan Beauheim, Assistant City Engineer, advised
a meeting had been held with the attorney representing the property owner discussion included property
appraisals, c ontamination o n th e pr operty a nd DE Q’s in volvement, a nd the  c oncept o f poss ible
consideration of a re-arrangement of the intersection design.  He requested consideration that the
resolution be postponed to Nov. 28, 2011.  Car olyn Dennis ex pressed her concerns with the use of
eminent domain for this type of purpose and suggested the City consider negotiations to purchase the
entire property and waiting to purchase, if necessary, until funding is available and then proceeding with
the intersection project.  Alex Davison, attorney representing the owners of the property, advised they
have had the opportunity to meet with City officials to discuss issues and that some progress has been
made, and spoke in favor  of a postponeme nt.  He commente d on the i ncentive on the  part of the
property ow ner t o n egotiate in g ood f aith to r esolve the  ma tter, re viewed le tters se nt t o th e City
involving neg otiations to t ry to resolve issues a nd stimul ate discussions.  He provided update d
information on the property appraisal they had received and contamination and DEQ involvement,
including the remediation program, and stated they have engaged the services of a round-about expert
to provide possible intersec tion design options.  During  discussion and upon inquiry , Mr. Davison
provided information on the proper ty appraisal relative to g round contamination and remediation
efforts; monitoring of underground wells and possible re-location of same; DEQ’s possible remediation
time frame, and status of current participation in DEQ’s remediation p rogram and affiliated fees.
Comment followed regarding staff follow-up with DEQ with regard to remediation time frames and
consequences if contaminated soils and wells are disturbed during the reconstruction project.  Reporting
for F inance Committee, Mr. Valdez stated ther e wa s no rec ommendation on the item from the
Committee.  M r. Collins moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Up on query, Mr. Beauheim
clarified his previous request was to postpone the item to the Nov. 28, 2011 City Council meeting, and
reviewed construction season effect (possible 2 season instead of 1 season project) if the item were
postponed.  Mayor Kaysen explained if the resolution was postponed to the Nov. 28  meeting, it wouldth
then be referred to the Dec. 5  Finance Committee meeting, returning to City Council for considerationth
at its Dec. 12  meeting.  Mr. Valdez moved to postpone to Nov. 28, 2011, seconded by Ms. Broyles.th
Mr. Davison commented in favor of the motion to postpone.  Discussion followed on the purpose of
the resolution and whether the reason(s) to postpone was application to the intent of the resolution, and
postponement allowing time for fur ther negotiation discussions t o, hopefully, avoid conde mnation
action.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins and Mr. Spiker.

Agencies: American Red Cross; Boys and Girls Club of Cheyenne, Wyoming, Inc.; CASA of Laramie
County; Cheyenne Health and Wellness Center (Dental Access, Unified Human Service Eligibility and
Cheyenne Area Centralized Pharmacy); Climb Wyoming; Comea, Inc.; Connections Corner; Family
Promise; Green Path; Meals on Wheels of Cheyenne, Inc.; Needs, Inc.; Peak Wellness Center, Inc.;
Safe Harbor, A Children’s Just ice Center; Safehouse Services, Inc.; Special Fr iends of Chey enne;
Southeast Wyoming Foster Grandparent Program (Helping Youth and Helping Community); Southern
Wyoming Retired and Senior Volunteer Program; Wyoming 2-1-1, Inc.; Wyoming Family Home
Ownership and Wy oming Senior Citizens, I nc. (2011-2014 1% Sal es Tax).  Motion (per Consent
Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $250,000.00). (#5745-#5767)

[CA] Lease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Downtown Development
Authority for parking spaces in the downtown parking garage.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried
($52,920.00 annual revenue to City). (#5768)
   
[CA] Cooperative Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the State of Wyoming, Department
of Environmental Quality, to purchase a 2010 EPA emissions compliant landfill grader for the City of
Cheyenne landfill (Solid Waste Management fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#5769)
[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Community Based Family Support and Preservation Grant Contract No.
5644 between the City of Cheyenne, Youth Alternatives, and the Wyoming Department of Family
Services, Protective Services Division, for counseling services to families involving youths returning
from out-of-home placement -- referred to Finance Committee.
   
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division and Wyoming Department of Health,
Aging Division to provide supportive services to persons 60 years or older (Title III-B Program Grant).
-- referred to Finance Committee. 
[CA] Amendment No. 1 to Contrac t No. 5520, L ease Agreement between the City of Cheyenne,
acting by and through its Board of Public Utilities (BOPU), and Swan Ranch Development for Swan
Ranch Development to encompass additional property to construct and donate to BOPU a private water
and sanit ary sewe r sy stem that runs throug h the Swan Ranc h Rail Park -- r eferred to Financ e
Committee.
Amendment No. 4 to Contract No. 4563 between the City of Cheyenne and William A. Gross,
Managing Partner, Wyoming Cattle Co., LLC, for grazing lease on the Belvoir Ranch.  Mr. Valdez
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Assistant Public Works
Director, clarified the original lease allows the City to have use of the property for other activities that
do not conflict with the grazing provisions, noting the City decides which areas will be approved for
grazing.  She reviewed information pertaining to the moving of cattle to various pastures at times when
work is being conducted; circumstances which may affect the number of cattle; and that the original
contract specifies that livestock will be yearlings.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” -- all members of the
governing body. (#4563)
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-8-12  for furnishing one new 2011-2012 articulating overcenter aerial
device for the Forestry Division (2011-2014 1% Sales Tax and General fund) (Postponed from Oct.
10, 2011).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Consideration of Bid #R-2-12 for the sale of surplus property described as the south 66 feet of
Lots 9, 10 and 11, Block 666, South Cheyenne Addition, located within Section 5, T.13N.  R.66W.,
6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (a/k/a 515 Morrie Avenue).  Mr. Valdez reported there was no
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Spiker moved to accept the bid from Tora
L. Curry and Scott C. Parson in the amount of $46,234.82, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry
about selling surplus property and whether a reserve bid price can be set, Dan White, City Attorney,
reviewed generally the provisions of Wyoming Statutes when utilizing the bid process, stating City
Council may reject bids if they are not satisfied with the bid amounts, and advised he did not recall any
language with regard to setting a reserve bid amount.  Comments followed with regard to the amount
the City paid to acquire the properties (City Clerk note: Also refer to affiliated following two agenda
items) vs. highest offer received, and future consideration of setting a reserve price.  Motion failed (due
to a tie vote).  Voting “yes” -- Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.
Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Pierson and Dr. Rinne.
Consideration of Bid #R-3-12 for the sale of surplus property described as All of Lots 19 and
20, Block 665, South C heyenne Addition, located within Section  5 , T.13N.  R.66W., 6t h P.M.,
Laramie County, Wy oming ( a/k/a 916 E. 5th Street) .  Mr. Valde z reported ther e wa s no
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Valdez moved to accept the bid from Tora
L. Curry and Scott C. Parson in the amount of $43,398.45.  Motion died due to the lack of a second to
the motion. (City Clerk note: Also refer to affiliated previous and the following agenda items) 
Consideration of Bid #R-4-12 for the sale of surplus property described as All of Lots 21 and
22, Block 665, South C heyenne Addition, located within Section  5, T.13N.  R.66W., 6t h P.M.,
Laramie County , Wy oming ( a/k/a 922 E. 5th Street) .  Mr. Valde z reported ther e was no
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Valdez moved to accept the bid from Tora
L. Curry and Scott C. Parson in the amount of $51,395.25, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry,
Mayor Kaysen advised appraisals were conducted on all 3 properties and each appraised value was
higher than bid amounts received, and Nathan Beauheim, Assistant City Engineer, stated bidding was
through a sealed bid process and selling the properties would relieve the City of l andlord/property
management responsibilities and expenses.  Upon inquiry regarding whether the City could donate the
property for a good cause, Dan White, City  Attorney, discussed state c onstitutional provisions and
concern that the City may not have the legal ability to donate the properties, and commented on the
City’s  fiduciary responsibility to taxpayers.  Mr. Beauheim stated money received from the property
sales may be used for future right-of-way acquisition purposes.  Discussion included history of property
acquisition by the City in conjunction with the Norris Viaduct project; surplus and bid process that was
followed; property ownership costs to the City, other potential property uses, and current condition of
the structures situated upon the 3 prope rties.  I n response to query  on  structural conditions, Mr.
Beauheim provided information on the age of the buildings relative to electrical/heating systems, roof
conditions, etc., and stated sale of the properties, if approved, would be through a quitclaim deed and
that the properties were offered in an “as is” condition. Discussion included appraised property value
amounts and elements considered during the appraisal process; difference in the amount of appraised
property values vs. highest bids received; possible re-bidding process, and whether the properties could
be utilized through City Housing and Community Development programs.  Motion failed (due to a tie
vote).  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting
“no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson and Dr. Rinne. (City Clerk note: Also refer to
affiliated previous two agenda items) 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-12-12 for furnishing 70 Panasonic Toughbook 31 lap top computers
for the Police Department (2007-2010, 2011-2014 1% Sales Tax) -- referred to Finance Committee.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 14, 2011 at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to determine whether compliance
of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist for a parcel of land being situated in
a portion of the N E1/4 of Section 1, T.13.N., R.67W. of the 6  P.M., Laramie County, Wyomingth
(generally located north of and adjacent to West Lincolnway and east of and adjacent to the BNSF
Railroad).
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 14, 2011 at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to receive input and comments
for gathering information on possible projects for  the inclusion on the 1% Specific  Purpose Tax
election ballot, which has been scheduled for August 21, 2012. 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 14, 2011 at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’ Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to re ceive comments on the
proposed sale of real property owned by the City of Cheyenne, described generally as Lot 2, Block 1,
Cahill Park Addition (located at the southeast corner of Windmill Road and Dell Range Boulevard) to
Menard, Inc. which would generate economic development benefits for the City of Cheyenne pursuant
to Wyo. Stat. §15-1-112 (b).  Estimated proposal value is $7.2 million.
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held November 14, 2011 at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for
The Peak Cafe II, LLC d/b/a The Peaks Cafe II at the Airport, 300 E. 8th Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 7:43 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-11-14 | CLIP_ID: 250 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 250

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
November 14, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR: Richard L. Kaysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne and
Mr. Sp iker. Ab sent: Mr . V aldez.  A lso pr esent: Ca rol I ntlekofer, City  Cle rk; B arb Do rr, Ci ty
Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mayor Kaysen noted a correction to consent agenda item #23
(Resolution involving proposed sale of real property near Windmill Road and Dell Range Blvd. to
Menard, Inc.), stating the item would be referred to Finance Committee instead of Public Services
Committee.  M r. Brown  mov ed to  a pprove Con sent A genda ite ms, se conded b y Mr . Co llins.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on October 24, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to
Ameri-Tech H VAC Service s, and vac ated Council Chamber s.  Mr. Spiker moved to approve
payment of vouche rs as pre sented, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Upon i nquiry, Police Chief B rian
Kozak provided information regarding vouchers payable to Intoximeters ($685.95), individual mouth
pieces for portable breathalyzer tests, and DLR Group ($1,800), Police building energy analysis
study.  Upon inquiry, Robin Fee zer, Recr eation Coordinator, I ce a nd Events Center, pr ovided
information relative to a voucher payable to Belfor Property Restoration ($11,696.51), painting of
the front lo bby area of the I ce and Events Center w ith payment from 5  penny funds, and theth
Center’s current revenue vs. expenditure budget status.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  F ollowing announcement of the vote, Mr.
Brown returned to Council Chambers.
Mayor Ka ysen opene d a public hea ring to rec eive c omments and input for g athering
information on possible projects for the inclusion on the 1% Spec ific Purpose Sales Tax election
ballot, which has been scheduled for August 21, 2012.  Mayor Kaysen advised another public
hearing before the governing body will be held at the Nov. 28  City Council meeting, and a publicth
meeting, hosted by City Council, will be held on Dec. 1 .  He provided information on the publicst
input p rocess b eing uti lized to  de termine pr oposed p rojects; referenced  pr oposed p roject(s)
information available on the City’s website, and reviewed guidelines that would be in place for those
wishing to provide comments during the public hearing, which included a 5 minute time limitation
per speaker.  Ms.  Ash requested procedural clarification regarding rules of debate, pursuant to
Roberts Rules of Orde r, and w hether the time fram e for speakers could be limited without an
affirmative vote of the governing body.  Following a 10 minute recess to allow City Attorney Dan
White an opportunity to review Roberts Rules of Order, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at
6:26 p.m.  Mr. Whi te advised the rules (i.e. Roberts Rules of Order) governing debate involve a
motion on the floor before the governing body, and that a public hearing, conducted for the purpose
of obtaining information and comment, does not constitute debate by Council members involving
a motion.  He further advised attendees/speakers are not considered members of the governing body
and reasonable (speaker) time limits could be imposed.   Jack Quirk questioned whether a menu-style
election ballot would be utilized and whether a total ballot projects dollar amount had been set;
commented on proposed City and County projects and past elections, and suggested timely visitation
with the County to determine a total ballot dollar amount.  Mayor Kaysen advised, with regard to a
menu-style ballot, that the structure of the ballot question is not controlled by the governing body.
John Sundahl, President, Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority, spoke in support of the 17th
Street Pedestrian Lighting and Beautification project ($1,960,000.00), and provided information on
elements that would be included in the downtown project area on 17  Street between Pioneer andth
Warren Avenues.  Ma tt Pope, former Chair of Citizens Recreation Center Committee, spoke in
support of a Recreation Center facility as a proposed project ($30,000,000.00), noting the proposed
project would be a “ scaled down” ve rsion from 2008 pl ans, and discussed ame nities that were
proposed for the building with the capa city for future expansion to add component ar eas.  Todd
Feezer, Assistant Director, Parks & Recreation Dept., confirmed an amount of 10% in funding for
operations an d maintenance is included in the proposa l, similar to the 2008 proposa l.  Wendy
Madsen, Friends of B otanic Gardens Foundation, spoke in support of a proje ct expanding the
Botanic Gardens Conservatory for $14,000,000.00 with an additional $2,000,000.00 for operations
and maintenance, advising construction would begin in 2013, provided information on the need to
upgrade the facility.  Darren Rudloff, President, Visit Cheyenne (convention and visitors bureau),
commented on the importance of tourism relative to the community’s tax base and spoke in favor
of the Wayfinding Sign project ($400,000.00-$500,000.00), stating the signage will direct residents
and visitors to places within the City.  Tim Rumpf, representative of the Cheyenne Police Protective
Association, spoke in support of the Public Safety Administration Building project, noting design
and structural deficiencies in the current Police Dept. building, and stated facility options may also
include space for Fire administration offices and/or a new fire station.  Mike Stanfield, Executive
Director, Cheyenne Housing Authority, requested the City consider a new stand-alone senior center
for inclusion as a  project ($7,500,000.00) and pr ovided information on the pr oposed new center
which w ould r eplace t he c urrent o ne-level a rea l ocated d owntown w ithin t he B urke h igh ri se
building that provide s meals and a n activity center for seniors.  Eric h Bochmann, Gymnastics
Coordinator, Parks &  Re creation Dept., spoke in support of a  building proje ct to repla ce the
Neighborhood Facility where the City’s gymnastics program is currently conducted, and provided
information on the disrepair of the current facility and lack of space to accommodate the growth in
gymnastic program participation.  Upon inquiry, Rick Parish, Director, Parks and Recreation Dept.,
provided information on the estimated $800,000.00 project relative to discussions with the school
district, which may be interested in acquiring the current facility, and potential new locations for
housing the gymnastics program, which might include adding on to a new recreation facility at a later
date.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Pope advised the dollar amount arrived at for the proposed new rec center
was to provide for a core facility that would be scaleable to add other future components.  During
the public hea ring, Ann Marg aret Bur ns also spoke in support of the ne ed for  repla cing the
Neighborhood Facility building, due to gymnastics program growth and competitive activities and
deficiencies in the c urrent building, as we ll as support for  a new  rec reation center  to meet
youth/senior needs w ith a g ymnastics area included.  Jack Palma spoke  in support of the lowe r
Capitol Basin Flood Control project to alleviate significant downtown area drainage and flooding
problems and noted use of grant funds toward the project.  Upon inquiry, Doug Vetter, Ci ty
Engineer, stated the updated estimated project cost is $4,800,000.00 to construct 3 of 4 phases; that
past ballot elec tion attempts t o form a storm wate r utility have be en defeated, and that moving
forward with the project would provide multiple benef its in addressing water control issues.  Dr.
Stephanie Erlich also spoke in support of the Capitol Basin Floor Control project, stating the primary
importance of  the  pr oject is  fo r p ublic sa fety wi th a dditional benefits o f p roviding his toric
preservation and revitalization of the west side of the City and downtown area.  During the public
hearing, Adrian Perry also spoke in support of the downtown area drainage and flood control project,
noting the project could help with revitalization opportunities and would leverage grant funding. 
John Volk spoke in support of the Christiansen Road Overpass project ($10,000,000.00) to improve
that portion of the transportation network due to growth in eastern Cheyenne, noting that 6  pennyth
funding is the only funding source available and that, potentially, industrial development funds, as
requested to the state through Laramie County, might also be available.  Upon inquiry regarding the
status of project plans, Mr. Vetter advised a conceptual planning study analysis has been done but
there are no construction plans in place, and stated design for the project is on the 5  penny projectth
list but is not funded.  Upon inquiry, Vicki Nemecek, Assistant Director, Public Works Dept., stated
approximately $1.5 million was allotted on the last 5  penny list (construction design plans) for theth
project but it is not a funded item due to reduced sales tax collection levels.  Wendy Volk, real estate
agent, also spoke in support of the Christiansen Overpass project and provided information on the
number of employees and truckers commuting in the eastern part of Cheyenne and problems with
GPS (global positioning system) devices diverting travelers/truckers off the highway onto private
property, noting this has resulted in a quality of life issue involving one particular property owner
in the area.   Throughout speakers’ presentations and comments, members of the governing body
posed questions to speakers and City staff on various projects presented, and Mayor Kaysen advised
any additional inform ation on projects being requested for consideration could be submitted to
members of the governing body.  He noted a website, a cooperative effort between the City, Laramie
County and the Towns of Albin, Burns and Pine Bluffs, is being developed for placement on the
Laramie County government’s website to provide information on projects and to receive additional
comments.  Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing and reminded those in attendance that there will
be another public hearing to receive input and comments on proposed projects on Nov. 28, 2011 at
6:00 p.m. as well as a public meeting, hosted by City Council members, on Dec. 1, 2011, both to be
held in City Council Chambers.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input to determine whether
compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist  for a parcel of land
being situated in a portion of t he NE1/4 of Section 1, T.13.N., R.67W. of the 6  P.M., Laramieth
County, Wyoming (generally located no rth of and adjacent to West L incolnway and east of and
adjacent to the BNSF Railroad).  Brandon Cammarata, Planning and Development office, reviewed
requirements and compliance of conditions contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific
information indicating compliance with Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi).  Upon inquiry, Mr.
Cammaratta advised this is a voluntary annexation (petition) request by the property owner.  There
being no further comments or input and purpose  of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen
closed the public hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments on the prop osed sale of real
property owned by the City of Cheyenne, described generally as Lot 2, Block 1, Cahill Park Addition
(located at the southeast corner of Windmill Road and Dell Range Boulevard) to Menard, Inc. which
would generate economic development benefits for the City of Cheyenne pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §15-
1-112 (b).  Estimated proposal value is $7.2 million.  Frank Cole spoke in opposition to selling park
land for c ommercial business purposes, and c ommented on why  he did not  view the proposed
commercial use as providing economic development benefits and whether proposed drawings for
the  building had been submitted.  Dr. Stephanie Ehrlich spoke in support of the property sale to
Menard.  Scott  Nuttelman, Mena rd re presentative, spoke in support of the pr oposed sale,
commenting on the value the Menard store can bring to the community and noting that Menard has
not had any serious consider ation of any other site within the community .  He pr ovided a br ief
history of how Menard learned about the possible availability of the property site and subsequent
conversations with City staff, and discussed the City’s RFP (request for proposals) that was issued;
the proposed purchase price and infrastructure elements Menard’s will b e providing to the a rea;
anticipated staffing levels; the possibilit y of a future distribution center facility in Cheyenne that
would serve the region, and benefits of the project, including additional commercial businesses that
could be located on “out lots” on the property.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Nuttelman advised design plans
have been submitted to City staff and Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, provided information
relative to agenda item #17 ( Resolution affirming that use of pr oceeds from any sale of the  land
would be used for Parks and Recreation purposes) which should resolve past title concerns involving
assertion of a property interest by Laramie County.  M r. Ashby stated information pertaining to
supporting documentation, including the proposed purchase and sale agreement and development
agreement, is included with the ag enda packet for the re solution (agenda item #23)  that will be
reviewed by Finance Committee, and confirmed building and site design standards are being worked
on and would be subject to the “big box” standards for large commercial developments.  Pursuant
to inquiry by City Council members, Mr. Ashby; Rick Parish, Director, Parks and Recreation Dept.;
Mayor Kaysen and Police Chief Brian Kozak provided information on economic development value
of the property; the original intent in disposing of the property with sales proceeds to benefit other
park lands in the City, and assessment of the site as an option for a new public safety administration
building, in conjunction with that project potentially being included in the next 6  penny ballotth
question, respectively.  Butch Heimsoth questioned the time frame the property was open to bid by
interested parties and the estimated proposed value and improvements that would benefit the City
as the result of the sale, and expressed concern with the site being possibly considered for a joint
safety administration building.  Spencer Straub commented on the current volume of traffic in the
area and concern with additional traffic congestion from commercial development, and greenway
path usage in the vicinity.  Upon inquiry regarding the process for disposing of City owned property,
Dan White, City  Attorney, advised W yo. Statute §15-1-112(b) permits the sale of  a par ticular
property to one purchaser for the purposes of promoting economic development, and reviewed past
use re strictions on the proper ty and ef forts to have  the re strictions eased; platting  and zoning
changes; C ouncil’s a pproval to  neg otiate wi th M enards, a nd fa ctors e ncompassing e conomic
development.  He indicated if the property were not sold, it could be used for any City purpose, and
explained rea sons why  the proposed sa le to Menar d was plac ed bac k on the Coun cil ag enda
subsequent to pre vious action, including  an expression of intere st from Menard in pursuing
acquisition of the property as there had been no vote on the actual merits of their proposal to
purchase.  Mayor Kaysen advised that since the previous action, clarity on the title issue involving
Laramie County had also been received.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Ashby provided information on the RFP
time frame in which proposals of interest could be submitted, and clarified there is no requirement
by state statute to conduct an RFP process.  There being no further comments or input and purpose
of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a restaurant liquor
license application filed with the City Clerk's Office for The Peak Cafe II, LLC d/b/a The Peaks Cafe
II at the Airport, 300 E. 8th Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Rosalie Iverson, co-partner/owner of The
Peaks Cafe, advised other partners were also in attendance at the meeting and were available to
answer any questions.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been
met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing and referred the item to Finance Committee.
Ordinance amending certain Chapters and Sections of Title 17, Zoning, of the Cheyenne City
Code by Repealing and Re-enacting Section 17.80.080, Signs, of Chapter 17.80, P Public District.
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve on third and final reading,
seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Ms. Ash moved to amend Table 1 under the column for “Distance from
right-of-way (ROW)” by replacing 5 o r less (feet) and 5 to 10 (feet) with NA, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.   It was clarified that NA meant Not Available.  Discussion included the following topics:
Other public signs that may be affected by the amendment; potential request for  similar changes
involving commercially zoned areas, and site distance factors, including restricted setback distances
in the downtown area and placement of signs too far back to be effective for visibility.  D uring
discussion M att A shby, Pl anning Se rvices D irector, p rovided in formation o n zo ning dis tricts,
commenting that each individual zone district has its own tailored sign regulations and that typically
the ROW is from the back of a sidewalk but varies throughout the community, and discussed current
requirements vs. the proposed amendment within the P Public district.  Motion to amend failed.
Voting “yes” -- Dr. Rinne.  V oting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. B rown, Ms. B royles, Mr.
Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mayor Kaysen. Comment followed on message display time
limits as amended at the previous meeting.  Mr. Allen moved to amend Section 17.80.080 B. 5. b.
to instead read “ Message displays shall change a maximum of once a n hour”, seconded by Mr.
Pierson.  Mr. Ashby clarified the previous amendment approved at the last City Council meeting was
that message displays shall remain fixed for a minimum of every 8 seconds.  Dru Rohla, Executive
Director, Cheyenne Civic Center, advised the proposed amendment wouldn’t provide an effective
advertising time frame for shows as he could be advertising up to 12 shows at a time, and explained
the current 30 year old sign marquee has become a safety issue for staff and is labor intensive to
manually change the message board.  Frank Cole commented that the proposed amendment may
affect oth er signs in a “ P” zoned are a, and disc ussed message display time frame s and driver
distraction, and potential effect of an ordinance change relative to signs in the private sector.  Motion
to amend failed.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen.  Voting “no” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr.
Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mayor Kaysen.  Main motion carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body present. (#3936)
Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated at

Ordinance amending Title 17, Z oning, Chapter 17.08, Z oning Districts Established and
