MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-11-28 | CLIP_ID: 252 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 252

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
November 28, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR: Richard L. Kaysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Allen and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #17 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request
by Mr. Allen, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins and Ms. Ash, agenda item #25(e) was
removed from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr . Collins moved to app rove remaining Consent Ag enda
items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”-- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on November 14, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
Mayor Ka ysen opene d a public hea ring to  receive comments and input for g athering
information on possible projects for the inclusion on the 1% Spec ific Purpose Sales Tax election
ballot, which has been scheduled for August 21, 2012.  He reviewed guidelines (identical to those
utilized at the November 14  public hearing) that would be in place  for those wishing to provideth
comments during the public hearing, which included a 5 minute time lim itation per speaker, and
announced tha t Cit y Council would be hosting a dditional public meetings to re ceive input on
proposed projects on December 1, 18 and 20 beginning at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Ms. Ash
moved to appeal to remove the imposition of the guidelines, as previously reviewed, for the conduct
of the public hearing, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Discussion followed on the purpose of the guidelines,
time limit for speakers, importance of projects to the community , and more public hearings and
meetings being scheduled on proposed 6  penny projects than in the past.  Motion failed due to lackth
of an affirmative majority vote.  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins and
Mr. Spiker.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Va ldez and Mayor Kaysen.
Carol Matteson Pascal spoke in support of the Greenway Expansion project, noting past efforts and
achievements involving g reenway paths; st affing; advisory  council; Fr iends of the Gr eenway
Foundation; g rant f unding; c ommunity pa th u sage, a nd the  ne ed to continue wi th p ath
connections/expansions and to provide funding for capital improvements and maintenance.  Matt
Pope spoke in support of a Recreation Center, stating his belief that there is a lot of community
support for the project and noting that the City has expended a financial investment for planning of
the project in the past.  He advised he would be providing more detailed project information at the
City Cou ncil’s D ecember 1  pu blic me eting.  B ob Clary c ommented in  fa vor o f t he L andfillst
Expansion project at the current Happy Jack site and provided information on the review, evaluation
and recommendations made by a past L andfill Advisory Counc il, of which he was a  member,
involving options for an e xtended or newly developed landfill site; DEQ coordina tion; financial
considerations affiliated with hauling of solid waste to Colorado, and ke eping the Belvoir Ranch
property in mind as a backup option.  He noted the project involves a new landfill disposal cell
which would extend the cur rent life of  the landfill by  30 y ears.  Rich Cumm ings, Chey enne
Gymnastics Parent Booster Club, spoke in support of an expansion to the City’s gymnastics program
through two options, either adding the facility to the City-owned Ice and Events Center or including
an area for the program in a new Recreation Center.  He provided information on the Club’s efforts
to alleviate costs of the competitive program, program growth and need for additional physical space,
and expressed concern with the future of the program relative to lack of facility space and possible
future no n-availability of  the  Neighborhood Ce nter.  Ki m Ma es, Director of  Se nior P rograms,
Cheyenne Housing Authority, spoke in support of a new Senior Center and provided information on
meal services, program activities and services provided to se niors in the community.  She stated
more space is nee ded due to the g rowing number of sen ior participants and prog rams offered;
commented on the a ge of the  curr ent ce nter and maintenance re pairs and a ffiliated c osts, and
indicated preference for the facility at or ne ar the current location if possible.  B arbara Stafford,
member, Chey enne B otanic Ga rdens F oundation, spoke in favor  of the B otanic Ga rdens
Conservatory project and provided information on the proposed project. She advised she would be
providing more detailed project information at the City Council’s December 1  public meeting.st
Christie DePoorter, Executive Director, Downtown Development Authority, spoke in support of the
downtown 17  Street Pedestrian Lighting and Beautification project, noting downtown beautificationth
and enhancement benefits will include lighting and streetscape elements, and stating that the project
will assist with promotion of further usage of the area and support private financial redevelopment
investments currently taking place in the downtown.  Renee Lone Elk commented in support of a
new Senior Citizen Center and the volume of senior citizens in the community who will benefit from
senior programs and expanded facilities. Adrian Perry spoke in support of the Capitol Basin Flood
Control project, citing the need for drainage improvements and noting the project will also provide
an opportunity for public green space amenities.  Jim Lambert also spoke in support of the Capitol
Basin F lood Co ntrol p roject to  a lleviate do wntown d rainage c oncerns a nd pr ovide be nefits t o
downtown.  Joey Marchand, member, Mayor’s Youth Advisory Council, spoke in support of the
Botanic Gardens Conservatory project, noting educational benefits to youth, needed infrastructure
improvements and benefits of the current facility and its programs.  Tanvir Dhami, member, Mayor’s
Youth Advisory Council, commented in support of improvements at Johnson Pool benefitting that
area of the community as well as providing youth activities.  During  the public hearing, Edward
Melko commented on the Landfill Expansion project vs. the Belvoir Ranch as a future landfill site
and funding utilized to acquire the ranch, and suggested consideration of a regional landfill to serve
other communities in the ar ea in addition to t he City. In separate comments, he stated propose d
projects should be compiled from the aspect of a “needs list not a want list” and offered comments
in support of the Capitol Basin Flood Control project and Public Safety Administration (Police and
Fire) B uilding p roject.  D uring the  pu blic he aring, Jam es Pe rrott Ma cNeil c ommented o n h is
concerns involving ownership of the Belvoir Ranch area and in opposition of ranch property being
used for landfill purposes.  In separate comments he expressed his concerns with the current national
and international financial and economic conditions and future uncertainties that may affect ballot
question voter results.  Following input and comments from those in attendance, Dr. Rinne, City
Council President, advised of pla nned presentations on proposed pr ojects to be he ard a t the
December 1  public meeting hosted by City Council.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen reviewed thest
process being utilized by the City as well as the governments of Laramie County and the Towns of
Albin, Burns and Pine Bluffs that would result in a recommended 1% Specific Purpose projects list
from each respective entity with a total proposed ballot dollar amount for placement on the election
ballot.  Purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated at
5618 Townsend Place and described as the North 170 feet of Tract 6, Briney Tracts, Laramie County,
Wyoming (generally located at the southwest corner of Townsend Place and east Carlson Street).
Reporting fo r P ublic S ervices C ommittee, M r. Collins m oved t o ap prove o n s econd re ading,
seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon inquiry  Stacey Dierisch, r epresenting her mothe r who owns the
property being annexed, clarified the Board of Public Utilities provided them an early permit to
install water and sewer lines, but advised they will not be connected until approval of the annexation.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  
Ordinance amending an existing PUD Or dinance, “Appendix #1” of the  Cheyenne and
Laramie County Zoning Ordinance 1988, for  Block 5, Mountview Park, and tha t portion of the
vacated right-of-way of East 19th Street as vacated under Ordinance No. 2001, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located at the southeast corner of the intersection of East Pershing Boulevard and Converse Avenue)
(“Cole Shopping Center”).  Ga ry Harrison, Concepts West Architec ture, representative for the
applicant, Safeway, Inc., provided information regarding the proposed zone change conditions for
the property, explaining Safeway is interested in purchasing the property for future development,
including c onstruction of new  buildings, and tha t curre nt PUD conditions restrict wher e new
buildings ma y be  pla ced.  He  g enerally re viewed Sa feway’s sit e development p lan e lements,
including bu ilding de sign to  c onform wi th t he City ’s “ big bo x” c riteria, b uffering, s etbacks,
landscaping, parking and lighting, and indicated the seller of the property has requested the proposed
ordinance be conditioned for effectiveness upon the sale of the property being closed.  Mr. Harrison
discussed Safeway’s plans for the site, advising  property redevelopment plans involve an estimated
$20 million dollar investment, and noted the importance of a timely decision on the City’s proposed
roundabout intersection configuration adjacent to the property as it affects the site plan that will be
submitted and the construction schedule.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Harrison confirmed no height limit
requirements were included in the amending ordinance and that it had been discusse d with staff,
noting a two-story (new Safeway) building was planned, and that the topic of allowing electronic
signs may be discussed further with staff.  Discussion followed on whether height requirements for
buildings and addressing the topic of allowing electronic signs should be included in the ordinance,
and p ossible r oundabout c onfiguration c hanges a ffecting th e s ite.  T erry K unz, Sa feway
representative, clarified Safeway would be open to working with the City regarding a proposed
roundabout design as it will affect some of the property area and project construction timing.  Danny
James suggested an alternative to the currently proposed roundabout design through utilization of
a counter clockwise route, controlled by traffic lights, with Mayor Kaysen requesting he share the
information, including a map depiction as prepared and referenced by Mr. James, with the City
Engineer.  Mr. Collins moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Comments
followed re garding a dec ision on the roundabout conf iguration to assist in moving Saf eway’s
redevelopment project forward in a timely manner.  Mr. Collins moved to amend wording in Section
#5. contained in the ordinance to replace the current wording with language that indicates the zone
change will be applicable contingent upon the sale of the property, and adding Section #6. which
would contain prior Section #5. wording that indicates that the ordinance shall be in full force and
effect upon its publication, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised,
pursuant to request by the property owner and Safeway representatives to have the zone change be
contingent upon sale of the property, that he had legally reviewed the issue and discussed with
planning and development staff and that it is permissible to state the sale of property as a condition
to the effectiveness of the ordinance.  He stated he planned to submit proposed amending language
for consideration by the parties and submit at the next Public Services Committee meeting.  Mr.
Collins withdrew his motion to amend with concur rence by Ms. Ash as sec ond to the motion.
Motion to approve on second reading carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
 
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne City  Map of the Che yenne City  Code, by  changin g the zoning
classification from MR-1 Medium Density Residential – Established District to MUB Mixed Use
with Business Emphasis District for Lots 5, 6 and the west 44-feet of Lot 7, Block 110, Original
City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northeast corner of the intersection of Warren Avenue and
E. 25  Street).  Mr. Collins moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Motionth
carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
Ordinance amending Title 17, Z oning, Chapter 17.08, Z oning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne City  Map of the Che yenne City  Code, by  chang ing the  z oning
classification from L R-2 Low Density Residential – Deve loping District to NB N eighborhood
Business District for Lot 6, Block Thr ee, and Lot 6, Block F our, Sundance Ridge II, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located north of Storey Boulevard, on either side of Sycamore Road).  Dr. Rinne declared
a conf lict of interest (ne arby proper ty owner  interest) a nd vaca ted Council Cham bers.  Rick
Hunnicutt, President, Western Development Corporation, and representative of DLH Trust, property
owners, stated the owne rs had determined the best use of  the property would be for  commercial
purposes, such as professional services offices, in addition to townhouses.  Concerns were expressed
regarding the elevation of a dirt pile due to soil grading in the area.  Mr. Hunnicutt advised storm
water drainage plans had been submitted, and commented that landscaping and a drainage retaining
wall will be implemented in the dirt pile area with the goal of having the area look more like the west
side of the pr oject.  Mr. Collins moved to approve on se cond reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.
Comment followed on possible restriction of the type of commercial business use to be allowed
either through covenants or zoning.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Dr. Rinne returned to
Council Chambers.
Resolution determining that conditions for annexation required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exist
for a parcel of land being situated in a portion of the NE1/4 of Section 1, T.13.N., R.67W. of the 6th
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located north of and adjacent to West Lincolnway and
east of and adjacent to the BNSF Railroad).  Mr. Collins m oved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Ash.
Concern was expressed relative to t he ability to s ustain more hotels in the area and future uses.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.  (#5329)
[CA] Resolution authoriz ing the  City  staff to issue a  Request for Proposals for desig n and
permitting of an expansion to the Happy  Jack Landfill.  Motion (per Consent Ag enda) carried.
(#5330)
Resolution a uthorizing th e M ayor a nd th e Ci ty Cl erk to  s ign a  F inal P lat f or G DC
Subdivision, being portions of Section 1, T.13N., R.66W., Section 6, T.13N., R.65W., Section 31,
T.14N., R.65W., Section 36, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., and a replat of a portion of the right-of-way
of HR Ranch Ro ad, JL Ranch, Chey enne, Wy oming ( located south of I nterstate 80, w est of

[CA] Resolution authorizing the May or and the  City Clerk to sign a  Final Plat for A nderson
Corner, Third Filing, a replat of a portion of Lot 3, Block 1, and 20’ Walkway & Drainage R/W
(right-of-way), Anderson Corner, Second Filing, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located
south of Mason Way, between Grandview and Converse Avenues).  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried.  (#5332)
Resolution acknowledging receipt of and approving the Interchange Enhancement Design
Guidebook prepared by Tom Cobb, P.E. and Nolte Associates, Inc. for the Cheyenne Metropolitan
Planning Organization.  Mr. Collins moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Collins moved
to postpone for two weeks (to Dec. 12, 2011), seconded by Mr. Allen.  John Palmer questioned the
reason for postponement and it was advised postponement would provide additional time to review
the document.  Discussion followed on review of the guidelines and that the guidelines will provide
consistency within the community for design concepts.  C omments of concern were made with
entitlement wording of the resolution with regard to “approving” the guidebook, and whether the
word “supporting” would be more applicable for a resolution vs. ordinance document.  Motion to
postpone failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body.  Mr. Collins moved to amend
the resolution entitlement to substitute the term “approving” with “supporting” and also making the
same change in the Now, Therefore, Be It Resolved paragraph, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Comments
followed on the proposed amendment; bridge designs following the same criteria, and concern with
supporting a past I-180 bridge design, as depicted on the cover of the guidebook, and the governing
body’s reflection of support for the guidebook to WYDOT.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash voting “no”.  Main motion as
amended carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash
voting “no”. (#5333)
Following a ten minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:10 p.m.
Resolution determin ing that the sale  of re al prope rty owned by  the City  of Chey enne,
Wyoming would benefit the economic development of the City pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-112(b)
and authorizing the Mayor to sign a purchase and sale agreement and a development agreement with
Menard, Inc. for the sale of 23.87 acres of City-owned land platted as Lot 2, Block 1, Cahill Park
Addition (located at the southeast corner of the intersection of Windmill Road and Dell Range
Boulevard, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, noted the resolution
includes a purchase and sale agreement to sell the property to Menard, which includes provisions of
the purchase and sale specifications, and a development agreement, which describes the conditions
under which the property would be developed, including public infrastructure.  He briefly reviewed
the proposed sa les amount (ca sh value to the City ); public infra structure areas and value ;
construction and development value; estimated number of jobs the development project will bring
to the community; and possibility of a Menard distribution center in the future.  Mr. Ashby advised,
pursuant to previous discussions, that a recent site selection report indicates a downtown location
is the recommended preferred option for a proposed new police headquarters.  D an White, City
Attorney, stated the County  Commi ssioners have  executed an unc onditional quitclaim deed
conveying to the City all of the County’s right, title and interest in Section 28 so the City now has
unconditional title to the entirety  of Section 28.  He advised the title insuranc e compa ny has
reviewed the deed and has cleared that particular matter from the title insurance commitment so the
City has the ability to convey a clear and marketable title to Menard should the sale move forward.
John Palmer questioned placement of the resolution on the agenda as a consent agenda item.  Mayor
Kaysen explained the proc ess utiliz ed for  placing  ag enda items on the Consent Ag enda ( i.e.
unanimous approval of recommendation from Finance Committee members) and how items ar e
removed.  Scott Nuttleman, Menard representative, referenced previous information he discussed
at the November 14  City Council meeting and briefly reiterated Menard’s interest in doing businessth
in Cheyenne through purchase of the property; community and infrastructure benefits affiliated with
commercial development of the site; proposed construction schedule; potential outparcel uses, and
potential for a future Menard industrial distribution center.  Jeanne Tucker questioned the wisdom
of bringing another big box store of this ty pe to Cheyenne which could adversely affect current
businesses in the community.   James Perrott MacNeil expressed his concerns with the proposed sale
to Menard a nd the cur rent e conomic situation; questioned t he VA (Vetera ns Administration)
manager’s authority to waive property use restrictions, and commented on the County’s relinquishing
its interest in the property.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White reviewed information pertaining to use of the
property as affiliated with the VA’s involvement and use requirements, and clarified the VA had
submitted a le tter cle aring the proposed sale to Mena rd for  the uses identified.  Tim Dr yden,
Cheyenne Girls Softball Association a/k/a Extreme Fast Pitch, a traveling competitive program,
spoke in support of the sa le, expressing interest that the funds fr om the sale be  used to build a
championship quality field at the Converse Softball Complex, and discussed benefits to the league
and the community that would be derived from having a championship field, including the ability
to host tournaments.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to adopt, seconded by
Mr. Collins.  Comments followed on typographical errors that should be corrected in printed dollar
amounts that had bee n noticed in the Purcha se and Sale Agreement as well as the Deve lopment
Agreement; use of the property for public vs. commercial use; envisioned truck traffic route to access
the site; response to the RFP (Request for Proposal) that was issued and RFP process concerns;
informal property appra isal information; due dilige nce; sa les proce eds bene fitting pa rks and
recreation facility improvements and projects; whether proceeds could be considered for use toward
the downtown fire  site as a poc ket par k; property  site bein g an area involving low potential
residential growth, and r emaining adjacent open spa ce still available  to the City  if the sale w as
approved.  During comments, Mr. Ashby  and Mr. Nuttleman pr ovided information on propose d
truck traffic routes, and Mr. Ashby advised he was not aware of any other business expressing an
interest in the property since the RFP was advertised last spring.  Mayor Kaysen stated that proposed
use of proceeds toward a pocke t park a t the downtown fire  site would need to be  looked into.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen,
Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez voting “no”. (#5334)

Contract A ddendum t o Co ntract #5 614 b etween th e City  of  Che yenne a nd WI Nhealth
Partners for City employee health coverage.  John Palmer questioned a change to  d rop re tiree
coverage over the age of 65.  Judi Just, Accounting Office, explained the reasons for the change,
citing the cost of high premiums that have resulted in no employees electing coverage in recent years.
Mr. Valdez moved to appr ove, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr. Va ldez moved to amend by  re-
inserting the following language in #8: “Elected and appointed officials who have served two or
more consecutive terms, defined as 8 consecutive years, and are age 50 or more when they leave
office,” seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Upon request, Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, restated wording of
the proposed amendment.  Discussion included the recommendation from Finance Committee to re-
insert the proposed amendment language that has been in past contracts for years, and how the age
of 50 was deter mined as the base age.  M s. Just explained the age of 50 is consistent with the
Wyoming Retirement System guidelines, which require someone to be at least age 50 before they
can retire.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the
exception of Ms. Ash voting “no.” . Upon inquiry, Steven Goldstone, President/CEO of WinHealth
Partners, advised if the age of 50 was dropped there may be, actuarially, a theoretical risk having an
adverse effect on costs to the City, and that on a short term basis it would probably be minimal.  Mr.
Pierson moved to amend #8 by striking the following language at the end of the sentence:  “ ... and
are age 50 or more when they leave office.”, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Barb Dorr, City Treasurer,
reviewed early retirement employee options offered in the past which also indicated the  retirement
age of 50; provided information pertaining to the firefighters contract, which states they have to be
at least age 50 before they can receive retirement, and clarified retirees pay 100% of the premium
but the fact they are in the pool reflects the overall rate the City pays.  Further discussion followed
on the proposed amendment and the City’s retirement policy.  During discussion, Ms. Just provided
information on related elements involving the state retirement program.  M s. Broyles moved to
postpone tw o w eeks (t o D ec. 1 2, 20 11), se conded b y Mr . Pi erson.  Comments f ollowed in
opposition to postponement.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” --  Ms. Broyles.  Voting “no”
-- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and
Mayor Kaysen.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” -- all members of the governing body.  Main
motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. (#5614)

MEETING: City Council Meeting | DATE: 2011-12-12 | CLIP_ID: 254 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 254

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
December 12, 2011
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Mr. Allen (arriving at 6:10 p.m.), Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr.
Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb
Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by
Mr. Collins and Ms. Ash, agenda item #14 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request
by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Rinne and Ms. Ash, agenda item #19 was
removed from the Consent Ag enda.  Mr . Collins moved to app rove remaining Consent Ag enda
items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body
present.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on November 28, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a  voucher payable to
Ameri-Tech H VAC Service s, and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Va ldez moved to approve
payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers. 
Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated at
5618 Townsend Place and described as the North 170 feet of Tract 6, Briney Tracts, Laramie County,
Wyoming (generally located at the southwest corner of Townsend Place and east Carlson Street).
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final reading,
seconded by Ms. Ash.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body present.
(#3937)
Ordinance amending an exis ting PUD  Ordinance, “Appendix #1” of the  Cheyenne and
Laramie County Zoning Ordinance 1988, for  Block 5, Mountview Park, and tha t portion of the
vacated right-of-way of East 19th Street as vacated under Ordinance No. 2001, Cheyenne, Wyoming
(located at the southeast corner of the intersection of East Pershing Boulevard and Converse Avenue)
(“Cole Shopping Cen ter”).  Gary Harrison, Concepts West Architec ture, representative for the
applicant, Safeway, Inc., briefly reviewed Safeway’s redevelopment plan s for the current Cole
Shopping Center site, noting the estimated $20 million dollar investment project will inc lude
construction of ne w buildings.  J eff Ferg ot, Safewa y corpor ate a rchitect and desig n manag er,
clarified the new Safeway building will result in approximately  20-25 additional employees, and
reviewed anticipated improvements to the property , which include a potential fuel fa cility and
revamping of current retail buildings.  He noted the importance of a timely decision by the City on
the proposed roundabout intersection configuration, adjacent to the property, as it affects the site plan
that will be submitted, the construction schedule, and the planned new store opening date, which has
now been delayed to 2013.  Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by
Ms. Ash.  Mr. Collins  moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Ms. Ash.  I t was advised the
substitute includes a  building heig ht limi t of three  stories unless approve d by  the B oard of
Adjustment, eliminates language restricting electronic message centers beyond what is currently in
the City Code for the  CB Community  Business District, a nd a provision to make the or dinance
effective only after the sale of the property.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members
of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Allen w ho did not vote (arrived at the
meeting as roll call (voting) was in progress. (#3938)
Ordinance amending Title 17, Zoning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 C heyenne City  Map of the Che yenne City  Code, by  chang ing the  zoning
classification from MR-1 Medium Density Residential – Established District to MUB Mixed Use
with Business Emphasis District for Lots 5, 6 and the we st 44-feet of Lot 7, Block 110, Original
City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northeast corner of the intersection of Warren Avenue and
E. 25  Street).  Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.th
Concern was expressed with businesses encroaching into residential areas.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  (#3939)
Ordinance amending Title 17, Z oning, Chapter 17.08, Zoning Districts Established and
Section 17.08.040 Chey enne City  Map of the Che yenne City  Code , by chang ing the  zoning
classification f rom LR-2 Low Density Residential – Developing Di strict to NB N eighborhood
Business District for Lot 6, Block Thr ee, and Lot 6, Block F our, Sundance Ridge  II, Cheyenne,
Wyoming (located north of Storey Boulevard, on either side of Sycamore Road).  Dr. Rinne declared
a conflict of int erest (nearby property owner interest) and vacated Council Chambers. Ka tharine
Wilkinson, speaking on behalf of April Brimmer Kunz and Carol and Art Merrill (residents in the
vicinity), reviewed the residential ownership and non-commercial nature of the area on the north side
of Storey Blvd.  She expressed the following concerns: Future of the vicinity relative to commercial
vs. residential uses, spot zoning, visual aesthetics, traffic volume, maintaining residential integrity
of the area, fill dirt issues, and lack of restriction on types of businesses that may be allowed.  Jack
Knudson and Sharon Knudson reviewed the development of the in-house business located in the area
affiliated with a past zone cha nge; expressed c oncerns with spot z oning a nd fut ure potential
commercial development off Storey Blvd., and discussed the neighborhood’s environment, future
planning decisions, and neighborhood quality of life issues.  Bob Parry, owner of Kiki’s Kandles,
and Ed Clark spoke  in support of the de velopment af filiated with the propose d zone chang e,
commenting that the project fits with the PlanCheyenne document, promotes jobs and sales tax
revenue, and brings water and sewer infrastructure to the area.  Reference was also made regarding
current businesses in the vicinity.  Rick Hunnicutt, President, Western Development Corporation,
summarized how the area in general had changed due to The Pointe subdivision; commented on
current businesses, including  a chur ch, in the Storey  Blvd. a rea that have be en built and that
proposed commercial use would be for office space, not bars or restaurants; reviewed infrastructure
that has been implemented in the neighborhood due to the current development project, and advised
the project will result in a nice subdivision.  Mr. Collins moved to approve on third and final reading,
seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon inquiry, Brett Walker, Development office, provided information on
zoning differences between commercial Community Business vs. Neighborhood Business Districts,
and allowable uses by right in a Neighborhood Business District.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” --
all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the
vote, Dr. Rinne returned to Council Chambers. (#3940)
Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated in
a portion of the Northeast Quarter (NE1/4) of Section 1, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the
6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located north of and adjacent to West Lincolnway
and east of and adjacent to the BNSF Railroad) -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsor -
Mr. Collins).
Ordinance amending Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, Section 5.08.010, Amusements,
of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne City Code pertaining to the license
fee for conducting a theater business -- referred to Finance Committee (sponsor - Mr. Collins).
Resolution a uthorizing the  City  of  Che yenne, th rough th e City  At torney’s Of fice, to
commence condemnation proceedings to obtain re al property located at 1900 Converse A venue
necessary for the completion of the East Pershing Boulevard Reconstruction Project (Phase 4) (2003-
2006, 2007-2010 1% Sales Tax).  John Palmer spoke in opposition to the resolution, commenting
on project planning concerns, and suggested further discussions be held with affected property
owners regarding possible other roundabout configuration alternatives in lieu of eminent domain
proceedings.  Danny  J ames questioned the status of a n alterna tive to the curr ently proposed
roundabout design that he presented at the last City Council meeting which involved changing the
timing of the lights to help control traffic.  In response, Doug Vetter, City Engineer, advised City
staff had reviewed the plan and concluded a signal timing plan was not a viable option due to traffic
volumes and current intersection problems.  He expressed appreciation to Mr. James for submitting
the alternate proposal.  Alex Davison, attorney representing the land owner involved in the potential
condemnation action, extensively reviewed the land owner’s issues and concerns regarding the intent
of the resolution and affiliated issues, and submitted a written position (i.e. City and property owner)
summary (on file with office of City Clerk), explaining the summary’s contents and referencing a
December 8, 2011 letter involving the “last offer”.  His comments included the following topics:
Property value a nd differences in appraisa ls that had bee n acquired; damage components to the
property due to the proposed roundabout configuration; engineer’s estimate of the  corridor plan;
alternate roundabout plan from MTJ Engineering he had previously submitted to the City; City’s
acquisition of the parcel desired for the roundabout vs. acquisition of the entire business property
site; future planning and zoning exemptions that may be required for any commercial activities at
the former Hollywood Video business site due to loss of the par cel the City  wishes to acquir e;
property acc ess/egress issues; potential loss  of future income to the proper ty owner s; gr ound
contamination issues; City’s monetary offer amount to acquire the parcel desired for the roundabout
project and Cit y funds that could be available to purchase the entire property (in re: reference to
information contained within the Ay res Associates J uly 2009 plan).  Scott L arsen, Benchmark
Engineers, reviewed information on the alternate dual roundabout configuration as prepared by MTJ
Engineering, stating the engineer, from Wisconsin, was unable to be present at the meeting.  During
his comments he noted the alternate proposal had been submitted by MTJ as a conceptual design
layout and that there are typically changes in design that occur between conceptual and final design
stages, and reviewed benefits of a dual road design.  Mr. Davison spoke in f avor of two smaller
(dual) roundabouts being constructed at the intersection which would result in less impact to the
property owner.  He advised the last offer on the table from the property owners was for the dual
roundabout design and the option for the City to purchase their entire property; stated negotiations
can continue but probably will not “bear fruit”, and briefly discussed the elements surrounding a
condemnation action through the courts relative to the City and his client.  Tom Mason, Director of
Metropolitan Planning Organization, provided information on how the proposed roundabout plan
was de veloped throug h profe ssional eng ineering service s, and af filiated pre liminary estimated
project costs; explained the different design phases utilized prior to proceeding to the final design,
advising of the diff erent engineering firms that were  involved, and noted a  competitive bidding
process was used throug hout the desig n phases.  Na than B eauheim, Ass istant City Eng ineer,
provided information on right-of-way acquisition and areas involved in the roundabout construction
project; expressed concerns with the proposed alternate dual design, as referenced by Mr. Davison
and Mr. Larsen, as it would require additional property; advised the proposed signalized traffic light
option, as suggested by Mr. James, would require additional property needed to achieve separation,
and commented that the goal has been to only acquire property needed for the intersection project.
He reviewed the project’s site design area and changes that had been made resulting in the f inal
design; stated there was a wide disparity between the parties regarding property values, and noted
the e minent d omain p rocess a llows a  thi rd pa rty (i .e. c ourt) t o d ecide iss ues, inc luding jus t
compensation, and provides an opportunity for parties to present their case.  Matt Ashby, Planning
Services Director, provided information relative to the zoning variance process that may be desired
for the remaining property not needed for the project; possible role of the City involving a variance
request; different e lements the B oard of  Adjustment would consider whe n revie wing varia nce
requests, and the potential option of a development agreement for future uses.  Upon inquiry, Dan
White, City Attorney, reviewed the process involving eminent domain proceedings pursuant to state
law that would occ ur if the r esolution was adopted, w hich would i nclude a right of entry  court
hearing, a determination by the court as to whether the City could use the property for purposes of
the construction project and, if so, a review by the court as to the difference in the parcel’s value
before and after the taking to determine a reasonable compensation amount.  Following a five minute
recess to allow Mr. White  to provide copies to Council members of a n e-mail correspondence
previously referenced by Mr. Davison during his comments, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting
at 7:50 p.m.  Mr. Valdez reported there was no recommendation from Finance Committee on the
item.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mayor Kaysen clarified for the record
that Mr. White had provided Council members with a copy of the e-mail Mr. White had sent to Mr.
Davison pursuant to the purpos e of the recess and, upon inquiry, Mr. White described the  topics
content contained in the e- mail.  Upon inquiry , Mr. Ve tter stated he  did not believe ther e were
significant differences in state  licensing requirements for an engineer’s license in Wyoming vs.
Colorado.  U pon inquiry, Mr. Davison stated the engineering firm hired to design an alternate
roundabout configuration was not a Wyoming firm, noting the alternate design was a concept.  Upon
inquiry involving the previous Henderson Basin Flood Control Project in the vicinity, Mr. Beauheim
provided information on the flood control project relative to the Hollywood Video site, drainage
detention and proposed reconstruction of the intersection.  Discussion included the following topics:
Purported areas of non-reconciliation between the City and property owner; moving forward with
condemnation proceedings to allow for an unbiased third party review (i.e. court system); potential
exemption requests for future variances for non-conforming use for land owner’s remaining property
not needed for the intersection project; purchasing of the entire property in addition to the parce l
needed for the construction project, and concern with moving forward with a condemnation process.
Upon further inquiry regarding condemnation proceedings, Mr. White explained issues the cour t
would review to deter mine the value of  the prope rty invol ved in the proceedings.  D iscussion
continued on the reconstruction intersection project; traffic flow/volume studies and future increased
traffic volume proje ctions for the a rea; access traffic flow points re lative to the location of the
intersection; reasonable purchase price for property parcel the City wishes to acquire for the project;
neutrality element of court process to address property issues; intersection improvement needs;
fiduciary responsibility to taxpayers; differing property appraisals; Safeway redevelopment project;
neighborhood benefits, and concern with implementing eminent domain procedures.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” -- Ms. Ash, Mr. B rown, Ms. B royles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Ri nne and Ma yor Kaysen.
Voting “no” -- Mr. Allen, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Following announcement of the
vote and upon in quiry by Mayor Kaysen, Mr. Davison shared that he (i.e. pr operty owners) was
obviously open to further discussions, however, he will recommend to his clients that they put their
property up for sale.  (#5335)

       Announcement was made of a Public Meeting to be held Tue sday, December 20, 2011
starting at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, to
receive input and comments for gathering information on possible projects for the inclusion on the
1% Specific Purpose Sales Tax election ballot, which has been scheduled for August 21, 2012. 
Announcement was made that due to the Christmas holiday and City offices being closed on
Monday, December 26, 2011, the regularly scheduled meeting of the Governing Body will instead
be held on Tuesday, December 27, 2011 beginning at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers, 2101
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.                   
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held December 27, 2011 at 6:00 p.m. in
City Council Chambers for a microbrewery permit application filed with the City Clerk's Office for
Freedoms E dge B rewing Co., LLC d/ b/a F reedoms E dge B rewing Co. , 3 01 W. 1 6th Str eet,
Cheyenne, Wyoming.

Freedoms Edg e B rewing 2, L LC d/b/a Fre edoms Edg e B rewing Co., 1509 Pioneer  Avenue ,
Cheyenne, Wyoming.
Announcement w as made of  the  Ap pointment o f T errence Bell t o th e Laramie Cou nty
Community Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board, to be confirmed December 27, 2011.

Gonzales, Diane Lozano, Dr. Richard Berry, Craig Jones, Ester Davison, William Long, Gus Lopez,
Ross McKelvey, Danny Glick, Jeff Lamm, Rick Robinson and Gay VanHorn to the Laramie County
Community Juvenile Services Joint Powers Board, to be confirmed December 27, 2011.
Under other business:
Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, explained a proposed resolution for introduction
that will support an application for a Land and Water Conservation grant for a playground expansion
project in Holliday Park that will address ADA accessibility issues.   Upon request, Carol Intlekofer,
City Clerk, re ad the r esolution entitlement as follows: “A Resolution  supporting the City  of
Cheyenne’s application for a Land and Water Conservation Fund grant for the Holliday Park West
Playground Expansion Project.”  It was noted that Councilman Valdez was sponsor of the resolution.
Mayor Kaysen referred the item to Finance Committee.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, Mayor Kaysen adjourned
the meeting at 8:48  p.m.
Submitted by,
Carol Intlekofer, MMC
City Clerk

December 27, 2011
Due to the h oliday season, the r egularly schedule d Dec ember 26, 2011 meeting  of the
governing body of the City of Cheyenne was instead held on this date beginning at 6:00 p.m. in
Council Chambers.  Present we re: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr.
Allen, Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Also prese nt: Kristy Anderson, Deputy City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan
White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 Mayor Kaysen read a proclamation and presented a plaque in recognition of City Treasurer
Barb Dorr’s retirement, thanking her for 21 years of service to the City and community.  Barb Dorr
expressed her appreciation to the May or, Council members, c ity staff a nd the community  for
allowing her the opportunity to serve as City Treasurer. 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Brown moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Allen.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body.
[CA] Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on December 12, 2011.  Motion (per
Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown, Mr. Valdez and Mr. Allen each declared a conflict of interest (business interest
in vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, Radio Station KYOY FM and Scotty’s Pizza
Factory respectively) and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker moved to appr ove payment of
vouchers as presented, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  City Treasurer Barb Dorr, Councilwoman Broyles,
Police Chief Brian Kozak, Parks and Recreation Director Rick Parish and Mayor Kaysen provided
information on the following vouchers: First American Title, federal loans used to assist with closing
costs for CHOP program offered through the Housing and Community Development office; Laramie
County She riff’s Of fice - C ARI, federal grant t hat a llows of ficers to r espond a nd inv estigate
domestic violence issues; W. G. Dale Electric Co., disconnecting power at Saddle Tramps building
in preparation for demolition of building, and Action Ads, Transit wall calendars that contain CTP
logo purchased with federal funds. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing
body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown, Mr. Valdez
and Mr. Allen returned to Council Chambers. 
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input for a microbrewery
permit application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Freedoms Edge Brewing Co., LLC d/b/a
Freedoms Edge Brewing Co., 301 W. 16th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Dr. Stephanie Erlich spoke
in support of the application and having a microbrewery in this historic building to help keep it
vibrant.  Ther e being no further  comments and pur pose of the he aring having been me t, Mayor
Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee.
Mayor Ka ysen opene d a publi c hea ring to rec eive a nd comments and input for a
microbrewery permit application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Freedoms Edge Brewing 2,
LLC d/b/a Freedoms Edge Brewing Co., 1509 Pioneer Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Upon inquiry,
Tim Moore, applicant, clarified the microbrewery to be located at 301 W. 16   will be a dispensingth
area with sales to the public as well as an area for brewing.  He further advised the microbrewery
permit to be located on Pioneer Ave. is specifically for a manufacturing and distribution facility, not
open to the public.  There being no further comments and purpose of the hearing having been met,
Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee.
Ordinance annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a parcel of land being situated in
a portion of the Northeast Quarter (NE1/4) of Section 1, Township 13 North, Range 67 West of the
6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming (generally located north of and adjacent to West Lincolnway
and east of and adja cent to the B NSF Railroad).  Reporting for Public Service s Committee, Mr.
Collins moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” -- all members of the governing body.
Ordinance amending Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, Section 5.08.010, Amusements,
of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne City Code pertaining to the license
fee for conducting a theater business.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Kristy Anderson, Deputy City
Clerk, reviewed the reason for reducing the annual business license fee to a flat fee of $130.00 from
$225.00 per screen, explaining theaters now utilize digital media instead of cellulose nitrate film,
which has r educed the time expended for on-site Fire Dept. inspections. Ms. Anderson fur ther
clarified the fee reflects an amount relative to the time allotted for fire inspection costs as well as
administrative processing of applications.  Further comments followed in reference to past ordinance
changes for business licenses with similar fee structures that are now a flat annual business license
fee. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body. 
Ordinance creating Section 10.52.240, Parking of Oversized Vehicles, Trailers, and Semi-
trailers in Residential and Mixed Use Districts, Prohibited, of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and
Parking generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming
-- referred to Committee of the Whole (sponsor - Ms. Broyles).  Mayor Kaysen clarified this item
and Agenda Item #21 (Resolution determining and prioritizing the projects to be proposed to the 1%
Specific Purpose Sales Tax Committee) are being referred to the Committee of the Whole, which
will meet January 4, 2011 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers and is open to the public.

incorporated herein by reference -- referred to Public Services Committee (sponsors - Ms. Ash, Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen).

[CA] Preliminary Plat for North Cheyenne Community Park, Second Filing, a replat of North
Cheyenne Community  Park, Chey enne, Wy oming ( located nor th of and a djacent to St orey
Boulevard, east of and adjacent to Chief Washakie Avenue).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Preliminary Plat for Rivett Subdivison, a portion of the Northeast Quarter (NE1/4) of Section
1, Township 13 North, Range 67 West, of the 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming (located north of and
adjacent to W. Lincolnway, east of and adjacent to the BNSF Railroad).  Mr. Collins moved to
acknowledge receipt with staff recommendations #1 and 2, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Collins
moved to amend by removing staff recommendation  #2 and replacing it with Planning condition
#3 as follows: “Include access and utility easements on final plat before signatures are affixed to
accommodate shared access and utility easements.  This will require additional easements be placed
on the lot adjacent to the east,” seconded by Ms. Ash. Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body. Comments followed reg arding the L ong Range Missile Drive
Corridor plan, location of street easements prior to final plat approval, concerns regarding lack of
right-of-way, 10 acres of undeveloped property located north of Lincoln Court and access questions
regarding development of the property.  Main motion as amended carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.
Amended and Restated Lease Agreement to Contract No. 2660 between the City of Cheyenne
and Safehouse/Sexual Assault Services, Inc., for the City-owned property located at 714 West Fox
Farm Road, Cheyenne, Wyoming.   Mr. Valdez moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr.
Valdez moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Upon inquiry, Dan White, City
Attorney, explained the amendment clarifies that Safehouse is responsible for all maintenance of
buildings on the site and all utility charges on the site. James Perrott MacNeil questioned ownership
of the buildings and whether the property is leased. Upon inquiry, Mr. White and Mayor Kaysen
clarified the City owns the buildings and land.  M otion to a mend carried.  Vo ting “yes” -- all
members of the governing body. Discussion followed regarding clarification between Safehouse and
the City on ownership of the buildings, a new lease with removal of certain clauses that are in the
existing lease, approving the use of the property for 25 years with a renewal option for another 25
years, rental fee of $1.00/year, the lease recognizing the City owns the buildings, and Safehouse
continuing to provide current services.  Mr. Collins moved to amend by substitute as approved by
the city attorney and dated Dec. 27, 2011, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Upon inquiry, Barbara Rogers,
President of the Board of Directors for Safehouse, thanked council members and the Mayor for their
work on the amended lease and advised she could not speak on behalf of the Board, but they would
need to review the proposed substitute for consideration.  Further discussion followed regarding the
renewal clause  in the lease which provides the option for an additional 25 years, extending the lease
to the y ear 2037.  Dr. Ri nne moved to amend Sec tion 25 by  adding  lang uage that states the
agreement can be renewed at the option of lessee WITH CONSENT OF LESSOR, seconded by Mr.
Allen.  Mr. White clarifie d the language contained in that Section provides the w ord “may” not
“shall”as stated by Dr. Rinne. The maker of the motion and second to it concurred on the language
as “may”. Discussion followed regarding the 50 year lease term, conditions if Safehouse should want
to assign the lease, terms in the lease that protect the City, and support for the non-profit group .
Upon inquiry  and for  clarification purposes, Kristy  Ander son, Deputy  City  Clerk, re ad the
amendment as proposed: “This Lease Agreement may be renewed at the option of the Lessee WITH
CONSENT OF LESSOR for an additional term of twenty-five (25) years commencing December
31, 2037, provided however that Lessee shall not have the option to renew this Lease Agreement if
Lessee is then in default.  No further extensions or renewals of this Lease Agreement shall be valid
with the express written consent of the Lessor.” With no objection raised from Council members,
Mayor Kaysen requested permission to ask questions of Safehouse’s attorneys.  Upon inquiry, Holly
Olson, Holland and Hart, legal representative for Safehouse, advised that the proposed amendment
has not been presented to the Safehouse Board for review yet and advised they were unaware of
conceding to the claim of the City’s ownership of the buildings.  She also mentioned that 50 years
is a much longer lease term than had been discussed previously, noting it is an attractive option, and
commented they would like to rea ch terms both sides are comfortable with by  terminating the
assigned lease agreement with Attention Homes and entering into a new one.  Discussion continued
on the length of the lease term.  Mr. B rown raised a point of orde r regarding discussion on what
would happen if Safehouse does not acknowledge the new lease.  Mayor Kaysen ruled discussion
should remain germane to the proposed amendment on the floor regarding Section 25 of the pending
substitute.  Motion to amend failed (tie vote).  Voting “yes” -- Mr. Allen, Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Dr.
Rinne, Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” -- Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Ma yor
Kaysen.  Upon further inquiry regarding whether the Safehouse Board may not acknowledge the
proposed substitute, Mr. White advised the lease agreement entered into in 1988 with Attention
Homes would still be in effect and Safehouse would be leasing on a month to month basis. Upon
further inquiry, Mr. White clarified if Safehouse recommended changes to the proposed lease, those
requests would have to come ba ck before the governing body for approval.  Motion to amend by
substitute dated Dec. 27, 2011 carried.  Voting “yes” -- all members of the governing body with the
exception of Mayor Kaysen who voted “no”.  Main motion as twice amended carried. Voting “yes”
-- all members of the governing body with the exception of May or Kaysen voting “no”. Ma yor
Kaysen clarified he supported Safehouse but his reason for voting “no” was due to concern with the
length of the lease term.  (#5783)
 
[CA] Memorandum of Understanding between Laramie County, City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne
Board of Public Utilities, Chey enne/Laramie County Boa rd of He alth and Chey enne Re gional
Airport Board regarding the management of the Environmental Systems Research Institute (Esri)
Enterprise L icense for the Coope rative G eographic I nformation Sy stems (GI S) prog ram, a nd
authorizing the City of Cheyenne as a software user participant in the Multi-Jurisdictional Licensing
Agreement.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#5784)
[CA] Cooperative Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of

[CA] Contract Modifications No. 1, 2 and 3 to Contract No. 5740 between the City of Cheyenne,

replacement for the Pioneer Park Baseball Field-Infield Renovation Project (SLIB funding).  Motion
(per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $35,682.81). (#5740)
[CA] Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5776 between the City of Cheyenne, Public

Development fund).  Motion (per  Consent Ag enda) carried (amount not to ex ceed $10,000.00).
(#5776)

Announcement was made that due to the Ne w Year’s Day holiday and City offices being
